# Accenture MCQ Bank

*Built 22 Sep 2026 · **2,610 unique questions** across 10 topics · every question has a marked answer*

## How to use this file

- **`-S-` IDs (Source)** come from your uploaded file `Accenture_MOCK_OA_-1-merged---.md` (de-duplicated, cleaned, and re-answered where the source had no key or a wrong one).
- **`-P-` IDs (Practice)** are original questions I wrote or generated to fill gaps. I did **not** copy questions from any website.
- **⚠ Source check** = the source's own answer was wrong, contradictory or version-dependent. Details are in Appendix A.
- IDs are stable (e.g. `NET-P042`). **To add more questions later, upload this file again**; I will compare against every question here and add only ones that are genuinely new.

## Read this first: what your file did and did not contain

- Your upload contained real questions for only **5 of the 10 topics**: MS Office, Networking, Cybersecurity, Cloud and Pseudocode. It also held many *coding problems* (not MCQs) and cheat-sheet notes; those are **not** in this file.
- **DBMS, SQL, Java/OOP, DevOps and Computer Fundamentals had zero questions in the upload**, so those sections are entirely practice questions.
- Public prep sites describe Accenture's online technical assessment as *Common Applications & MS Office (12), Pseudocode (18), Networking/Security/Cloud (10)*. DBMS, SQL, OOP and DevOps show up mainly in coding/interview stages. Weight your time accordingly (see Appendix B).
- Practice questions marked as computed were **verified by execution**: pseudocode and Java output by running equivalent code, SQL results by running each query on a real database, and number-system, subnetting, scheduling, page-fault and normal-form answers by algorithm. Knowledge questions (definitions, shortcuts, services) are written from established facts, but **re-check anything version-specific** (shortcuts, cloud product names).

## 1. MS Office

### 1.1 Source questions (from your upload) (60)

**MSO-S001.** Which feature in MS Word allows you to create a set of documents that have the same layout but different content?

- A) Mail Merge
- B) SmartArt
- C) Macros
- D) Templates

**Answer: A** — Mail Merge

**MSO-S002.** In Excel, what does the function =VLOOKUP(A2, B2:C10, 2, FALSE) do?

- A) Looks up a value in the first column and returns a value in the same row from another column.
- B) Looks up a value in any column and returns a value from the first column.
- C) Searches horizontally across the first row.
- D) Finds the maximum value in a range.

**Answer: A** — Looks up a value in the first column and returns a value in the same row from another column.

**MSO-S003.** Which key combination is used to insert a new slide in a PowerPoint presentation?

- A) Ctrl + N
- B) Ctrl + M
- C) Ctrl + S
- D) Ctrl + O

**Answer: B** — Ctrl + M

**MSO-S004.** In MS Outlook, what does the "Out of Office" feature do?

- A) Deletes all emails while you are away.
- B) Sends an automated reply to incoming emails.
- C) Redirects all emails to another address.
- D) Blocks all emails until you return.

**Answer: B** — Sends an automated reply to incoming emails.

**MSO-S005.** What is the default file extension for an Excel 2016 workbook?

- A) .xls
- B) .xlsx
- C) .csv
- D) .xltx

**Answer: B** — .xlsx

**MSO-S006.** Which MS Word feature automatically corrects common typing errors as you type?

- A) AutoFormat
- B) AutoCorrect
- C) SmartArt
- D) Spell Check

**Answer: B** — AutoCorrect

**MSO-S007.** Which Excel function would you use to count the number of cells in a range that meet a single condition?

- A) COUNTIF
- B) COUNT
- C) SUMIF
- D) AVERAGEIF

**Answer: A** — COUNTIF

**MSO-S008.** In Word, what happens when you press 'Ctrl + Shift + F' while text is selected?

- A) Opens the Font dialog box
- B) Increases font size
- C) Changes font to default
- D) Clears formatting

**Answer: A** — Opens the Font dialog box
> ⚠️ **Source check:** Ambiguous in the original. Strictly, Ctrl+Shift+F puts focus in the Font box; Ctrl+D opens the Font dialog. Option A is the closest match reported.

**MSO-S009.** Which feature in PowerPoint allows you to create a transition effect between slides that matches the previous slide?

- A) Morph
- B) Slide Sorter
- C) Slide Master
- D) Rehearse Timings

**Answer: A** — Morph

**MSO-S010.** Which Outlook feature allows you to schedule emails to be sent at a later time?

- A) Send Later
- B) Delay Delivery
- C) Scheduled Send
- D) Email Timer

**Answer: B** — Delay Delivery
> ⚠️ **Source check:** Classic Outlook calls this Delay Delivery; newer Outlook labels it Schedule Send. Answer depends on version.

**MSO-S011.** What is the default paper size in MS Word?

- A) A4
- B) Letter
- C) Legal
- D) Executive

**Answer: B** — Letter
> ⚠️ **Source check:** Word's default paper size depends on region/installation: Letter (US) vs A4 (India/Europe). Most reports key this as Letter.

**MSO-S012.** Which of the following Excel functions can be used to combine the text from multiple cells into one cell?

- A) CONCATENATE
- B) TEXTJOIN
- C) CONCAT
- D) JOIN

**Answer: A** — CONCATENATE
> ⚠️ **Source check:** CONCATENATE, CONCAT and TEXTJOIN all combine text; only JOIN does not exist. Most reports key CONCATENATE.

**MSO-S013.** Starting with Microsoft Office 2003, Photo Editor was renamed to:

- A) Manager
- B) Picture Manager
- C) Picture Editor
- D) Paint Editor

**Answer: B** — Picture Manager

**MSO-S014.** Which feature is used to make the selected sentence in All Capital Letters or All Small Letters?

- A) Change Letter
- B) Change Sentence
- C) Change Case
- D) Change Word

**Answer: C** — Change Case

**MSO-S015.** Which of these software applications was not part of the first version of Microsoft Office?

- A) Paint
- B) Outlook
- C) PowerPoint
- D) MS Word

**Answer: B** — Outlook
> ⚠️ **Source check:** Answer as reported by the source.

**MSO-S016.** How would you insert the international character shown in the image given below?

- A) Ctrl+Shift+~+?
- B) Ctrl+Shift+Alt+?
- C) Ctrl+Shift+Alt+Del+?
- D) Ctrl+Shift+Alt

**Answer: B** — Ctrl+Shift+Alt+?

**MSO-S017.** When you activate the Mini Translator in MS Word, it will stay active until you deactivate it within the Translation menu. Also, when the Mini Translator is activated, the document will be sent to Microsoft Translator as you type. Are both statements correct?

- A) Both the statements are correct.
- B) Both the statements are incorrect.
- C) Only statement 2 is correct.
- D) Only statement 1 is correct.

**Answer: A** — Both the statements are correct.
> ⚠️ **Source check:** Answer as reported by the source.

**MSO-S018.** What can be done to stop Word’s Numbering command from supplying automatic numbers to sequential paragraphs?

- A) Click the Numbering button again.
- B) Press the Enter key twice to stop numbering.
- C) Both I and II
- D) Neither I nor II

**Answer: C** — Both I and II

**MSO-S019.** If there is any contextual error in the document, the text will be underlined with a green line in a word document. If there is any spelling error in the document, the text will be underlined with a blue line in a word document. If there is any grammatical error in the document, the text will be underlined with a red line in a word document. Are these statements correct?

- A) Only statements II and III are correct and statement I is wrong.
- B) All statements are false.
- C) All statements are true.
- D) Only statements I and II are correct and statement III is wrong.

**Answer: B** — All statements are false.

**MSO-S020.** Which of the following helps navigate from one slide to another slide in the same presentation and to a slide in another presentation?

- A) Footer
- B) Header
- C) Hyperlink
- D) Attachment

**Answer: C** — Hyperlink

**MSO-S021.** Which of the following can be used to close a tab on a browser?

- A) Ctrl+T
- B) Ctrl+A
- C) Ctrl+W
- D) Ctrl+X

**Answer: C** — Ctrl+W

**MSO-S022.** In Excel, the notation “B8:B10” indicates:

- A) all cells from B8 to B10
- B) the product of cell B8 and Cell B10
- C) cell B8 and cell B10 only
- D) cell B only

**Answer: A** — all cells from B8 to B10

**MSO-S023.** If a mail is accidentally deleted, which folder will save the mail?

- A) Draft
- B) Spam
- C) Inbox
- D) Trash

**Answer: D** — Trash

**MSO-S024.** Which key is to be held down to select a word or phrase in MS Word?

- A) Alt
- B) Shift
- C) Ctrl
- D) Tab

**Answer: B** — Shift
> ⚠️ **Source check:** Answer as reported by the source (Shift-click selects a range; Ctrl-click adds a word).

**MSO-P001.** In MS Word, which shortcut is used to: make selected text bold?

- A) Shift+F3
- B) Ctrl+B
- C) Ctrl+P
- D) Ctrl+Shift+C

**Answer: B** — Ctrl+B

**MSO-P002.** In MS Word, what does the shortcut Ctrl+B do?

- A) Make selected text bold
- B) Delete the previous word
- C) Copy formatting (Format Painter)
- D) Print the document

**Answer: A** — Make selected text bold

- B) Ctrl+Shift+C
- C) F7
- D) Ctrl+I

**MSO-P388.** What does an .ost file in Outlook represent?

- A) An offline cache of a mailbox
- B) A PDF attachment
- C) A macro
- D) An Outlook template

**Answer: A** — An offline cache of a mailbox

**MSO-P389.** Which of the following is a web browser?

- A) Excel
- B) Mozilla Firefox
- C) Notepad
- D) Paint

**Answer: B** — Mozilla Firefox

**MSO-P390.** What does clearing the browser cache do?

- A) Deletes bookmarks
- B) Removes stored copies of web pages and files
- C) Removes saved passwords only
- D) Uninstalls extensions

**Answer: B** — Removes stored copies of web pages and files

**MSO-P391.** What are cookies in a web browser?

- A) Malware files
- B) Small files that websites store to remember user information
- C) Browser extensions
- D) Downloaded PDFs

**Answer: B** — Small files that websites store to remember user information

**MSO-P392.** Which browser mode does not save browsing history locally?

- A) Compatibility mode
- B) Safe mode
- C) Incognito / Private mode
- D) Reader mode

**Answer: C** — Incognito / Private mode

**MSO-P393.** What does HTTPS in the address bar indicate?

- A) The connection to the site is encrypted
- B) The page is cached
- C) The site is government-owned
- D) The site has no ads

**Answer: A** — The connection to the site is encrypted

**MSO-P394.** Which command in Windows Command Prompt displays the current directory contents?

- A) dir
- B) list
- C) show
- D) ls

**Answer: A** — dir

**MSO-P395.** Which command in Command Prompt moves up one directory level?

- A) cd /
- B) cd ~
- C) up
- D) cd ..

**Answer: D** — cd ..

**MSO-P396.** Which command takes you to the root of the current drive in Command Prompt?

- A) cd ..
- B) cd ~
- C) cd /root
- D) cd \

**Answer: D** — cd \

**MSO-P397.** Which command shows all files, including hidden ones, in Command Prompt?

- A) dir /a
- B) ls -a
- C) dir /h only
- D) show all

**Answer: A** — dir /a

**MSO-P398.** Which key press closes a program window in Windows?

- A) Shift+F4
- B) Alt+F4
- C) Ctrl+F4 always
- D) Ctrl+Alt+F4

**Answer: B** — Alt+F4

**MSO-P399.** Which key combination captures the full screen in Windows to the clipboard?

- A) Ctrl+P
- B) PrtScn
- C) Alt+S
- D) Ctrl+Shift+S

**Answer: B** — PrtScn

**MSO-P400.** In MS Word, which tab contains the option to insert a Table of Contents?

- A) Home
- B) Insert
- C) References
- D) View

**Answer: C** — References
> ℹ️ Table of Contents options are located under the References tab in modern versions of MS Word.

**MSO-P401.** In MS Excel, what does the formula `=COUNTA(A1:A5)` return if A1=10, A2="Hello", A3=TRUE, A4 is an empty cell, and A5=0?

- A) 3
- B) 4
- C) 5
- D) 2

**Answer: B** — 4
> ℹ️ COUNTA counts all non-empty cells in the given range. Cells A1, A2, A3, and A5 are non-empty (total 4), while A4 is empty.

**MSO-P402.** In MS PowerPoint, which view displays all slides as thumbnails in a grid layout to facilitate reordering?

- A) Slide Show
- B) Slide Sorter
- C) Reading View
- D) Notes Page

**Answer: B** — Slide Sorter
> ℹ️ Slide Sorter view displays thumbnails of all slides in horizontal rows so you can easily reorder them.

**MSO-P403.** In MS Excel, which feature automatically fills data in a column based on an established pattern detected in adjacent columns?

- A) AutoSum
- B) Flash Fill
- C) Data Validation
- D) Conditional Formatting

**Answer: B** — Flash Fill
> ℹ️ Flash Fill (shortcut Ctrl+E) automatically senses patterns and extracts or formats data based on user examples.

**MSO-P404.** In MS Outlook, what is the default keyboard shortcut to reply to an active email?

- A) Ctrl+R
- B) Alt+R
- C) Ctrl+Shift+R
- D) F2

**Answer: A** — Ctrl+R
> ℹ️ Ctrl+R replies to the original sender of an email, while Ctrl+Shift+R initiates a Reply All response.

---

## 2. Networking

### 2.1 Source questions (from your upload) (14)

**NET-S001.** Which protocol is used to assign IP addresses automatically to devices on a network?

- A) DNS
- B) DHCP
- C) FTP
- D) HTTP

**Answer: B** — DHCP

**NET-S002.** What is the primary purpose of a router in a network?

- A) To connect different networks together
- B) To connect multiple devices within the same network
- C) To encrypt data for secure transmission
- D) To host websites

**Answer: A** — To connect different networks together

**NET-S003.** Which layer of the OSI model is responsible for establishing, managing, and terminating connections?

- A) Network Layer
- B) Transport Layer
- C) Session Layer
- D) Data Link Layer

**Answer: C** — Session Layer

**NET-S004.** What does the ping command do?

- A) Measures the data transfer speed between two devices.
- B) Tests the reachability of a host on an IP network.
- C) Encrypts data before sending it over the network.
- D) Blocks network traffic to specific IP addresses.

**Answer: B** — Tests the reachability of a host on an IP network.

**NET-S005.** Which network topology has a central hub to which all other nodes are connected?

- A) Mesh
- B) Ring
- C) Star
- D) Bus

**Answer: C** — Star

**NET-S006.** What is the primary function of a VLAN (Virtual Local Area Network)?

- A) To reduce broadcast traffic
- B) To connect different networks together
- C) To secure the network from external threats
- D) To allow multiple networks on a single switch

**Answer: D** — To allow multiple networks on a single switch
> ⚠️ **Source check:** A VLAN's primary role is logically segmenting one physical switch into multiple broadcast domains; reduced broadcast traffic is a benefit.

**NET-S007.** Which protocol is used to securely transmit data over a VPN?

- A) PPTP
- B) IPSec
- C) L2TP
- D) GRE

**Answer: B** — IPSec

**NET-S008.** What is the purpose of the ARP (Address Resolution Protocol)?

- A) To map IP addresses to MAC addresses
- B) To map MAC addresses to IP addresses
- C) To route packets across a network
- D) To translate domain names to IP addresses

**Answer: A** — To map IP addresses to MAC addresses

**NET-S009.** In the context of networking, what is the role of a gateway?

- A) To connect two different networks
- B) To provide IP addresses to devices
- C) To forward data packets within the same network
- D) To encrypt data during transmission

**Answer: A** — To connect two different networks

**NET-S010.** Which layer of the OSI model is responsible for error detection and correction?

- A) Data Link Layer
- B) Network Layer
- C) Transport Layer
- D) Application Layer

**Answer: A** — Data Link Layer

**NET-S011.** What command is used to view the routing table in a network device?

- A) netstat
- B) ipconfig
- C) route
- D) tracert

**Answer: C** — route

**NET-S012.** What is the primary function of a router in a network?

- A) To connect devices within a single network
- B) To forward data packets between computer networks
- C) To store and retrieve data
- D) To monitor network traffic

**Answer: B** — To forward data packets between computer networks

**NET-S013.** What command allows you to determine whether an IP access list is enabled on a particular interface?

- A) show ip access-list
- B) show ip interface
- C) show ip route
- D) show ip protocol

**Answer: B** — show ip interface

**NET-S014.** What router command will permit SMTP mail to only host 1.1.1.1?

- A) access-list 110 deny tcp any host 1.1.1.1 eq smtp
- B) access-list 110 permit tcp any host 1.1.1.1 eq smtp
- C) access-list 110 permit udp any host 1.1.1.1 eq smtp
- D) access-list 110 permit icmp any host 1.1.1.1 eq smtp

**Answer: B** — access-list 110 permit tcp any host 1.1.1.1 eq smtp

**Answer key:** S001:B · S002:A · S003:C · S004:B · S005:C · S006:D · S007:B · S008:A · S009:A · S010:A · S011:C · S012:B · S013:B · S014:B

- A) The device is on the internet directly

- C) The device is a DNS server
- D) The device is a router

**Answer: B** — The device could not obtain an address from a DHCP server

**NET-P190.** What is the default subnet mask of a Class A network?

- B) 255.255.255.0
- C) 255.255.255.255
- D) 255.0.0.0

**Answer: D** — 255.0.0.0

**NET-P191.** What is the default subnet mask of a Class B network?

- A) 255.255.255.0
- B) 255.0.0.0
- C) 255.255.0.0

**Answer: C** — 255.255.0.0

- B) 255.255.255.128
- C) 255.0.0.0
- D) 255.255.255.0

**Answer: D** — 255.255.255.0

**Answer: A** — 128 – 191

- B) Unicast

**Answer: B** — ipconfig

- A) ipconfig
- B) ifconfig
- C) nicinfo
- D) netshow

**Answer: B** — ifconfig

**Answer: A** — tracert

- A) ipconfig
- B) traceroute
- C) tracert
- D) nslookup

**Answer: B** — traceroute

- B) ping

- D) netstat

**Answer: B** — ping

**NET-P202.** Which command queries DNS servers for domain information?

- A) ping
- B) netstat

- D) nslookup

**Answer: D** — nslookup

**NET-P203.** Which command displays active connections and listening ports?

- B) netstat

- A) ipconfig /all

- C) arp -a

**Answer: C** — arp -a

**NET-P205.** Which command flushes the DNS resolver cache in Windows?

- A) ipconfig /flushdns

- C) netsh /flush

**NET-P206.** Which command releases the current DHCP lease on Windows?

- A) ipconfig /release
- B) ipconfig /renew
- C) ipconfig /flushdns
- D) ipconfig /all

**Answer: A** — ipconfig /release

**NET-P207.** What does a TTL (Time To Live) value in an IP packet do?

- A) Defines packet size
- B) Sets the packet's priority
- C) Limits the number of hops a packet can take
- D) Encrypts the packet

**Answer: C** — Limits the number of hops a packet can take

**NET-P208.** What happens when the TTL of a packet reaches zero?

- A) The packet is discarded
- B) The packet is broadcast
- C) The packet is retransmitted
- D) The packet is encrypted

**Answer: A** — The packet is discarded

**NET-P209.** What is the maximum transmission unit (MTU) of standard Ethernet?

- A) 576 bytes
- B) 65535 bytes

- D) 9000 bytes

**Answer: C** — 1500 bytes

**NET-P210.** Which cable type is least susceptible to electromagnetic interference?

**Answer: D** — Fibre optic

**NET-P211.** Which connector is used with Ethernet twisted-pair cables?

**Answer: D** — RJ-45

**Answer: D** — Cat 5e or Cat 6

**NET-P213.** What is the maximum length of a standard Ethernet twisted-pair segment?

**NET-P214.** Which wireless standard is commonly known as Wi-Fi?

**Answer: A** — IEEE 802.11

**NET-P217.** What does SSID stand for?

**NET-P218.** What does CSMA/CD stand for?

**NET-P219.** Which method does Wi-Fi use to handle shared medium access?

**NET-P220.** What is the purpose of the Spanning Tree Protocol (STP)?

**NET-P221.** Which routing protocol is a distance-vector protocol?

- B) OSPF

**NET-P222.** Which routing protocol is a link-state protocol?

**NET-P223.** Which protocol is used to exchange routing information between autonomous systems on the internet?

**NET-P224.** What metric does RIP use?

- B) Server Error

**NET-P226.** What does the HTTP status code 200 mean?

- B) Redirect

- D) OK

**Answer: D** — OK

**NET-P227.** What does the HTTP status code 500 mean?

- B) Forbidden

**NET-P230.** Which HTTP method is typically used to submit data to create a resource?

- C) A domain name to an IPv4 address

**NET-P237.** What is a collision domain?

**Answer: B** — A network segment where data packets can collide

**NET-P238.** Which device breaks up collision domains?

**NET-P239.** Which device breaks up broadcast domains?

- B) Repeater
- C) Hub

**NET-P240.** What is bandwidth?

- A) The number of hops
- B) The delay of a link

- D) The size of a packet

**Answer: C** — The maximum rate of data transfer of a link

**NET-P241.** What is latency?

- A) The time taken for data to travel from source to destination
- B) The width of the cable
- C) The number of dropped packets
- D) The amount of data sent per second

**Answer: A** — The time taken for data to travel from source to destination

**NET-P242.** What is throughput?

- A) The actual rate of successful data transfer
- B) The delay in the network
- C) The theoretical maximum rate
- D) The number of users

**Answer: A** — The actual rate of successful data transfer

**NET-P243.** What is jitter?

- A) Signal strength
- B) Bandwidth limit
- C) Variation in packet delay
- D) Packet loss

**Answer: C** — Variation in packet delay

**NET-P244.** What is the usable host IP address range for a subnetwork with network address `192.168.1.32/27`?

- A) 192.168.1.32 to 192.168.1.63
- B) 192.168.1.33 to 192.168.1.62
- C) 192.168.1.33 to 192.168.1.63
- D) 192.168.1.32 to 192.168.1.62

**Answer: B** — 192.168.1.33 to 192.168.1.62
> ℹ️ A /27 subnet mask gives a block size of 32. Network ID = 192.168.1.32, Broadcast ID = 192.168.1.63. Usable host addresses range from .33 to .62.

**NET-P245.** Which IPv6 address block is reserved for unique local addresses (ULA), equivalent to IPv4 private IP addresses?

- A) fe80::/10
- B) fc00::/7
- C) ff00::/8
- D) ::1/128

**Answer: B** — fc00::/7
> ℹ️ fc00::/7 (commonly implemented as fd00::/8) is designated by RFC 4193 for IPv6 Unique Local Addresses.

**NET-P246.** Which protocol is used by network administrators to query domain registration records (owner details, registrar, name servers)?

- A) SNMP
- B) ICMP
- C) WHOIS
- D) BGP

**Answer: C** — WHOIS
> ℹ️ WHOIS operates over TCP port 43 to retrieve registration details of domain names and IP address blocks.

**NET-P247.** What is the maximum segment size (MSS) for a standard TCP segment over an Ethernet MTU of 1500 bytes (assuming standard 20-byte IP and 20-byte TCP headers)?

- A) 1460 bytes
- B) 1480 bytes
- C) 1500 bytes
- D) 1518 bytes

**Answer: A** — 1460 bytes
> ℹ️ MSS = MTU (1500) - IP Header (20) - TCP Header (20) = 1460 bytes.

**NET-P248.** Which spanning tree state in standard IEEE 802.1D discards data frames and does NOT learn MAC addresses, but processes received BPDUs?

- A) Forwarding
- B) Learning
- C) Blocking
- D) Disabled

**Answer: C** — Blocking
> ℹ️ In the Blocking state, a switch port receives and evaluates BPDUs to prevent loops, but discards incoming data frames and ignores MAC address learning.

## 3. Cybersecurity

### 3.1 Source questions (from your upload) (33)

**CYB-S001.** Which protocol is commonly used for secure communication over a network?

- A) HTTP
- B) FTP
- C) HTTPS
- D) SMTP

**Answer: C** — HTTPS

**CYB-S002.** What is the primary function of a firewall in network security?

- A) To accelerate internet speed.
- B) To encrypt network traffic.
- C) To block unauthorized access to or from a private network.
- D) To store backup data securely.

**Answer: C** — To block unauthorized access to or from a private network.

**CYB-S003.** Which of the following is considered a form of social engineering attack?

- A) Phishing
- B) Denial of Service
- C) SQL Injection
- D) Malware

**Answer: A** — Phishing

**CYB-S004.** What does the term "zero-day exploit" refer to?

- A) A security vulnerability that is unknown to the software vendor.
- B) A virus that has been active for less than a day.
- C) A software patch that fixes security vulnerabilities.
- D) An antivirus software update released on the first day of the month.

**Answer: A** — A security vulnerability that is unknown to the software vendor.

**CYB-S005.** Which of the following methods is most effective in preventing unauthorized access to a user's account?

- A) Using a strong password
- B) Enabling two-factor authentication (2FA)
- C) Changing passwords frequently
- D) Using public Wi-Fi networks for login

**Answer: B** — Enabling two-factor authentication (2FA)

**CYB-S006.** What is the purpose of encryption in cybersecurity?

- A) To compress files for faster transmission
- B) To hide data from unauthorized users
- C) To detect intrusions in a network
- D) To back up data securely

**Answer: B** — To hide data from unauthorized users

**CYB-S007.** Which type of malware encrypts a user's files and demands payment for the decryption key?

- A) Trojan Horse
- B) Ransomware
- C) Spyware
- D) Worm

**Answer: B** — Ransomware

**CYB-S008.** Which type of attack involves intercepting and altering communication between two parties without their knowledge?

- A) Man-in-the-Middle (MITM)
- B) Phishing
- C) Denial of Service (DoS)
- D) SQL Injection

**Answer: A** — Man-in-the-Middle (MITM)

**CYB-S009.** Which of the following is a technique used to identify vulnerabilities in a system by simulating an attack?

- A) Penetration Testing
- B) Social Engineering
- C) Brute Force Attack
- D) Phishing

**Answer: A** — Penetration Testing

**CYB-S010.** Which cryptographic algorithm is most commonly used for securing internet communications?

- A) AES
- B) RSA
- C) DES
- D) 3DES

**Answer: A** — AES
> ⚠️ **Source check:** Ambiguous: TLS uses RSA/ECDHE for key exchange and AES for bulk encryption. AES is the most commonly reported key.

**CYB-S011.** What is the primary purpose of a honeypot in cybersecurity?

- A) To detect unauthorized access
- B) To act as a decoy to lure attackers
- C) To protect sensitive data
- D) To encrypt data before transmission

**Answer: B** — To act as a decoy to lure attackers

**CYB-S012.** Which security protocol is used to ensure secure communication over a wireless network?

- A) WPA3
- B) WEP
- C) HTTP
- D) FTP

**Answer: A** — WPA3

**CYB-S013.** What is the purpose of a digital certificate in a public key infrastructure (PKI)?

- A) To verify the identity of the certificate holder
- B) To encrypt data during transmission
- C) To store encryption keys
- D) To sign digital documents

**Answer: A** — To verify the identity of the certificate holder

**CYB-S014.** What protocol is used for secure data transmission over the internet?

- A) HTTP
- B) FTP
- C) HTTPS
- D) SMTP

**Answer: C** — HTTPS

**CYB-S015.** What is a firewall's main purpose?

- A) To enhance network speed
- B) To block unauthorized access while permitting outward communication
- C) To store backup data
- D) To manage network addresses

**Answer: B** — To block unauthorized access while permitting outward communication

**CYB-S016.** Which security method encrypts data to prevent unauthorized access?

- A) Compression
- B) Encryption
- C) Decryption
- D) Fragmentation

**Answer: B** — Encryption

**CYB-S017.** What does AES stand for in encryption?

- A) Advanced Encryption Standard
- B) Application Encryption System
- C) Automatic Encryption System
- D) Advanced Encoding System

**Answer: A** — Advanced Encryption Standard

**CYB-S018.** Which encryption algorithm uses a 56-bit key?

- A) RSA
- B) AES
- C) DES
- D) Blowfish

**Answer: C** — DES

**CYB-S019.** What type of device is a UTM?

- A) Universal Traffic Monitor
- B) Unified Threat Management device
- C) Universal Transmission Module
- D) Unified Transmission Manager

**Answer: B** — Unified Threat Management device

**CYB-S020.** Which device inspects incoming and outgoing network traffic and decides whether to allow or block specific traffic based on a defined set of security rules?

- A) Switch
- B) Router
- C) Firewall
- D) Hub

**Answer: C** — Firewall

**CYB-S021.** What is a DDoS attack?

- A) Data Decryption Over System
- B) Distributed Denial of Service attack
- C) Direct Data Overwrite Service
- D) Distributed Data Operation Service

**Answer: B** — Distributed Denial of Service attack

**CYB-S022.** Which attack involves sending unsolicited bulk messages?

- A) Phishing
- B) Hacking
- C) Spam
- D) Spoofing

**Answer: C** — Spam

**CYB-S023.** What is phishing?

- A) A technique to gain personal information for identity theft, using fraudulent emails
- B) A type of malware
- C) A network protocol
- D) A firewall configuration

**Answer: A** — A technique to gain personal information for identity theft, using fraudulent emails

**CYB-S024.** What is a stateful firewall?

- A) A firewall that blocks all incoming traffic
- B) A firewall that inspects each packet individually
- C) A firewall that monitors the state of active connections and determines which network packets to allow through the firewall
- D) A firewall that only allows outgoing traffic

**Answer: C** — A firewall that monitors the state of active connections and determines which network packets to allow through the firewall

**CYB-S025.** What is a packet-filtering firewall?

- A) A firewall that filters traffic by examining the header of each packet
- B) A firewall that encrypts all outgoing traffic
- C) A firewall that monitors user activities
- D) A firewall that blocks all external connections

**Answer: A** — A firewall that filters traffic by examining the header of each packet

**CYB-S026.** What is the function of a VPN?

- A) To increase internet speed
- B) To create a secure connection over a less secure network
- C) To block unwanted emails
- D) To manage network traffic

**Answer: B** — To create a secure connection over a less secure network

**CYB-S027.** Which protocol is used to ensure secure remote login?

- A) FTP
- B) HTTP
- C) SSH (Secure Shell)
- D) SMTP

**Answer: C** — SSH (Secure Shell)

**CYB-S028.** What does IDS stand for in network security?

- A) Information Detection System
- B) Internet Data System
- C) Intrusion Detection System
- D) Internal Data System

**Answer: C** — Intrusion Detection System

**CYB-S029.** What type of network security device scans for viruses and malware?

- A) Firewall
- B) IDS
- C) Antivirus scanning devices
- D) VPN

**Answer: C** — Antivirus scanning devices

**CYB-S030.** In a network, what attack involves forging an IP address to impersonate another device?

- A) Spoofing
- B) Phishing
- C) Hacking
- D) Spamming

**Answer: A** — Spoofing

**CYB-S031.** Which type of cyberattack involves overwhelming a network or service with excessive traffic, making it inaccessible to users?

- A) Ransomware
- B) Phishing
- C) Spoofing
- D) DDoS (Distributed Denial of Service)

**Answer: D** — DDoS (Distributed Denial of Service)
> ℹ️ Derived from the source's Q&A key (options added).

**CYB-S032.** Which cybersecurity threat encrypts a victim's data and demands payment for the decryption key?

- A) Spyware
- B) Ransomware
- C) Phishing
- D) DDoS

**Answer: B** — Ransomware
> ℹ️ Derived from the source's Q&A key (options added).

**CYB-S033.** Which form of cyberattack involves tricking individuals into providing sensitive information by pretending to be a trustworthy entity?

- A) Brute force
- B) Phishing
- C) DDoS
- D) Ransomware

**Answer: B** — Phishing
> ℹ️ Derived from the source's Q&A key (options added).

### 3.2 Practice questions (185)

**CYB-P001.** Which attack or threat is described as tricking users into revealing sensitive information through fake emails or websites?

- A) Logic bomb
- B) DNS poisoning
- C) Phishing
- D) DDoS attack

**Answer: C** — Phishing

**CYB-P002.** Which of the following best describes Phishing?

- A) Tricking users into revealing sensitive information through fake emails or websites
- B) Forcing a logged-in user's browser to perform unwanted actions on a site
- C) Redirecting users to fake websites without their knowledge, often via DNS
- D) A security risk that originates from within the organisation

**Answer: A** — Tricking users into revealing sensitive information through fake emails or websites

**CYB-P003.** Which attack or threat is described as a targeted phishing attack aimed at a specific person or organisation?

- A) Cryptojacking
- B) Logic bomb
- C) Session hijacking
- D) Spear phishing

**Answer: D** — Spear phishing

**CYB-P004.** Which of the following best describes Spear phishing?

- A) A targeted phishing attack aimed at a specific person or organisation
- B) Malware that encrypts files and demands payment for the key
- C) An attack on a vulnerability for which no patch yet exists
- D) Secretly using a victim's computing resources to mine cryptocurrency

**Answer: A** — A targeted phishing attack aimed at a specific person or organisation

**CYB-P005.** Which attack or threat is described as phishing carried out over voice calls?

- A) Botnet
- B) Watering hole attack
- C) Dictionary attack
- D) Vishing

**Answer: D** — Vishing

**CYB-P006.** Which of the following best describes Vishing?

- A) Luring victims with a promise such as free media or an infected USB drive
- B) Phishing carried out over voice calls
- C) Software that secretly gathers information about a user's activity
- D) Taking over a valid user session by stealing the session token

**Answer: B** — Phishing carried out over voice calls

**CYB-P007.** Which attack or threat is described as phishing carried out through SMS text messages?

- A) Smishing
- B) Worm
- C) Cross-site request forgery (CSRF)
- D) Rootkit

**Answer: A** — Smishing

**CYB-P008.** Which of the following best describes Smishing?

- A) A rogue Wi-Fi access point mimicking a legitimate one
- B) Using leaked username/password pairs to log in to other services
- C) A network of compromised devices controlled by an attacker
- D) Phishing carried out through SMS text messages

**Answer: D** — Phishing carried out through SMS text messages

**CYB-P009.** Which attack or threat is described as malware that encrypts files and demands payment for the key?

- A) Ransomware
- B) Man-in-the-middle attack
- C) Buffer overflow
- D) Worm

**Answer: A** — Ransomware

**CYB-P010.** Which of the following best describes Ransomware?

- A) Tricking users into revealing sensitive information through fake emails or websites
- B) Malicious code that triggers when specific conditions are met
- C) Injecting malicious scripts into web pages viewed by other users
- D) Malware that encrypts files and demands payment for the key

**Answer: D** — Malware that encrypts files and demands payment for the key

**CYB-P011.** Which attack or threat is described as software that secretly gathers information about a user's activity?

- A) DDoS attack
- B) Buffer overflow
- C) Dictionary attack
- D) Spyware

**Answer: D** — Spyware

**CYB-P012.** Which of the following best describes Spyware?

- A) A network of compromised devices controlled by an attacker
- B) Software that secretly gathers information about a user's activity
- C) Malware that encrypts files and demands payment for the key
- D) Malware that hides its presence and provides privileged access

**Answer: B** — Software that secretly gathers information about a user's activity

**CYB-P013.** Which attack or threat is described as malware disguised as legitimate software?

- A) Rootkit
- B) Phishing
- C) Pharming
- D) Trojan horse

**Answer: D** — Trojan horse

**CYB-P014.** Which of the following best describes Trojan horse?

- A) A network of compromised devices controlled by an attacker
- B) Tricking users into revealing sensitive information through fake emails or websites
- C) Injecting malicious scripts into web pages viewed by other users
- D) Malware disguised as legitimate software

**Answer: D** — Malware disguised as legitimate software

**CYB-P015.** Which attack or threat is described as self-replicating malware that spreads across networks without user action?

- A) Worm
- B) Credential stuffing
- C) Shoulder surfing
- D) Botnet

**Answer: A** — Worm

**CYB-P016.** Which of the following best describes Worm?

- A) Self-replicating malware that spreads across networks without user action
- B) Flooding a target with traffic from many sources to make it unavailable
- C) Inserting malicious SQL through user input to manipulate a database
- D) Injecting malicious scripts into web pages viewed by other users

**Answer: A** — Self-replicating malware that spreads across networks without user action

**CYB-P017.** Which attack or threat is described as malicious code that attaches to a host file and spreads when the file is run?

- A) SQL injection
- B) Zero-day exploit
- C) Virus
- D) Buffer overflow

**Answer: C** — Virus

**CYB-P018.** Which of the following best describes Virus?

- A) Taking over a valid user session by stealing the session token
- B) A targeted phishing attack aimed at a specific person or organisation
- C) Malicious code that attaches to a host file and spreads when the file is run
- D) Malicious code that triggers when specific conditions are met

**Answer: C** — Malicious code that attaches to a host file and spreads when the file is run

**CYB-P019.** Which attack or threat is described as malware that hides its presence and provides privileged access?

- A) Brute-force attack
- B) Rootkit
- C) Keylogger
- D) Botnet

**Answer: B** — Rootkit

**CYB-P020.** Which of the following best describes Rootkit?

- A) Following an authorised person into a restricted area without authorisation
- B) An attack on a vulnerability for which no patch yet exists
- C) Malware that hides its presence and provides privileged access
- D) Malware that encrypts files and demands payment for the key

**Answer: C** — Malware that hides its presence and provides privileged access

**CYB-P021.** Which attack or threat is described as software or hardware that records keystrokes?

- A) Ransomware
- B) Tailgating
- C) Phishing
- D) Keylogger

**Answer: D** — Keylogger

**CYB-P022.** Which of the following best describes Keylogger?

- A) Corrupting DNS cache data to redirect users to malicious sites
- B) A rogue Wi-Fi access point mimicking a legitimate one
- C) An attack on a vulnerability for which no patch yet exists
- D) Software or hardware that records keystrokes

**Answer: D** — Software or hardware that records keystrokes

**CYB-P023.** Which attack or threat is described as a network of compromised devices controlled by an attacker?

- A) Botnet
- B) DDoS attack
- C) Shoulder surfing
- D) Evil twin

**Answer: A** — Botnet

**CYB-P024.** Which of the following best describes Botnet?

- A) Flooding a target with traffic from many sources to make it unavailable
- B) A rogue Wi-Fi access point mimicking a legitimate one
- C) A network of compromised devices controlled by an attacker
- D) Phishing carried out through SMS text messages

**Answer: C** — A network of compromised devices controlled by an attacker

**CYB-P025.** Which attack or threat is described as flooding a target with traffic from many sources to make it unavailable?

- A) DDoS attack
- B) Brute-force attack
- C) DNS poisoning
- D) Spear phishing

**Answer: A** — DDoS attack

**CYB-P026.** Which of the following best describes DDoS attack?

- A) Flooding a target with traffic from many sources to make it unavailable
- B) A targeted phishing attack aimed at a specific person or organisation
- C) A security risk that originates from within the organisation
- D) Malicious code that triggers when specific conditions are met

**Answer: A** — Flooding a target with traffic from many sources to make it unavailable

**CYB-P027.** Which attack or threat is described as inserting malicious SQL through user input to manipulate a database?

- A) Phishing
- B) Session hijacking
- C) SQL injection
- D) Buffer overflow

**Answer: C** — SQL injection

**CYB-P028.** Which of the following best describes SQL injection?

- A) A network of compromised devices controlled by an attacker
- B) Redirecting users to fake websites without their knowledge, often via DNS
- C) Inserting malicious SQL through user input to manipulate a database
- D) Phishing carried out over voice calls

**Answer: C** — Inserting malicious SQL through user input to manipulate a database

**CYB-P029.** Which attack or threat is described as injecting malicious scripts into web pages viewed by other users?

- A) Cross-site scripting (XSS)
- B) Man-in-the-middle attack
- C) Worm
- D) DDoS attack

**Answer: A** — Cross-site scripting (XSS)

**CYB-P030.** Which of the following best describes Cross-site scripting (XSS)?

- A) Corrupting DNS cache data to redirect users to malicious sites
- B) Injecting malicious scripts into web pages viewed by other users
- C) Trying many password combinations until the right one is found
- D) Secretly using a victim's computing resources to mine cryptocurrency

**Answer: B** — Injecting malicious scripts into web pages viewed by other users

**CYB-P031.** Which attack or threat is described as forcing a logged-in user's browser to perform unwanted actions on a site?

- A) Session hijacking
- B) Cryptojacking
- C) Trojan horse
- D) Cross-site request forgery (CSRF)

**Answer: D** — Cross-site request forgery (CSRF)

**CYB-P032.** Which of the following best describes Cross-site request forgery (CSRF)?

- A) Forcing a logged-in user's browser to perform unwanted actions on a site
- B) Phishing carried out over voice calls
- C) Using leaked username/password pairs to log in to other services
- D) Compromising a website frequently visited by the targets

**Answer: A** — Forcing a logged-in user's browser to perform unwanted actions on a site

**CYB-P033.** Which attack or threat is described as secretly intercepting and possibly altering communication between two parties?

- A) Man-in-the-middle attack
- B) Spyware
- C) SQL injection
- D) Tailgating

**Answer: A** — Man-in-the-middle attack

**CYB-P034.** Which of the following best describes Man-in-the-middle attack?

- A) Malware that hides its presence and provides privileged access
- B) Taking over a valid user session by stealing the session token
- C) Secretly intercepting and possibly altering communication between two parties
- D) Following an authorised person into a restricted area without authorisation

**Answer: C** — Secretly intercepting and possibly altering communication between two parties

**CYB-P035.** Which attack or threat is described as trying many password combinations until the right one is found?

- A) Dictionary attack
- B) Brute-force attack
- C) Zero-day exploit
- D) Spyware

**Answer: B** — Brute-force attack

**CYB-P036.** Which of the following best describes Brute-force attack?

- A) An attack on a vulnerability for which no patch yet exists
- B) Trying many password combinations until the right one is found
- C) Observing someone entering credentials by watching them
- D) A network of compromised devices controlled by an attacker

**Answer: B** — Trying many password combinations until the right one is found

**CYB-P037.** Which attack or threat is described as trying passwords from a list of common words?

- A) Dictionary attack
- B) Spyware
- C) Zero-day exploit
- D) Rootkit

**Answer: A** — Dictionary attack

**CYB-P038.** Which of the following best describes Dictionary attack?

- A) Software or hardware that records keystrokes
- B) Trying passwords from a list of common words
- C) Phishing carried out through SMS text messages
- D) A rogue Wi-Fi access point mimicking a legitimate one

**Answer: B** — Trying passwords from a list of common words

**CYB-P039.** Which attack or threat is described as using leaked username/password pairs to log in to other services?

- A) Spyware
- B) Credential stuffing
- C) Insider threat
- D) Vishing

**Answer: B** — Credential stuffing

**CYB-P040.** Which of the following best describes Credential stuffing?

- A) Corrupting DNS cache data to redirect users to malicious sites
- B) Forcing a logged-in user's browser to perform unwanted actions on a site
- C) Secretly intercepting and possibly altering communication between two parties
- D) Using leaked username/password pairs to log in to other services

**Answer: D** — Using leaked username/password pairs to log in to other services

**CYB-P041.** Which attack or threat is described as an attack on a vulnerability for which no patch yet exists?

- A) Cryptojacking
- B) Rootkit
- C) Logic bomb
- D) Zero-day exploit

**Answer: D** — Zero-day exploit

**CYB-P042.** Which of the following best describes Zero-day exploit?

- A) Phishing carried out over voice calls
- B) An attack on a vulnerability for which no patch yet exists
- C) Phishing carried out through SMS text messages
- D) Luring victims with a promise such as free media or an infected USB drive

**Answer: B** — An attack on a vulnerability for which no patch yet exists

**CYB-P043.** Which attack or threat is described as observing someone entering credentials by watching them?

- A) Tailgating
- B) Shoulder surfing
- C) SQL injection
- D) Virus

**Answer: B** — Shoulder surfing

**CYB-P044.** Which of the following best describes Shoulder surfing?

- A) Malicious code that attaches to a host file and spreads when the file is run
- B) Malware disguised as legitimate software
- C) Malicious code that triggers when specific conditions are met
- D) Observing someone entering credentials by watching them

**Answer: D** — Observing someone entering credentials by watching them

**CYB-P045.** Which attack or threat is described as following an authorised person into a restricted area without authorisation?

- A) Tailgating
- B) Keylogger
- C) Watering hole attack
- D) Session hijacking

**Answer: A** — Tailgating

**CYB-P046.** Which of the following best describes Tailgating?

- A) Malicious code that triggers when specific conditions are met
- B) Phishing carried out through SMS text messages
- C) Malware that hides its presence and provides privileged access
- D) Following an authorised person into a restricted area without authorisation

**Answer: D** — Following an authorised person into a restricted area without authorisation

**CYB-P047.** Which attack or threat is described as corrupting DNS cache data to redirect users to malicious sites?

- A) DNS poisoning
- B) Cryptojacking
- C) Rootkit
- D) Dictionary attack

**Answer: A** — DNS poisoning

**CYB-P048.** Which of the following best describes DNS poisoning?

- A) Corrupting DNS cache data to redirect users to malicious sites
- B) Luring victims with a promise such as free media or an infected USB drive
- C) Secretly using a victim's computing resources to mine cryptocurrency
- D) Software or hardware that records keystrokes

**Answer: A** — Corrupting DNS cache data to redirect users to malicious sites

**CYB-P049.** Which attack or threat is described as taking over a valid user session by stealing the session token?

- A) Zero-day exploit
- B) Session hijacking
- C) Evil twin
- D) Brute-force attack

**Answer: B** — Session hijacking

**CYB-P050.** Which of the following best describes Session hijacking?

- A) Taking over a valid user session by stealing the session token
- B) A security risk that originates from within the organisation
- C) Trying passwords from a list of common words
- D) A rogue Wi-Fi access point mimicking a legitimate one

**Answer: A** — Taking over a valid user session by stealing the session token

**CYB-P051.** Which attack or threat is described as writing more data to a buffer than it can hold, overwriting adjacent memory?

- A) Session hijacking
- B) Worm
- C) Buffer overflow
- D) Rootkit

**Answer: C** — Buffer overflow

**CYB-P052.** Which of the following best describes Buffer overflow?

- A) Observing someone entering credentials by watching them
- B) A network of compromised devices controlled by an attacker
- C) Writing more data to a buffer than it can hold, overwriting adjacent memory
- D) Luring victims with a promise such as free media or an infected USB drive

**Answer: C** — Writing more data to a buffer than it can hold, overwriting adjacent memory

**CYB-P053.** Which attack or threat is described as a security risk that originates from within the organisation?

- A) Botnet
- B) Spyware
- C) Insider threat
- D) Baiting

**Answer: C** — Insider threat

**CYB-P054.** Which of the following best describes Insider threat?

- A) Corrupting DNS cache data to redirect users to malicious sites
- B) Trying many password combinations until the right one is found
- C) A security risk that originates from within the organisation
- D) Malware that encrypts files and demands payment for the key

**Answer: C** — A security risk that originates from within the organisation

**CYB-P055.** Which attack or threat is described as malicious code that triggers when specific conditions are met?

- A) Tailgating
- B) Vishing
- C) Logic bomb
- D) Credential stuffing

**Answer: C** — Logic bomb

**CYB-P056.** Which of the following best describes Logic bomb?

- A) Malicious code that triggers when specific conditions are met
- B) Corrupting DNS cache data to redirect users to malicious sites
- C) Luring victims with a promise such as free media or an infected USB drive
- D) Trying many password combinations until the right one is found

**Answer: A** — Malicious code that triggers when specific conditions are met

**CYB-P057.** Which attack or threat is described as compromising a website frequently visited by the targets?

- A) Man-in-the-middle attack
- B) Watering hole attack
- C) Shoulder surfing
- D) Dictionary attack

**Answer: B** — Watering hole attack

**CYB-P058.** Which of the following best describes Watering hole attack?

- A) Compromising a website frequently visited by the targets
- B) A network of compromised devices controlled by an attacker
- C) A rogue Wi-Fi access point mimicking a legitimate one
- D) Tricking users into revealing sensitive information through fake emails or websites

**Answer: A** — Compromising a website frequently visited by the targets

**CYB-P059.** Which attack or threat is described as redirecting users to fake websites without their knowledge, often via DNS?

- A) Man-in-the-middle attack
- B) Brute-force attack
- C) Zero-day exploit
- D) Pharming

**Answer: D** — Pharming

**CYB-P060.** Which of the following best describes Pharming?

- A) Redirecting users to fake websites without their knowledge, often via DNS
- B) Trying passwords from a list of common words
- C) Following an authorised person into a restricted area without authorisation
- D) Secretly using a victim's computing resources to mine cryptocurrency

**Answer: A** — Redirecting users to fake websites without their knowledge, often via DNS

**CYB-P061.** Which attack or threat is described as secretly using a victim's computing resources to mine cryptocurrency?

- A) Tailgating
- B) Insider threat
- C) Cryptojacking
- D) Brute-force attack

**Answer: C** — Cryptojacking

**CYB-P062.** Which of the following best describes Cryptojacking?

- A) Compromising a website frequently visited by the targets
- B) Following an authorised person into a restricted area without authorisation
- C) A network of compromised devices controlled by an attacker
- D) Secretly using a victim's computing resources to mine cryptocurrency

**Answer: D** — Secretly using a victim's computing resources to mine cryptocurrency

**CYB-P063.** Which attack or threat is described as a rogue Wi-Fi access point mimicking a legitimate one?

- A) Shoulder surfing
- B) DNS poisoning
- C) Rootkit
- D) Evil twin

**Answer: D** — Evil twin

**CYB-P064.** Which of the following best describes Evil twin?

- A) Flooding a target with traffic from many sources to make it unavailable
- B) Corrupting DNS cache data to redirect users to malicious sites
- C) A rogue Wi-Fi access point mimicking a legitimate one
- D) Software or hardware that records keystrokes

**Answer: C** — A rogue Wi-Fi access point mimicking a legitimate one

**CYB-P065.** Which attack or threat is described as luring victims with a promise such as free media or an infected USB drive?

- A) Dictionary attack
- B) Cryptojacking
- C) Baiting
- D) Spyware

**Answer: C** — Baiting

**CYB-P066.** Which of the following best describes Baiting?

- A) A security risk that originates from within the organisation
- B) Software or hardware that records keystrokes
- C) Luring victims with a promise such as free media or an infected USB drive
- D) Using leaked username/password pairs to log in to other services

**Answer: C** — Luring victims with a promise such as free media or an infected USB drive

**CYB-P067.** AES is best classified as which of the following?

- A) key exchange
- B) cryptographic hash function
- C) symmetric encryption
- D) asymmetric encryption

**Answer: C** — symmetric encryption

**CYB-P068.** DES is best classified as which of the following?

- A) asymmetric encryption
- B) cryptographic hash function
- C) symmetric encryption
- D) key exchange

**Answer: C** — symmetric encryption

**CYB-P069.** 3DES is best classified as which of the following?

- A) symmetric encryption
- B) key exchange
- C) cryptographic hash function
- D) asymmetric encryption

**Answer: A** — symmetric encryption

**CYB-P070.** Blowfish is best classified as which of the following?

- A) asymmetric encryption
- B) key exchange
- C) symmetric encryption
- D) cryptographic hash function

**Answer: C** — symmetric encryption

**CYB-P071.** RC4 is best classified as which of the following?

- A) cryptographic hash function
- B) symmetric stream cipher
- C) asymmetric encryption
- D) key exchange

**Answer: B** — symmetric stream cipher

**CYB-P072.** RSA is best classified as which of the following?

- A) symmetric encryption
- B) cryptographic hash function
- C) asymmetric encryption
- D) message authentication code

**Answer: C** — asymmetric encryption

**CYB-P073.** ECC is best classified as which of the following?

- A) asymmetric encryption
- B) symmetric encryption
- C) cryptographic hash function
- D) message authentication code

**Answer: A** — asymmetric encryption

**CYB-P074.** Diffie-Hellman is best classified as which of the following?

- A) symmetric encryption
- B) key exchange
- C) message authentication code
- D) cryptographic hash function

**Answer: B** — key exchange

**CYB-P075.** DSA is best classified as which of the following?

- A) symmetric encryption
- B) cryptographic hash function
- C) digital signature algorithm
- D) key exchange

**Answer: C** — digital signature algorithm

**CYB-P076.** SHA-256 is best classified as which of the following?

- A) cryptographic hash function
- B) key exchange
- C) symmetric encryption
- D) asymmetric encryption

**Answer: A** — cryptographic hash function

**CYB-P077.** MD5 is best classified as which of the following?

- A) symmetric encryption
- B) key exchange
- C) asymmetric encryption
- D) cryptographic hash function

**Answer: D** — cryptographic hash function

**CYB-P078.** bcrypt is best classified as which of the following?

- A) symmetric encryption
- B) key exchange
- C) password hashing function
- D) asymmetric encryption

**Answer: C** — password hashing function

**CYB-P079.** HMAC is best classified as which of the following?

- A) message authentication code
- B) symmetric encryption
- C) asymmetric encryption
- D) key exchange

**Answer: A** — message authentication code

**CYB-P080.** What does the CIA triad stand for in information security?

- A) Cryptography, Integrity, Authentication
- B) Confidentiality, Identity, Authorisation
- C) Control, Identity, Access
- D) Confidentiality, Integrity, Availability

**Answer: D** — Confidentiality, Integrity, Availability

**CYB-P081.** Which element of the CIA triad ensures data is not disclosed to unauthorised parties?

- A) Integrity
- B) Availability
- C) Confidentiality
- D) Accountability

**Answer: C** — Confidentiality

**CYB-P082.** Which element of the CIA triad ensures data is not altered without authorisation?

- A) Non-repudiation
- B) Availability
- C) Confidentiality
- D) Integrity

**Answer: D** — Integrity

**CYB-P083.** Which element of the CIA triad ensures systems and data are accessible when needed?

- A) Confidentiality
- B) Availability
- C) Integrity
- D) Authenticity

**Answer: B** — Availability

**CYB-P084.** Which security concept ensures a sender cannot deny sending a message?

- A) Availability
- B) Obfuscation
- C) Non-repudiation
- D) Confidentiality

**Answer: C** — Non-repudiation

**CYB-P085.** What does AAA stand for in security?

- A) Authentication, Authorisation, Accounting
- B) Access, Audit, Alert
- C) Authorisation, Auditing, Availability
- D) Authentication, Availability, Accounting

**Answer: A** — Authentication, Authorisation, Accounting

**CYB-P086.** What is authentication?

- A) Recording user activity
- B) Granting permissions to a user
- C) Encrypting data
- D) Verifying the identity of a user or system

**Answer: D** — Verifying the identity of a user or system

**CYB-P087.** What is authorisation?

- A) Backing up data
- B) Verifying a password
- C) Encrypting traffic
- D) Determining what an authenticated user is allowed to do

**Answer: D** — Determining what an authenticated user is allowed to do

**CYB-P088.** What is multi-factor authentication (MFA)?

- A) Using two passwords
- B) Using two or more different types of credentials to verify identity
- C) Logging in from two devices
- D) Changing passwords often

**Answer: B** — Using two or more different types of credentials to verify identity

**CYB-P089.** Which of the following is a "something you have" authentication factor?

- A) PIN
- B) Fingerprint
- C) Password
- D) Smart card

**Answer: D** — Smart card

**CYB-P090.** Which of the following is a "something you are" authentication factor?

- A) Password
- B) Smart card
- C) OTP token
- D) Fingerprint

**Answer: D** — Fingerprint

**CYB-P091.** Which of the following is a "something you know" authentication factor?

- A) PIN
- B) Retina scan
- C) Hardware token
- D) Fingerprint

**Answer: A** — PIN

**CYB-P092.** What is symmetric encryption?

- A) Encryption with no key
- B) Encryption using the same key for encryption and decryption
- C) Hashing of data
- D) Encryption using a public and private key pair

**Answer: B** — Encryption using the same key for encryption and decryption

**CYB-P093.** What is asymmetric encryption?

- A) Encryption using one shared key
- B) A hash function
- C) Encryption using a public key and a private key pair
- D) Encryption with no keys

**Answer: C** — Encryption using a public key and a private key pair

**CYB-P094.** In public-key cryptography, which key is used to decrypt a message encrypted with the recipient's public key?

- A) The recipient's private key
- B) The sender's public key
- C) The sender's private key
- D) The recipient's public key

**Answer: A** — The recipient's private key

**CYB-P095.** In public-key cryptography, which key is used to create a digital signature?

- A) The signer's private key
- B) A shared secret
- C) The signer's public key
- D) The receiver's public key

**Answer: A** — The signer's private key

**CYB-P096.** What is the key length of DES?

- A) 64 bits of effective key
- B) 56 bits
- C) 256 bits
- D) 128 bits

**Answer: B** — 56 bits

**CYB-P097.** Which AES key sizes are supported?

- A) 64 and 128 bits
- B) 512 and 1024 bits
- C) 128, 192 and 256 bits
- D) 56 and 112 bits

**Answer: C** — 128, 192 and 256 bits

**CYB-P098.** What is the output size of SHA-256?

- A) 512 bits
- B) 128 bits
- C) 160 bits
- D) 256 bits

**Answer: D** — 256 bits

**CYB-P099.** What is the output size of MD5?

- A) 160 bits
- B) 256 bits
- C) 128 bits
- D) 64 bits

**Answer: C** — 128 bits

**CYB-P100.** Which property must a cryptographic hash function have?

- A) It is one-way (irreversible)
- B) It produces variable-length output
- C) It requires two keys
- D) It can be reversed with a key

**Answer: A** — It is one-way (irreversible)

**CYB-P101.** Which of the following is a hash function?

- A) RSA
- B) Diffie-Hellman
- C) SHA-256
- D) AES

**Answer: C** — SHA-256

**CYB-P102.** Why is salting used when hashing passwords?

- A) To encrypt the passwords
- B) To shorten the hash
- C) To speed up hashing
- D) To make identical passwords produce different hashes and defeat rainbow tables

**Answer: D** — To make identical passwords produce different hashes and defeat rainbow tables

**CYB-P103.** What is a rainbow table?

- A) A table of encryption keys
- B) A precomputed table of hashes used to crack passwords
- C) A firewall rule set
- D) A log of failed logins

**Answer: B** — A precomputed table of hashes used to crack passwords

**CYB-P104.** What is a digital signature used for?

- A) Hiding messages
- B) Compressing files
- C) Speeding up email
- D) Verifying authenticity and integrity of a message

**Answer: D** — Verifying authenticity and integrity of a message

**CYB-P105.** What is a digital certificate?

- A) A virus signature
- B) A password file
- C) A firewall rule
- D) An electronic document binding a public key to an identity

**Answer: D** — An electronic document binding a public key to an identity

**CYB-P106.** Which entity issues digital certificates?

- A) Certificate Authority (CA)
- B) Domain registrar
- C) ISP
- D) DHCP server

**Answer: A** — Certificate Authority (CA)

**CYB-P107.** What does PKI stand for?

- A) Public Key Infrastructure
- B) Protected Key Integration
- C) Public Key Identity
- D) Private Key Interface

**Answer: A** — Public Key Infrastructure

**CYB-P108.** Which protocol provides encryption for HTTP traffic?

- A) FTP
- B) SNMP
- C) ARP
- D) TLS/SSL

**Answer: D** — TLS/SSL

**CYB-P109.** What is the successor to SSL?

- A) IPSec
- B) SSH
- C) TLS
- D) PGP

**Answer: C** — TLS

**CYB-P110.** Which protocol provides a secure replacement for Telnet?

- A) FTP
- B) SNMPv1
- C) SSH
- D) HTTP

**Answer: C** — SSH

**CYB-P111.** Which protocol provides a secure replacement for FTP using SSH?

- A) SMTP
- B) SNMP
- C) TFTP
- D) SFTP

**Answer: D** — SFTP

**CYB-P112.** Which security protocol operates at the network layer to encrypt IP packets?

- A) IPSec
- B) SSH
- C) TLS
- D) HTTPS

**Answer: A** — IPSec

**CYB-P113.** Which Wi-Fi security protocol is the most secure among the options?

- A) WPA
- B) WPA3
- C) Open network
- D) WEP

**Answer: B** — WPA3

**CYB-P114.** Which Wi-Fi security protocol is considered broken and should not be used?

- A) WPA2
- B) 802.1X
- C) WEP
- D) WPA3

**Answer: C** — WEP

**CYB-P115.** What is a firewall?

- A) A password manager
- B) A disk encryption tool
- C) A device or software that filters network traffic based on rules
- D) A backup service

**Answer: C** — A device or software that filters network traffic based on rules

**CYB-P116.** What is an IDS?

- A) A system that blocks all traffic
- B) A disk cleaner
- C) A system that detects and alerts on suspicious activity
- D) A password vault

**Answer: C** — A system that detects and alerts on suspicious activity

**CYB-P117.** What is the difference between an IDS and an IPS?

- A) An IPS can actively block detected threats
- B) They are identical
- C) An IDS blocks threats but an IPS only alerts
- D) An IPS only works on hosts

**Answer: A** — An IPS can actively block detected threats

**CYB-P118.** What is a DMZ in network security?

- A) A network segment that exposes public-facing services while isolating the internal network
- B) A type of firewall rule
- C) A VPN tunnel
- D) A backup site

**Answer: A** — A network segment that exposes public-facing services while isolating the internal network

**CYB-P119.** What is a honeypot?

- A) A decoy system designed to attract and study attackers
- B) A password vault
- C) A patch server
- D) An antivirus engine

**Answer: A** — A decoy system designed to attract and study attackers

**CYB-P120.** What is the purpose of a VPN?

- A) Create backups
- B) Speed up downloads
- C) Block viruses
- D) Create an encrypted tunnel over an untrusted network

**Answer: D** — Create an encrypted tunnel over an untrusted network

**CYB-P121.** What is penetration testing?

- A) Encrypting a hard disk
- B) Installing antivirus
- C) Authorised simulated attacks to find vulnerabilities
- D) Removing malware

**Answer: C** — Authorised simulated attacks to find vulnerabilities

**CYB-P122.** What is a vulnerability?

- A) A weakness that can be exploited
- B) A firewall rule
- C) An attack itself
- D) A security policy

**Answer: A** — A weakness that can be exploited

**CYB-P123.** What is an exploit?

- A) A log file
- B) A patch
- C) Code or technique that takes advantage of a vulnerability
- D) A security policy

**Answer: C** — Code or technique that takes advantage of a vulnerability

**CYB-P124.** What is a threat?

- A) A weakness in a system
- B) A security control
- C) A backup copy
- D) A potential cause of harm to an asset

**Answer: D** — A potential cause of harm to an asset

**CYB-P125.** What is risk in information security?

- A) The likelihood that a threat exploits a vulnerability, and its impact
- B) A type of malware
- C) A software bug only
- D) A firewall rule

**Answer: A** — The likelihood that a threat exploits a vulnerability, and its impact

**CYB-P126.** What is patch management?

- A) Deleting old files
- B) Regularly applying software updates to fix vulnerabilities
- C) Blocking all USB ports
- D) Encrypting data at rest

**Answer: B** — Regularly applying software updates to fix vulnerabilities

**CYB-P127.** What is the principle of least privilege?

- A) Passwords are never changed
- B) Users get only the access needed to do their job
- C) All users get admin rights
- D) Privileges are never revoked

**Answer: B** — Users get only the access needed to do their job

**CYB-P128.** What is defence in depth?

- A) Using multiple layers of security controls
- B) Using one very strong firewall
- C) Hiding servers
- D) Encrypting everything twice

**Answer: A** — Using multiple layers of security controls

**CYB-P129.** What is a security policy?

- A) A documented set of rules for protecting an organisation's assets
- B) A password
- C) A firewall rule
- D) An antivirus definition

**Answer: A** — A documented set of rules for protecting an organisation's assets

**CYB-P130.** What is data at rest?

- A) Data stored on a device or medium
- B) Data moving across a network
- C) Deleted data
- D) Data being processed in memory

**Answer: A** — Data stored on a device or medium

**CYB-P131.** What is data in transit?

- A) Archived data
- B) Data moving across a network
- C) Data in CPU registers
- D) Data stored on disk

**Answer: B** — Data moving across a network

**CYB-P132.** Which technology protects data at rest on a laptop?

- A) TLS
- B) Full-disk encryption
- C) NAT
- D) VPN

**Answer: B** — Full-disk encryption

**CYB-P133.** Which technology protects data in transit across the internet?

- A) TLS
- B) Defragmentation
- C) Full-disk encryption
- D) RAID

**Answer: A** — TLS

**CYB-P134.** What is the purpose of a backup for ransomware resilience?

- A) Block malware execution
- B) Prevent phishing emails
- C) Restore data without paying the ransom
- D) Encrypt network traffic

**Answer: C** — Restore data without paying the ransom

**CYB-P135.** What is the 3-2-1 backup rule?

- A) 3 days, 2 weeks, 1 month retention
- B) 3 copies of data, on 2 different media, with 1 stored offsite
- C) 3 passwords, 2 tokens, 1 PIN
- D) 3 servers, 2 firewalls, 1 router

**Answer: B** — 3 copies of data, on 2 different media, with 1 stored offsite

**CYB-P136.** Which is the best defence against SQL injection?

- A) Disabling cookies
- B) Parameterised queries and input validation
- C) Using HTTP
- D) Longer passwords

**Answer: B** — Parameterised queries and input validation

**CYB-P137.** Which is the best defence against XSS?

- A) Hashing passwords
- B) Output encoding and input validation
- C) Using strong Wi-Fi
- D) Disabling SQL

**Answer: B** — Output encoding and input validation

**CYB-P138.** Which is the best defence against CSRF?

- A) Anti-CSRF tokens
- B) Using GET for state changes
- C) Longer session cookies
- D) Disabling HTTPS

**Answer: A** — Anti-CSRF tokens

**CYB-P139.** Which is the best defence against brute-force login attacks?

- A) Removing captcha
- B) Account lockout and MFA
- C) Disabling logging
- D) Shorter passwords

**Answer: B** — Account lockout and MFA

**CYB-P140.** What is a CAPTCHA used for?

- A) Encrypting forms
- B) Distinguishing humans from bots
- C) Storing passwords
- D) Scanning for viruses

**Answer: B** — Distinguishing humans from bots

**CYB-P141.** What is phishing awareness training meant to do?

- A) Block spam servers
- B) Help users recognise and report suspicious messages
- C) Patch software
- D) Encrypt email

**Answer: B** — Help users recognise and report suspicious messages

**CYB-P142.** Which of the following is a strong password practice?

- A) Using a long unique passphrase with a password manager
- B) Using birthdays
- C) Reusing one strong password everywhere
- D) Writing passwords on sticky notes

**Answer: A** — Using a long unique passphrase with a password manager

**CYB-P143.** What is a zero-trust security model?

- A) Trust only the firewall
- B) Trust verified vendors permanently
- C) Trust all internal traffic
- D) Never trust, always verify every access request

**Answer: D** — Never trust, always verify every access request

**CYB-P144.** What is a security information and event management (SIEM) system used for?

- A) Load balancing
- B) Collecting and analysing security logs for threats
- C) Compiling code
- D) Encrypting databases

**Answer: B** — Collecting and analysing security logs for threats

**CYB-P145.** What is endpoint protection?

- A) Security software on devices such as laptops and phones
- B) A DNS server
- C) A backup tape
- D) A network firewall

**Answer: A** — Security software on devices such as laptops and phones

**CYB-P146.** Which of the following is an example of malware?

- A) VPN
- B) Firewall
- C) Hash
- D) Ransomware

**Answer: D** — Ransomware

**CYB-P147.** What is the difference between a virus and a worm?

- A) A worm only affects hardware
- B) A worm spreads by itself over networks; a virus needs a host file
- C) They are the same
- D) A virus spreads by itself; a worm needs a host file

**Answer: B** — A worm spreads by itself over networks; a virus needs a host file

**CYB-P148.** What is a false positive in security monitoring?

- A) A legitimate activity flagged as malicious
- B) A blocked port
- C) A malicious activity that is missed
- D) A confirmed attack

**Answer: A** — A legitimate activity flagged as malicious

**CYB-P149.** What is a false negative in security monitoring?

- A) A malicious activity that is not detected
- B) A legitimate activity flagged as malicious
- C) A log entry
- D) A blocked attack

**Answer: A** — A malicious activity that is not detected

**CYB-P150.** What is the purpose of the HTTPS padlock icon?

- A) Indicates the site is safe from malware
- B) Indicates the connection is encrypted
- C) Indicates the site is government approved
- D) Indicates the page is cached

**Answer: B** — Indicates the connection is encrypted

**CYB-P151.** What is biometric authentication?

- A) Authenticating with a location
- B) Authenticating with a token
- C) Authenticating with a password
- D) Authenticating using physical characteristics such as fingerprints

**Answer: D** — Authenticating using physical characteristics such as fingerprints

**CYB-P152.** What is single sign-on (SSO)?

- A) One login gives access to multiple applications
- B) Signing documents once
- C) Using a single password for every site
- D) Signing on from a single device

**Answer: A** — One login gives access to multiple applications

**CYB-P153.** What is OAuth mainly used for?

- A) Virus scanning
- B) Disk encryption
- C) Delegated authorisation
- D) Packet filtering

**Answer: C** — Delegated authorisation

**CYB-P154.** What is Kerberos?

- A) A firewall product
- B) A hash function
- C) A network authentication protocol using tickets
- D) A VPN client

**Answer: C** — A network authentication protocol using tickets

**CYB-P155.** What does GDPR primarily regulate?

- A) Internet speed
- B) Hardware exports
- C) Software licensing
- D) Protection of personal data of EU residents

**Answer: D** — Protection of personal data of EU residents

**CYB-P156.** What is the purpose of an air-gapped system?

- A) Physical isolation from other networks
- B) Faster networking
- C) Wireless charging
- D) Cloud storage

**Answer: A** — Physical isolation from other networks

**CYB-P157.** What is steganography?

- A) Encrypting data with RSA
- B) Detecting malware
- C) Hiding data inside other files such as images
- D) Compressing archives

**Answer: C** — Hiding data inside other files such as images

**CYB-P158.** What is a sandbox in security?

- A) A password vault
- B) A DNS zone
- C) A backup server
- D) An isolated environment for running untrusted code

**Answer: D** — An isolated environment for running untrusted code

**CYB-P159.** What is a security audit log used for?

- A) Compressing files
- B) Filtering spam
- C) Recording events for review and investigation
- D) Encrypting traffic

**Answer: C** — Recording events for review and investigation

**CYB-P160.** What is the difference between encoding and encryption?

- A) Encoding needs a key; encryption does not
- B) They are identical
- C) Encoding is reversible without a key; encryption needs a key
- D) Encoding is one-way

**Answer: C** — Encoding is reversible without a key; encryption needs a key

**CYB-P161.** What is the difference between hashing and encryption?

- A) Both are reversible
- B) Hashing is one-way; encryption is reversible with a key
- C) Both need two keys
- D) Hashing is reversible; encryption is not

**Answer: B** — Hashing is one-way; encryption is reversible with a key

**CYB-P162.** Which attack overwhelms a service using amplification through many reflecting servers?

- A) Keylogging
- B) Phishing
- C) SQL injection
- D) DDoS reflection/amplification

**Answer: D** — DDoS reflection/amplification

**CYB-P163.** Which of the following is a social engineering technique?

- A) Pretexting
- B) Port scanning
- C) Buffer overflow
- D) SYN flood

**Answer: A** — Pretexting

**CYB-P164.** What is a SYN flood?

- A) A phishing campaign
- B) A DoS attack sending many TCP SYN requests without completing the handshake
- C) A password attack
- D) A DNS attack

**Answer: B** — A DoS attack sending many TCP SYN requests without completing the handshake

**CYB-P165.** What is port scanning?

- A) Probing a host to discover open ports and services
- B) Encrypting ports
- C) Assigning ports
- D) Blocking ports

**Answer: A** — Probing a host to discover open ports and services

**CYB-P166.** Which tool is commonly used for port scanning?

- A) Excel
- B) Wireshark
- C) Nmap
- D) Photoshop

**Answer: C** — Nmap

**CYB-P167.** Which tool is commonly used for packet capture and analysis?

- A) Word
- B) Wireshark
- C) Notepad
- D) Nmap

**Answer: B** — Wireshark

**CYB-P168.** What is a CVE?

- A) A firewall vendor
- B) A public identifier for a known vulnerability
- C) A type of malware
- D) An encryption standard

**Answer: B** — A public identifier for a known vulnerability

**CYB-P169.** What is the CVSS score used for?

- A) Rating the severity of vulnerabilities
- B) Measuring bandwidth
- C) Rating password strength
- D) Counting malware families

**Answer: A** — Rating the severity of vulnerabilities

**CYB-P170.** What is the difference between a threat actor and a vulnerability?

- A) A vulnerability is a person
- B) A threat actor is an entity that may exploit a weakness; a vulnerability is the weakness
- C) They mean the same thing
- D) A threat actor is a software bug

**Answer: B** — A threat actor is an entity that may exploit a weakness; a vulnerability is the weakness

**CYB-P171.** What is the purpose of network segmentation?

- A) Increase IP addresses
- B) Speed up DNS
- C) Limit the spread of an attack by dividing the network
- D) Reduce cable length

**Answer: C** — Limit the spread of an attack by dividing the network

**CYB-P172.** What is a WAF?

- A) Wireless Access Filter
- B) Windows Antivirus Framework
- C) Wide Area Firewall
- D) Web Application Firewall that filters HTTP traffic to web apps

**Answer: D** — Web Application Firewall that filters HTTP traffic to web apps

**CYB-P173.** What is the purpose of hashing passwords with a slow algorithm such as bcrypt?

- A) Make hashes reversible
- B) Make brute-force attacks costly
- C) Speed up logins
- D) Reduce storage

**Answer: B** — Make brute-force attacks costly

**CYB-P174.** What is the OWASP Top 10?

- A) A list of the most critical web application security risks
- B) A list of network protocols
- C) A ranking of programming languages
- D) A list of top firewall vendors

**Answer: A** — A list of the most critical web application security risks

**CYB-P175.** What is the key-management difference between symmetric and asymmetric encryption?

- A) There is no difference
- B) Asymmetric encryption needs a shared secret key
- C) Symmetric needs a secure way to share one secret key; asymmetric uses a public/private key pair
- D) Symmetric encryption uses two different keys

**Answer: C** — Symmetric needs a secure way to share one secret key; asymmetric uses a public/private key pair

**CYB-P176.** What is a brute-force attack countermeasure that delays repeated login attempts called?

- A) Sandboxing
- B) Rate limiting / throttling
- C) Salting
- D) Tokenisation

**Answer: B** — Rate limiting / throttling

**CYB-P177.** What is data masking?

- A) Encrypting the disk
- B) Compressing data
- C) Deleting data
- D) Obscuring sensitive data so it cannot be read while keeping its format

**Answer: D** — Obscuring sensitive data so it cannot be read while keeping its format

**CYB-P178.** What is tokenisation?

- A) Encrypting with AES
- B) Hashing with salt
- C) Replacing sensitive data with non-sensitive tokens
- D) Splitting text into words

**Answer: C** — Replacing sensitive data with non-sensitive tokens

**CYB-P179.** What is a security token (hardware) used for?

- A) Speeding up logins
- B) Generating or storing credentials for authentication
- C) Blocking ports
- D) Encrypting Wi-Fi

**Answer: B** — Generating or storing credentials for authentication

**CYB-P180.** What is TOTP?

- A) Total One-Time Protocol
- B) Trusted Online Transfer Protocol
- C) Two-Option Token Password

**Answer: D** — Time-based One-Time Password
