**CYB-P181.** Which cryptographic mode of operation turns a block cipher into a self-synchronizing stream cipher by encrypting the previous ciphertext block?

- A) Electronic Codebook (ECB)
- B) Cipher Block Chaining (CBC)
- C) Cipher Feedback (CFB)
- D) Galois/Counter Mode (GCM)

**Answer: C** — Cipher Feedback (CFB)
> ℹ️ Cipher Feedback (CFB) mode feeds the previous ciphertext block into the block cipher to generate a keystream for stream encryption.

**CYB-P182.** What type of attack involves an attacker sending crafted ARP responses onto a LAN to associate their MAC address with the IP address of a legitimate gateway?

- A) DNS Poisoning
- B) ARP Spoofing
- C) DHCP Starvation
- D) MAC Flooding

**Answer: B** — ARP Spoofing
> ℹ️ ARP spoofing/poisoning allows an attacker to intercept or modify network traffic intended for the default gateway.

**CYB-P183.** In modern asymmetric cryptography, which framework uses curves over finite fields to offer equivalent RSA 3072-bit security with much smaller 256-bit key sizes?

- A) AES-256
- B) Elliptic Curve Cryptography (ECC)
- C) Diffie-Hellman (DH-1024)
- D) SHA-256

**Answer: B** — Elliptic Curve Cryptography (ECC)
> ℹ️ ECC provides public key security equivalent to traditional RSA while requiring significantly smaller key lengths and computational overhead.

**CYB-P184.** What HTTP security header instructs web browsers to only communicate with a web server using HTTPS, mitigating SSL stripping attacks?

- A) X-Frame-Options
- B) Content-Security-Policy
- C) HTTP Strict Transport Security (HSTS)
- D) X-Content-Type-Options

**Answer: C** — HTTP Strict Transport Security (HSTS)
> ℹ️ HSTS enforces secure HTTPS connections by telling compliant browsers never to load the site over unencrypted HTTP.

**CYB-P185.** What cybersecurity assessment approach is defined as "gray box" testing?

- A) Tester has complete internal access to source code and architectural documentation.
- B) Tester has zero prior knowledge of internal systems or application infrastructure.
- C) Tester has partial knowledge of internal systems, such as standard user credentials or design docs.
- D) Tester assesses physical facility access control systems only.

**Answer: C** — Tester has partial knowledge of internal systems, such as standard user credentials or design docs.
> ℹ️ Gray box testing simulates an insider threat or authenticated user by providing partial operational context.

---

## 4. Cloud Computing

### 4.1 Source questions (from your upload) (29)

**CLD-S001.** Which of the following is a benefit of using cloud computing?

- A) Reduced need for physical storage devices
- B) Higher upfront costs
- C) Limited scalability
- D) Increased manual management

**Answer: A** — Reduced need for physical storage devices

**CLD-S002.** What does "SaaS" stand for in cloud computing?

- A) Storage as a Service
- B) Software as a Service
- C) Security as a Service
- D) System as a Service

**Answer: B** — Software as a Service

**CLD-S003.** Which cloud service model provides virtualized computing resources over the internet?

- A) SaaS
- B) PaaS
- C) IaaS
- D) DaaS

**Answer: C** — IaaS

**CLD-S004.** Which of the following is NOT a characteristic of cloud computing?

- A) On-demand self-service
- B) Broad network access
- C) Rapid elasticity
- D) Fixed pricing

**Answer: D** — Fixed pricing

**CLD-S005.** What is the purpose of a Virtual Private Cloud (VPC)?

- A) To create an isolated section of the cloud for security
- B) To reduce cloud storage costs
- C) To increase the speed of cloud services
- D) To back up data automatically

**Answer: A** — To create an isolated section of the cloud for security

**CLD-S006.** Which cloud deployment model is used by a single organization?

- A) Public Cloud
- B) Private Cloud
- C) Hybrid Cloud
- D) Community Cloud

**Answer: B** — Private Cloud

**CLD-S007.** Which of the following is a key benefit of serverless computing?

- A) Automatic scaling
- B) Dedicated server management
- C) Fixed server costs
- D) In-house data storage

**Answer: A** — Automatic scaling

**CLD-S008.** In cloud computing, what is "auto-scaling"?

- A) The automatic adjustment of computing resources based on demand
- B) The manual scaling of resources to match user requirements
- C) A feature that reduces cloud storage costs
- D) A method to secure cloud data

**Answer: A** — The automatic adjustment of computing resources based on demand

**CLD-S009.** Which of the following is a characteristic of Platform as a Service (PaaS)?

- A) Provides a platform to develop, run, and manage applications
- B) Offers raw computing resources like virtual machines
- C) Provides networking capabilities like VPN
- D) Supplies end-user software applications

**Answer: A** — Provides a platform to develop, run, and manage applications

**CLD-S010.** Which cloud deployment model is most suitable for companies that require dedicated infrastructure with enhanced security?

- A) Private Cloud
- B) Public Cloud
- C) Hybrid Cloud
- D) Community Cloud

**Answer: A** — Private Cloud

**CLD-S011.** Which of the following cloud service models allows users to develop, run, and manage applications without the complexity of building and maintaining the infrastructure?

- A) PaaS
- B) SaaS
- C) IaaS
- D) DaaS

**Answer: A** — PaaS

**CLD-S012.** Which of the following cloud services is most commonly used for scalable storage?

- A) Amazon S3
- B) Google Compute Engine
- C) Microsoft Azure VM
- D) IBM Watson

**Answer: A** — Amazon S3

**CLD-S013.** What is cloud computing?

- A) The delivery of computing services over the internet
- B) A type of computer hardware
- C) A software development method
- D) A network protocol

**Answer: A** — The delivery of computing services over the internet

**CLD-S014.** What does IaaS stand for?

- A) Information as a Service
- B) Infrastructure as a Service
- C) Internet as a Service
- D) Interface as a Service

**Answer: B** — Infrastructure as a Service

**CLD-S015.** What is the primary role of a client in a client-server architecture?

- A) To request services from a server
- B) To manage network traffic
- C) To store data
- D) To provide services to other clients

**Answer: A** — To request services from a server

**CLD-S016.** What is a server?

- A) A network protocol
- B) A computer or system that provides resources, data, services, or programs to other computers, known as clients, over a network
- C) A type of software application
- D) A security device

**Answer: B** — A computer or system that provides resources, data, services, or programs to other computers, known as clients, over a network

**CLD-S017.** What is a cloud data center?

- A) A facility used to house computer systems and associated components for cloud computing
- B) A type of cloud storage
- C) A software development platform
- D) A type of network protocol

**Answer: A** — A facility used to house computer systems and associated components for cloud computing

**CLD-S018.** What is a key advantage of using cloud data centers?

- A) Limited storage capacity
- B) High maintenance cost
- C) Scalability and flexibility in resource management
- D) Fixed resource allocation

**Answer: C** — Scalability and flexibility in resource management

**CLD-S019.** Name a popular cloud service provider.

- A) Oracle
- B) AWS (Amazon Web Services)
- C) SAP
- D) IBM

**Answer: B** — AWS (Amazon Web Services)

**CLD-S020.** What is the primary service offered by Google Cloud?

- A) Cloud computing services
- B) Hardware sales
- C) Internet browsing
- D) Social networking

**Answer: A** — Cloud computing services

**CLD-S021.** What is Microsoft Azure primarily known for?

- A) Providing search engine services
- B) Offering e-commerce solutions
- C) Providing a range of cloud services including analytics, storage, and networking
- D) Developing mobile applications

**Answer: C** — Providing a range of cloud services including analytics, storage, and networking

**CLD-S022.** Which cloud platform is known for its extensive machine learning services?

- A) IBM Cloud
- B) Google Cloud Platform
- C) Oracle Cloud
- D) Salesforce

**Answer: B** — Google Cloud Platform

**CLD-S023.** What does PaaS stand for?

- A) Program as a Service
- B) Platform as a Service
- C) Protocol as a Service
- D) Process as a Service

**Answer: B** — Platform as a Service

**CLD-S024.** Which cloud service model provides on-demand software applications?

- A) SaaS (Software as a Service)
- B) DaaS (Data as a Service)
- C) CaaS (Content as a Service)
- D) BaaS (Backup as a Service)

**Answer: A** — SaaS (Software as a Service)

**CLD-S025.** What is the main benefit of cloud elasticity?

- A) Fixed resource allocation
- B) High maintenance cost
- C) The ability to scale computing resources up or down as needed
- D) Limited storage capacity

**Answer: C** — The ability to scale computing resources up or down as needed

**CLD-S026.** What does hybrid cloud refer to?

- A) A private cloud only
- B) A public cloud only
- C) A combination of private and public cloud environments
- D) A type of cloud storage

**Answer: C** — A combination of private and public cloud environments

**CLD-S027.** What is cloud migration?

- A) The process of moving data, applications, or other business elements to a cloud computing environment
- B) The process of creating cloud applications
- C) The process of securing cloud data
- D) The process of networking cloud resources

**Answer: A** — The process of moving data, applications, or other business elements to a cloud computing environment

**CLD-S028.** Which cloud service provider is known for its simple storage service (S3)?

- A) Google Cloud
- B) Microsoft Azure
- C) IBM Cloud
- D) AWS (Amazon Web Services)

**Answer: D** — AWS (Amazon Web Services)

**CLD-S029.** Which cloud service provider should be chosen for extensive support of Linux, Windows Server, and SQL Server?

- A) Google Cloud
- B) AWS
- C) Azure
- D) IBM Cloud

**Answer: C** — Azure

**CLD-P145.** What is cloud computing?

- A) Delivery of computing services over the internet on demand

**Answer: A** — Delivery of computing services over the internet on demand

**CLD-P146.** Which cloud service model provides virtualised infrastructure such as servers and storage?

- A) SaaS
- B) IaaS
- C) PaaS
- D) CaaS

**Answer: B** — IaaS

- A) DaaS
- B) IaaS
- C) SaaS
- D) PaaS

**Answer: D** — PaaS

**CLD-P148.** Which cloud service model delivers ready-to-use software over the internet?

- A) PaaS
- B) CaaS
- C) IaaS
- D) SaaS

**CLD-P149.** Gmail and Microsoft 365 are examples of which service model?

- A) On-premises
- B) PaaS
- C) SaaS
- D) IaaS

- A) PaaS
- B) SaaS
- C) IaaS

- A) SaaS
- B) BaaS only
- C) IaaS
- D) PaaS

**CLD-P152.** Which service model gives the customer the most control over the operating system?

- A) Serverless
- B) PaaS
- C) IaaS
- D) SaaS

**CLD-P153.** Which service model requires the least management effort from the customer?

- A) On-premises
- B) PaaS
- C) IaaS
- D) SaaS

**Answer: D** — SaaS

**CLD-P154.** Which cloud deployment model is owned and operated by a third-party provider and shared among many customers?

- A) Public cloud
- B) Community cloud
- C) Private cloud
- D) Hybrid cloud

**Answer: A** — Public cloud

**CLD-P155.** Which deployment model is dedicated to a single organisation?

- A) Private cloud
- B) Public cloud
- C) Multi-tenant cloud
- D) Community cloud

**Answer: A** — Private cloud

**CLD-P156.** Which deployment model combines public and private clouds?

- A) Multi-cloud
- B) Community cloud
- C) Hybrid cloud
- D) Edge cloud

**Answer: C** — Hybrid cloud

**CLD-P157.** Which deployment model is shared by several organisations with common concerns?

- A) Private cloud
- B) Community cloud
- C) Hybrid cloud
- D) Public cloud

**Answer: B** — Community cloud

**CLD-P158.** What is multi-cloud?

- A) Using one provider in many regions
- B) Running two VMs
- C) Using a hybrid of on-premises and one cloud
- D) Using services from more than one cloud provider

**Answer: D** — Using services from more than one cloud provider

**CLD-P159.** What is the main advantage of pay-as-you-go pricing?

- A) Fixed cost regardless of usage
- B) Pay only for the resources used
- C) No billing at all
- D) Unlimited free usage

**Answer: B** — Pay only for the resources used

**CLD-P160.** What is elasticity in cloud computing?

- A) Storing data permanently
- B) Encrypting traffic
- C) Automatically scaling resources up or down with demand
- D) Fixed resource allocation

**Answer: C** — Automatically scaling resources up or down with demand

**CLD-P161.** What is scalability?

- A) The ability to delete data quickly
- B) The ability to encrypt data
- C) The ability to handle increased workload by adding resources
- D) The ability to log in from anywhere

**Answer: C** — The ability to handle increased workload by adding resources

**CLD-P162.** What is vertical scaling?

- A) Moving to another region
- B) Adding more power (CPU/RAM) to an existing machine
- C) Reducing the number of servers
- D) Adding more machines

**Answer: B** — Adding more power (CPU/RAM) to an existing machine

**CLD-P163.** What is horizontal scaling?

- A) Increasing the CPU of one machine
- B) Adding more machines or instances
- C) Upgrading the disk
- D) Reducing traffic

**Answer: B** — Adding more machines or instances

**CLD-P164.** What does auto scaling do?

- A) Encrypts disks
- B) Backs up data
- C) Adjusts the number of instances automatically based on demand
- D) Increases the price

**Answer: C** — Adjusts the number of instances automatically based on demand

**CLD-P165.** What is high availability?

- A) Designing systems to remain operational with minimal downtime
- B) Having many users
- C) Storing data on one server
- D) Using the cheapest hardware

**Answer: A** — Designing systems to remain operational with minimal downtime

**CLD-P166.** What is fault tolerance?

- A) The ability to reduce costs
- B) The ability to scale up
- C) The ability to continue operating when a component fails
- D) The ability to log events

**Answer: C** — The ability to continue operating when a component fails

**CLD-P167.** What is an availability zone (AZ)?

- A) An isolated data center location within a region
- B) A country
- C) A billing account
- D) A type of VM

**Answer: A** — An isolated data center location within a region

**CLD-P168.** What is a cloud region?

- A) A geographic area containing multiple data centers/availability zones
- B) A virtual network
- C) A backup tape
- D) A single server

**Answer: A** — A geographic area containing multiple data centers/availability zones

**CLD-P169.** Why deploy across multiple availability zones?

- A) To speed up compilation
- B) To avoid using regions
- C) To reduce licensing costs
- D) To improve resilience to data-center failures

**Answer: D** — To improve resilience to data-center failures

**CLD-P170.** What does the shared responsibility model state?

- A) The customer is responsible for everything
- B) The provider is responsible for everything
- C) The provider secures the cloud infrastructure; the customer secures what they put in it
- D) Security is optional

**Answer: C** — The provider secures the cloud infrastructure; the customer secures what they put in it

**CLD-P171.** In IaaS, who is responsible for patching the guest operating system?

- A) The customer
- B) No one
- C) The provider
- D) Both equally

**Answer: A** — The customer

**CLD-P172.** In SaaS, who is responsible for the application infrastructure?

- A) The provider
- B) The customer
- C) A third-party auditor only
- D) The end user

**Answer: A** — The provider

**CLD-P173.** What is multi-tenancy?

- A) Multiple regions
- B) A type of firewall
- C) One customer per data center
- D) Multiple customers sharing the same infrastructure while isolated logically

**Answer: D** — Multiple customers sharing the same infrastructure while isolated logically

**CLD-P174.** What is a virtual machine?

- A) A network cable
- B) A software emulation of a physical computer
- C) A physical server
- D) A storage bucket

**Answer: B** — A software emulation of a physical computer

**CLD-P175.** What is a hypervisor?

- A) A programming tool
- B) A firewall
- C) A storage service
- D) Software that creates and runs virtual machines

**Answer: D** — Software that creates and runs virtual machines

**CLD-P176.** What is a Type 1 hypervisor?

- A) Runs directly on the hardware (bare metal)
- B) Runs on top of a host OS
- C) Runs in a browser
- D) A container runtime

**Answer: A** — Runs directly on the hardware (bare metal)

**CLD-P177.** Which of the following is a Type 2 hypervisor?

- A) VMware ESXi
- B) Hyper-V on Windows Server (bare metal)
- C) Xen
- D) VirtualBox

**Answer: D** — VirtualBox

**CLD-P178.** Which of the following is a Type 1 hypervisor?

- A) VirtualBox
- B) Parallels Desktop
- C) VMware ESXi
- D) VMware Workstation

**Answer: C** — VMware ESXi

**CLD-P179.** What is a container?

- A) A hardware appliance
- B) A storage bucket
- C) A full virtual machine with its own kernel
- D) A lightweight package of an application and its dependencies sharing the host OS kernel

**Answer: D** — A lightweight package of an application and its dependencies sharing the host OS kernel

**CLD-P180.** What is the main difference between containers and VMs?

- A) Containers include a hypervisor; VMs do not
- B) VMs are lighter than containers
- C) There is no difference
- D) Containers share the host OS kernel; VMs each have their own OS

**Answer: D** — Containers share the host OS kernel; VMs each have their own OS

**CLD-P181.** What is serverless computing?

- A) Running only on-premises
- B) A type of VPN
- C) Running code without managing servers, billed per execution
- D) Computing with no servers at all

**Answer: C** — Running code without managing servers, billed per execution

**CLD-P182.** What is FaaS?

- A) File as a Service
- B) Framework as a Service
- C) Firewall as a Service
- D) Function as a Service

**Answer: D** — Function as a Service

**CLD-P183.** Which is a benefit of serverless computing?

- A) Full OS control
- B) Guaranteed lowest latency always
- C) No server management and automatic scaling
- D) Unlimited execution time

**Answer: C** — No server management and automatic scaling

**CLD-P184.** What is object storage best suited for?

- A) Storing unstructured data such as images, videos and backups
- B) Low-latency transactional databases
- C) Boot volumes only
- D) Running operating systems

**Answer: A** — Storing unstructured data such as images, videos and backups

**CLD-P185.** What is block storage best suited for?

- A) Email storage
- B) Archival backups only
- C) Static website images only
- D) Disks attached to VMs and databases

**Answer: D** — Disks attached to VMs and databases

**CLD-P186.** What is file storage best suited for?

- A) Message queues
- B) Booting VMs
- C) Caching in memory
- D) Shared file systems accessed by multiple servers

**Answer: D** — Shared file systems accessed by multiple servers

**CLD-P187.** What is a CDN?

- A) A database
- B) A network of edge servers that cache content closer to users
- C) A VPN
- D) A billing tool

**Answer: B** — A network of edge servers that cache content closer to users

**CLD-P188.** Why use a CDN?

- A) Patch servers
- B) Reduce latency by serving content from locations near the user
- C) Encrypt disks
- D) Increase database size

**Answer: B** — Reduce latency by serving content from locations near the user

**CLD-P189.** What is a Virtual Private Cloud (VPC)?

- A) A VPN client
- B) A physical private data center
- C) A type of CDN
- D) A logically isolated private network within a public cloud

**Answer: D** — A logically isolated private network within a public cloud

**CLD-P190.** What is a subnet in a VPC?

- A) A storage class
- B) A range of IP addresses within the VPC
- C) A type of VM
- D) A billing group

**Answer: B** — A range of IP addresses within the VPC

**CLD-P191.** What is a security group in AWS?

- A) A set of regions
- B) A virtual firewall controlling inbound and outbound traffic for instances
- C) A user group
- D) A billing group

**Answer: B** — A virtual firewall controlling inbound and outbound traffic for instances

**CLD-P192.** What is IAM?

- A) Identity and Access Management
- B) Instance Allocation Module
- C) Internal Application Monitor
- D) Internet Access Manager

**Answer: A** — Identity and Access Management

**CLD-P193.** What is an IAM role?

- A) A billing plan
- B) A network gateway
- C) A physical server
- D) A set of permissions that can be assumed by users or services

**Answer: D** — A set of permissions that can be assumed by users or services

**CLD-P194.** What is an S3 bucket?

- A) A virtual machine
- B) A container for objects in Amazon S3
- C) A database
- D) A load balancer

**Answer: B** — A container for objects in Amazon S3

**CLD-P195.** What is DevOps' relation to cloud computing?

- A) DevOps replaces the cloud
- B) DevOps only works on-premises
- C) Cloud provides automation and on-demand infrastructure that suits DevOps
- D) No relation

**Answer: C** — Cloud provides automation and on-demand infrastructure that suits DevOps

**CLD-P196.** What is Infrastructure as Code (IaC)?

- A) Buying hardware with code
- B) A type of firewall
- C) Writing code inside data centers
- D) Managing infrastructure through machine-readable definition files

**Answer: D** — Managing infrastructure through machine-readable definition files

**CLD-P197.** Which tool is used for multi-cloud Infrastructure as Code?

- A) Terraform
- B) Photoshop
- C) Wireshark
- D) Excel

**Answer: A** — Terraform

**CLD-P198.** What is a service level agreement (SLA)?

- A) A contract defining expected service levels such as uptime
- B) A billing invoice
- C) A network protocol
- D) A software licence

**Answer: A** — A contract defining expected service levels such as uptime

**CLD-P199.** What does 99.99% availability ("four nines") allow per year, roughly?

- A) No downtime
- B) About 8.7 hours of downtime
- C) About 3.65 days of downtime
- D) About 52 minutes of downtime

**Answer: D** — About 52 minutes of downtime

**CLD-P200.** What does 99.9% availability ("three nines") allow per year, roughly?

- A) About 5 seconds of downtime
- B) About 52 minutes of downtime
- C) About 8.76 hours of downtime
- D) About 3.65 days of downtime

**Answer: C** — About 8.76 hours of downtime

**CLD-P201.** What is vendor lock-in?

- A) A security certificate
- B) A type of SLA
- C) Locking a VM
- D) Difficulty moving away from a provider because of proprietary services

**Answer: D** — Difficulty moving away from a provider because of proprietary services

**CLD-P202.** What is the difference between RTO and RPO?

- A) RTO is data loss; RPO is downtime
- B) Both mean data loss
- C) Both mean downtime
- D) RTO is acceptable downtime; RPO is acceptable data loss period

**Answer: D** — RTO is acceptable downtime; RPO is acceptable data loss period

**CLD-P203.** What is disaster recovery?

- A) Restoring systems and data after a major failure
- B) Preventing all failures
- C) Encrypting backups
- D) Reducing cloud costs

**Answer: A** — Restoring systems and data after a major failure

**CLD-P204.** Which disaster recovery strategy keeps a scaled-down copy of the environment always running?

- A) Multi-site active-active
- B) Backup and restore
- C) Cold standby with nothing running
- D) Pilot light

**Answer: D** — Pilot light

**CLD-P205.** What is a cloud migration strategy that moves an application as-is without changes called?

- A) Retire
- B) Repurchase
- C) Rehost (lift and shift)
- D) Refactor

**Answer: C** — Rehost (lift and shift)

**CLD-P206.** What is "refactoring" in cloud migration?

- A) Moving as-is
- B) Re-architecting the application to use cloud-native features
- C) Replacing with SaaS
- D) Decommissioning it

**Answer: B** — Re-architecting the application to use cloud-native features

**CLD-P207.** What are the 6 Rs of cloud migration commonly listed as?

- A) Rehost, Replatform, Repurchase, Refactor, Retire, Retain
- B) Rehost, Reboot, Remove, Reset, Retire, Review
- C) Reduce, Reuse, Recycle, Rehost, Retire, Retain
- D) Restore, Replicate, Repair, Reboot, Retire, Retain

**Answer: A** — Rehost, Replatform, Repurchase, Refactor, Retire, Retain

**CLD-P208.** What is cloud bursting?

- A) Deleting cloud resources
- B) A cloud outage
- C) A security attack
- D) Overflowing workloads from a private cloud into a public cloud during peaks

**Answer: D** — Overflowing workloads from a private cloud into a public cloud during peaks

**CLD-P209.** What is edge computing?

- A) Processing in the browser only
- B) Processing data close to where it is generated
- C) Processing only in the central data center
- D) Storing backups offline

**Answer: B** — Processing data close to where it is generated

**CLD-P210.** What is a managed service?

- A) A service managed by the customer's admin
- B) A physical appliance
- C) A service where the provider handles operation, maintenance and scaling
- D) An unmonitored service

**Answer: C** — A service where the provider handles operation, maintenance and scaling

**CLD-P211.** What is Kubernetes used for?

- A) Editing documents
- B) Orchestrating containerised applications
- C) Hosting DNS only
- D) Storing objects

**Answer: B** — Orchestrating containerised applications

**CLD-P212.** What is cloud-native?

- A) Any app on a VM
- B) Designed to exploit cloud features such as microservices, containers and auto-scaling
- C) Only SaaS apps
- D) Apps that run offline

**Answer: B** — Designed to exploit cloud features such as microservices, containers and auto-scaling

**CLD-P213.** What is a microservice architecture?

- A) An application built as small independent services
- B) A single large application
- C) A network topology
- D) A type of database

**Answer: A** — An application built as small independent services

**CLD-P214.** What is an API gateway?

- A) A VM image
- B) A managed entry point that routes and secures API requests
- C) A database engine
- D) A firewall for disks

**Answer: B** — A managed entry point that routes and secures API requests

**CLD-P215.** What is a cloud-based data lake?

- A) A relational database
- B) A repository that stores raw data in its native format at scale
- C) A CDN
- D) A load balancer

**Answer: B** — A repository that stores raw data in its native format at scale

**CLD-P216.** What is Amazon Glacier (S3 Glacier) designed for?

- A) Web hosting
- B) High-speed databases
- C) Container orchestration
- D) Low-cost long-term archival storage

**Answer: D** — Low-cost long-term archival storage

**CLD-P217.** What is a spot instance (AWS)?

- A) A reserved fixed-price VM
- B) Spare capacity offered at a discount that can be reclaimed
- C) A free tier VM
- D) A dedicated host

**Answer: B** — Spare capacity offered at a discount that can be reclaimed

**CLD-P218.** What is a reserved instance / savings plan?

- A) A discount for committing to usage for 1 or 3 years
- B) A spot bid
- C) A dedicated network
- D) A free VM

**Answer: A** — A discount for committing to usage for 1 or 3 years

**CLD-P219.** Which pricing option is best for a steady predictable workload?

- A) Free tier
- B) Reserved instances / savings plans
- C) On-demand only
- D) Spot instances

**Answer: B** — Reserved instances / savings plans

**CLD-P220.** Which pricing option is best for fault-tolerant, interruptible batch jobs?

- A) Reserved instances
- B) Spot instances
- C) On-demand with commitment
- D) Dedicated hosts

**Answer: B** — Spot instances

**CLD-P221.** What is the AWS Free Tier?

- A) Unlimited free usage forever
- B) A free support plan only
- C) A discount for enterprises
- D) Limited free usage of some services for new accounts

**Answer: D** — Limited free usage of some services for new accounts

**CLD-P222.** Which Azure feature groups related resources for management?

- A) Resource group
- B) Storage account
- C) Load balancer
- D) Availability set

**Answer: A** — Resource group

**CLD-P223.** Which Azure service provides serverless workflows with visual designer?

- A) Logic Apps
- B) Blob Storage
- C) Azure DNS
- D) Azure Monitor

**Answer: A** — Logic Apps

**CLD-P224.** What is Microsoft Entra ID formerly known as?

- A) Azure Key Vault

**CLD-P234.** What is the primary purpose of an AWS Transit Gateway?

- A) To encrypt data stored in S3 buckets
- B) To act as a central network hub connecting VPCs and on-premises networks
- C) To run containerized serverless microservices
- D) To host relational databases with multi-region replication

**Answer: B** — To act as a central network hub connecting VPCs and on-premises networks
> ℹ️ AWS Transit Gateway simplifies complex network topographies by acting as a central hub for inter-VPC and hybrid network traffic routing.

**CLD-P235.** In cloud disaster recovery planning, what does Recovery Point Objective (RPO) measure?

- A) The maximum acceptable duration of service downtime following an outage.
- B) The maximum acceptable amount of data loss measured in time prior to a disaster.
- C) The total financial cost incurred during data restoration.
- D) The count of active backup instances in secondary data centers.

**Answer: B** — The maximum acceptable amount of data loss measured in time prior to a disaster.
> ℹ️ RPO defines the maximum tolerable data loss window (e.g. 15 minutes of data), whereas RTO defines acceptable recovery time.

**CLD-P236.** Which Google Cloud service provides fully managed, serverless event-driven message queuing and asynchronous messaging?

- A) Cloud Bigtable
- B) Pub/Sub
- C) Cloud Storage
- D) Cloud Functions

**Answer: B** — Pub/Sub
> ℹ️ Google Cloud Pub/Sub provides high-throughput, latency-optimized asynchronous messaging for event-driven systems and data ingestion pipelines.

**CLD-P237.** In Kubernetes and cloud-native architecture, what is a "Sidecar" pattern?

- A) Running two identical Kubernetes clusters in active-active mode.
- B) Deploying a helper container alongside a primary application container in the same pod.
- C) Mounting external virtual hard drives directly to VM nodes.
- D) Backing up container images automatically to a remote registry.

**Answer: B** — Deploying a helper container alongside a primary application container in the same pod.
> ℹ️ The sidecar design pattern attaches a supporting container (e.g. for logging, proxying, monitoring) to the main container inside the same Kubernetes Pod.

**CLD-P238.** Which AWS feature allows EC2 instances in a private subnet to connect to the internet while preventing incoming connections initiated from the internet?

- A) Internet Gateway (IGW)
- B) NAT Gateway
- C) VPC Peering
- D) Security Group

**Answer: B** — NAT Gateway
> ℹ️ A NAT Gateway enables outbound internet access for instances in private subnets while blocking unsolicited inbound connections from the public internet.

**PSE-S001.** What is the output of the following pseudocode?

```
Integer n = 5
Integer result = 1
for (i = 1; i <= n; i++)
result = result * i
if (result > 10)
break
Print result
```

- A) 5
- B) 10
- C) 120
- D) 24

**Answer: D** — 24

**PSE-S002.** What will be the output of the following pseudocode?

```
Function Fibo(Integer n)
  if (n <= 1)
    return n
  else
    return Fibo(n-1) + Fibo(n-2)
End Function
Print Fibo(5)
```

- A) 3
- B) 5
- C) 8
- D) 7

**Answer: B** — 5

for (i = 0; i < length(arr); i++)

sum = sum + arr[i]
Print sum
```

- A) 0
- B) 12
- C) 14
- D) 24

**Answer: B** — 12

count = count + 1
Print count
```

- D) 4

**PSE-S005.** What will be the output of the following pseudocode?

```
Integer a = 3, b = 5, c = 7
if (a < b)
if (b < c)
a = c - a
else
b = c + a
Print a, b, c
```

- A) 4, 5, 7
- B) 7, 5, 7
- C) 3, 5, 7
- D) 4, 7, 7

**Answer: A** — 4, 5, 7
> ⚠️ **Source check:** Source key said B (7, 5, 7) but the source's own explanation computes a = 7 − 3 = 4, so the output is 4, 5, 7. Corrected to A.

arr[i] = arr[i-1]
arr[0] = temp
Print arr
```

- D) 3 4 5 1 2

return 1
else

End Function
Output = mystery(5)
```

- A) 5
- B) 15
- C) 20
- D) 25

**Answer: B** — 15
> ⚠️ **Source check:** Source key printed 'C) 15' but 15 is option B. mystery(5) = 5 × mystery(3) = 5 × 3 × mystery(1) = 15.

**PSE-S011.** What is the output of the following pseudocode?

```
Function reverseArray(arr)
n = length(arr)
for i = 0 to n/2 - 1

arr[n-i-1] = temp
end for
return arr
End Function
Output = reverseArray([1, 2, 3, 4, 5])
```

- A) [5, 4, 3, 2, 1]
- B) [1, 2, 3, 4, 5]

**PSE-P184.** What will be the output of the following pseudocode?

```
Integer a, b, c
Set a = 4, b = 7, c = 2
a = a + b * c
b = (a - b) MOD c
Print a + b
```

- A) 18
- B) 19
- C) 22
- D) 23

**Answer: B** — 19
> ℹ️ `a = 4 + (7 * 2) = 18`. `b = (18 - 7) MOD 2 = 11 MOD 2 = 1`. `a + b = 18 + 1 = 19`.

**PSE-P185.** What will be the output of the following pseudocode?

```
Integer arr[] = {3, 8, 2, 11, 6}
Integer val = 0
For each element x in arr
    If x MOD 2 == 0 then
        val = val + x
    End If
End For
Print val
```

- A) 14
- B) 16
- C) 19
- D) 30

**Answer: B** — 16
> ℹ️ The loop sums even numbers in `arr`: 8 + 2 + 6 = 16.

**PSE-P186.** What will be the output of the following pseudocode?

```
Integer fun(Integer n)
    If n <= 1 then
        Return 1
    End If
    Return n + fun(n - 2)
End Function

Print fun(5)
```

- A) 8
- B) 9
- C) 12
- D) 15

**Answer: B** — 9
> ℹ️ Recursive calls: `fun(5) = 5 + fun(3) = 5 + (3 + fun(1)) = 5 + 3 + 1 = 9`.

**PSE-P187.** What will be the output of the following pseudocode?

```
Integer x = 12, y = 5
Integer result = 0
While x >= y
    x = x - y
    result = result + 1
End While
Print result, x
```

- A) 2, 2
- B) 2, 5
- C) 3, 2
- D) 2, 0

**Answer: A** — 2, 2
> ℹ️ Iteration 1: x = 7, result = 1. Iteration 2: x = 2, result = 2. Iteration 3 stops as 2 < 5. Prints quotient (2) and remainder (2).

**PSE-P188.** What will be the output of the following pseudocode?

```
Integer p = 3, q = 4, r = 1
Set r = (p XOR q) + (q AND p)
Print r
```

- A) 5
- B) 6
- C) 7
- D) 12

**Answer: C** — 7
> ℹ️ In binary: p = 0011, q = 0100. `p XOR q` = 0111 (7). `q AND p` = 0000 (0). `7 + 0 = 7`.

## 6. DevOps

### 6.2 Practice questions (243)

**DVO-P001.** Which Git command is used to: initialise a new Git repository?

- A) git push
- B) git pull
- C) git branch
- D) git init

**Answer: D** — git init

**DVO-P002.** What does the Git command `git init` do?

- A) Move HEAD/branch to a previous commit, optionally discarding changes
- B) Show the commit history
- C) Initialise a new Git repository
- D) Merge another branch into the current branch

**Answer: C** — Initialise a new Git repository

**DVO-P003.** Which Git command is used to: copy a remote repository to your machine?

- A) git merge
- B) git branch
- C) git clone
- D) git log

**Answer: C** — git clone

**DVO-P004.** What does the Git command `git clone` do?

- A) Copy a remote repository to your machine
- B) Switch to another branch
- C) Unstage a file while keeping changes (restore --staged)
- D) Stage a file for commit

**Answer: A** — Copy a remote repository to your machine

**DVO-P005.** Which Git command is used to: show the working tree status?

- A) git status
- B) git init
- C) git push
- D) git revert

**Answer: A** — git status

**Answer: B** — Show the working tree status

**Answer: C** — git add

**Answer: A** — Stage a file for commit

**Answer: C** — Record staged changes with a message

- B) Owner r-x, group rwx, others rwx
- C) Owner rw-, group r--, others r--
- D) Everyone rwx

**Answer: A** — Owner rwx, group r-x, others r-x

**DVO-P231.** What does the Linux permission 644 mean for a file?

- A) Owner rwx, group r-x, others r-x
- B) Everyone rw-
- C) Owner r--, group rw-, others rw-
- D) Owner rw-, group r--, others r--

**Answer: D** — Owner rw-, group r--, others r--

**DVO-P232.** What does chmod +x script.sh do?

- A) Makes the file read-only
- B) Makes the file executable
- C) Deletes the file
- D) Changes the owner

**Answer: B** — Makes the file executable

**DVO-P233.** Which shell operator redirects standard output to a file, overwriting it?

- A) |
- B) >
- C) >>
- D) <

**Answer: B** — >

**DVO-P234.** Which shell operator appends standard output to a file?

- A) |
- B) >
- C) &
- D) >>

**Answer: D** — >>

**DVO-P235.** What does the pipe operator (|) do in a shell?

- A) Runs commands in parallel in the background
- B) Comments out a command
- C) Sends the output of one command as input to another
- D) Redirects output to a file

**Answer: C** — Sends the output of one command as input to another

**DVO-P236.** Which file extension typically denotes a shell script?

- A) .sh
- B) .bat
- C) .exe
- D) .py

**Answer: A** — .sh

**DVO-P237.** What does "#!/bin/bash" at the top of a script indicate?

- A) A comment with no meaning
- B) The output directory
- C) The script's author
- D) The interpreter used to run the script

**Answer: D** — The interpreter used to run the script

**DVO-P238.** What does cron do?

- A) Monitors networks
- B) Compiles code
- C) Manages users
- D) Schedules commands to run at specified times

**Answer: D** — Schedules commands to run at specified times

**DVO-P239.** In Ansible, what is a "Playbook"?

- A) A compiled shell script binary.
- B) A YAML file defining tasks, configuration steps, and target hosts for automation.
- C) A private Docker image registry.
- D) A database schema version control log.

**Answer: B** — A YAML file defining tasks, configuration steps, and target hosts for automation.
> ℹ️ Ansible Playbooks express configurations, deployment steps, and orchestration tasks in human-readable YAML format.

**DVO-P240.** What is the primary benefit of using multi-stage builds in a Dockerfile?

- A) Running multiple containers concurrently on a single host.
- B) Drastically reducing final image size by copying only build artifacts to a minimal runtime image.
- C) Automatically scaling container CPU and memory limits.
- D) Bypassing container security scans.

**Answer: B** — Drastically reducing final image size by copying only build artifacts to a minimal runtime image.
> ℹ️ Multi-stage builds separate build-time dependencies from runtime requirements, resulting in leaner, more secure container images.

**DVO-P241.** In Terraform, what file stores the state mapping between your infrastructure code and real-world deployed resources?

- A) `terraform.tfvars`
- B) `main.tf`
- C) `terraform.tfstate`
- D) `.terraform.lock.hcl`

**Answer: C** — `terraform.tfstate`
> ℹ️ `terraform.tfstate` records resource metadata and attributes to track managed infrastructure state.

**DVO-P242.** Which Dockerfile instruction specifies default commands and arguments that execute when a container starts up?

- A) `FROM`
- B) `RUN`
- C) `COPY`
- D) `CMD`

**Answer: D** — CMD
> ℹ️ `CMD` provides defaults for an executing container, whereas `RUN` executes commands during image build time.

**DVO-P243.** In Prometheus monitoring architecture, what is a "Target"?

- A) A database used for persistent metric storage.
- B) An end system or metric endpoint scraped by Prometheus to collect metrics.
- C) A dashboard layout template in Grafana.
- D) An alert notification recipient email.

**Answer: B** — An end system or metric endpoint scraped by Prometheus to collect metrics.
> ℹ️ Targets are monitored HTTP endpoints exposing metrics in Prometheus format that Prometheus periodically scrapes.

## 7. DBMS

### 7.2 Practice questions (227)

**DBM-P001.** Consider the relation R(A, B, C, D, E) with FDs: CE → A, AE → B, CD → E, BD → E. What is the attribute closure {B}⁺?

- A) BD
- B) AB
- C) B
- D) BC

**Answer: C** — B
> ℹ️ Repeatedly add the right-hand sides of FDs whose left sides are contained in the current set.

- D) BE

**Answer: D** — BE
> ℹ️ Repeatedly add the right-hand sides of FDs whose left sides are contained in the current set.

**DBM-P041.** Relation R(A, B, C, D) has the functional dependencies AC → D, B → C. What is the highest normal form that R satisfies?

- A) 2NF
- B) BCNF
- C) 3NF
- D) 1NF

**Answer: D** — 1NF
> ℹ️ Candidate key(s): AB.

**DBM-P042.** Relation R(A, B, C, D) has the functional dependencies A → C, AB → D, BC → A. What is the highest normal form that R satisfies?

- A) BCNF
- B) 2NF
- C) 3NF
- D) 1NF

**Answer: C** — 3NF
> ℹ️ Candidate key(s): AB and BC.

**DBM-P043.** Relation R(A, B, C, D) has the functional dependencies B → C, AD → B, B → A. What is the highest normal form that R satisfies?

- C) BCNF
- D) 3NF

**Answer: A** — 1NF
> ℹ️ Candidate key(s): AD and BD.

- D) BCNF

**Answer: B** — 1NF

- D) 1NF

**Answer: B** — 3NF
> ℹ️ Candidate key(s): AC and BCD.

**Answer: B** — 1NF

- D) 3NF

**Answer: D** — 3NF
> ℹ️ Candidate key(s): ABD and BCD.

**DBM-P048.** Relation R(A, B, C, D) has the functional dependencies C → A, D → B, BD → A. What is the highest normal form that R satisfies?

- D) BCNF

**Answer: A** — 1NF

**Answer: C** — 1NF
> ℹ️ Candidate key(s): ACD.

- D) 1NF

**DBM-P079.** What does DBMS stand for?

- A) Database Management System
- B) Database Migration Service
- C) Data Base Modelling Standard
- D) Data Backup Management System

**Answer: A** — Database Management System

**DBM-P080.** Which of the following is a DBMS?

- A) Notepad
- B) MySQL
- C) Excel
- D) Chrome

**Answer: B** — MySQL

**DBM-P081.** What is a database?

- A) An organised collection of related data
- B) A programming language
- C) A type of network
- D) A spreadsheet only

**Answer: A** — An organised collection of related data

**DBM-P082.** What is the main advantage of a DBMS over a file system?

- A) Uses less RAM always
- B) No need for backups
- C) Cannot have duplicates ever
- D) Reduced redundancy and better consistency and security

**Answer: D** — Reduced redundancy and better consistency and security

**DBM-P083.** What is data redundancy?

- A) Backing up data
- B) Unnecessary duplication of data
- C) Encrypting data
- D) Deleting data

**Answer: B** — Unnecessary duplication of data

**DBM-P084.** What is data independence?

- A) Data without keys
- B) Changing the schema at one level without affecting the next higher level
- C) Data stored on multiple disks
- D) Data stored without a schema

**Answer: B** — Changing the schema at one level without affecting the next higher level

**DBM-P085.** What is logical data independence?

- A) Ability to change the physical storage without changing the conceptual schema
- B) Ability to change the DBMS vendor
- C) Ability to change hardware
- D) Ability to change the conceptual schema without changing external schemas or applications

**Answer: D** — Ability to change the conceptual schema without changing external schemas or applications

**DBM-P086.** What is physical data independence?

- A) Ability to change table names
- B) Ability to change the physical storage without changing the conceptual schema
- C) Ability to change user accounts
- D) Ability to change the conceptual schema without changing views

**Answer: B** — Ability to change the physical storage without changing the conceptual schema

**DBM-P087.** How many levels are there in the three-schema architecture?

- A) 3 (external, conceptual, internal)
- B) 2
- C) 4
- D) 5

**Answer: A** — 3 (external, conceptual, internal)

**DBM-P088.** Which schema describes how data is physically stored?

- A) Logical view schema
- B) Internal schema
- C) External schema
- D) Conceptual schema

**Answer: B** — Internal schema

**DBM-P089.** Which schema describes what users see (views)?

- A) Internal schema
- B) Conceptual schema
- C) Physical schema
- D) External schema

**Answer: D** — External schema

**DBM-P090.** What is a schema?

- A) A query result
- B) The data stored at one moment
- C) A backup file
- D) The logical structure/design of a database

**Answer: D** — The logical structure/design of a database

**DBM-P091.** What is an instance of a database?

- A) A table definition
- B) The database design
- C) A DBMS software copy
- D) The data stored in the database at a particular moment

**Answer: D** — The data stored in the database at a particular moment

**DBM-P092.** Who is responsible for the design and administration of a database?

- A) DBA
- B) Compiler
- C) End user
- D) Network engineer

**Answer: A** — DBA

**DBM-P093.** What is an entity in the ER model?

- A) A query
- B) A table column
- C) An index
- D) A real-world object about which data is stored

**Answer: D** — A real-world object about which data is stored

**DBM-P094.** What is an attribute?

- A) A property of an entity
- B) A relationship
- C) A key only
- D) A table

**Answer: A** — A property of an entity

**DBM-P095.** What is a weak entity?

- A) An entity that cannot be uniquely identified by its own attributes alone
- B) An entity with too many attributes
- C) An entity that is deleted
- D) An entity with no attributes

**Answer: A** — An entity that cannot be uniquely identified by its own attributes alone

**DBM-P096.** In an ER diagram, what shape represents an entity?

- A) Rectangle
- B) Triangle
- C) Diamond
- D) Ellipse

**Answer: A** — Rectangle

**DBM-P097.** In an ER diagram, what shape represents an attribute?

- A) Rectangle
- B) Diamond
- C) Circle with arrows
- D) Ellipse

**Answer: D** — Ellipse

**DBM-P098.** In an ER diagram, what shape represents a relationship?

- A) Rectangle
- B) Diamond
- C) Parallelogram
- D) Ellipse

**Answer: B** — Diamond

**DBM-P099.** What is a multivalued attribute?

- A) A key attribute
- B) A derived attribute
- C) An attribute with a long value
- D) An attribute that can hold multiple values for one entity, e.g. phone numbers

**Answer: D** — An attribute that can hold multiple values for one entity, e.g. phone numbers

**DBM-P100.** What is a derived attribute?

- A) An attribute whose value can be computed from others, e.g. age from date of birth
- B) A foreign key
- C) A composite attribute
- D) A key attribute

**Answer: A** — An attribute whose value can be computed from others, e.g. age from date of birth

**DBM-P101.** What is a composite attribute?

- A) A computed attribute
- B) An attribute with multiple values
- C) A primary key
- D) An attribute that can be divided into sub-parts, e.g. name into first and last

**Answer: D** — An attribute that can be divided into sub-parts, e.g. name into first and last

**DBM-P102.** What is cardinality in a relationship?

- A) The data type of an attribute
- B) The size of a table
- C) The number of columns in a table
- D) The number of entity instances that can be associated (1:1, 1:N, M:N)

**Answer: D** — The number of entity instances that can be associated (1:1, 1:N, M:N)

**DBM-P103.** How is a many-to-many (M:N) relationship implemented in a relational database?

- A) It cannot be implemented
- B) By a separate junction (associative) table
- C) By duplicating rows
- D) By a single foreign key column

**Answer: B** — By a separate junction (associative) table

**DBM-P104.** How is a one-to-many relationship implemented?

- A) Foreign key on the "one" side
- B) Composite key only
- C) Junction table always
- D) Foreign key on the "many" side

**Answer: D** — Foreign key on the "many" side

**DBM-P105.** What is a super key?

- A) A set of attributes that uniquely identifies a row
- B) A minimal set that identifies a row
- C) A foreign key
- D) A key with NULLs

**Answer: A** — A set of attributes that uniquely identifies a row

**DBM-P106.** What is a candidate key?

- A) A minimal super key
- B) A duplicate key
- C) Any set of attributes
- D) Any foreign key

**Answer: A** — A minimal super key

**DBM-P107.** What is a primary key?

- A) A candidate key chosen to uniquely identify rows
- B) A foreign key
- C) A non-unique key
- D) A key that may be NULL

**Answer: A** — A candidate key chosen to uniquely identify rows

**DBM-P108.** What is an alternate key?

- A) A foreign key
- B) A super key of two columns
- C) A candidate key that is not chosen as the primary key
- D) A composite key

**Answer: C** — A candidate key that is not chosen as the primary key

**DBM-P109.** What is a foreign key?

- A) An encrypted key
- B) A key used for hashing
- C) A key that may repeat within the same table only
- D) An attribute referencing the primary key of another table

**Answer: D** — An attribute referencing the primary key of another table

**DBM-P110.** What is a composite key?

- A) A key made of two or more attributes
- B) A key stored in two tables
- C) A key with NULLs
- D) A generated key

**Answer: A** — A key made of two or more attributes

**DBM-P111.** What is a surrogate key?

- A) An artificial key such as an auto-increment ID
- B) A natural business key
- C) A composite key
- D) A foreign key

**Answer: A** — An artificial key such as an auto-increment ID

**DBM-P112.** What is referential integrity?

- A) Data must be encrypted
- B) Foreign key values must match an existing primary key value or be NULL
- C) Tables must have the same number of rows
- D) Primary keys must be numeric

**Answer: B** — Foreign key values must match an existing primary key value or be NULL

**DBM-P113.** What is entity integrity?

- A) Foreign keys cannot be NULL
- B) Tables must have indexes
- C) Every column must be unique
- D) The primary key cannot be NULL

**Answer: D** — The primary key cannot be NULL

**DBM-P114.** What is a relation in the relational model?

- A) A table
- B) A join
- C) A query
- D) A key

**Answer: A** — A table

**DBM-P115.** What is a tuple?

- A) A relation's name
- B) A row of a relation
- C) A column of a relation
- D) A key

**Answer: B** — A row of a relation

**DBM-P116.** What is the degree of a relation?

- A) The number of tables
- B) The number of attributes (columns)
- C) The number of tuples (rows)
- D) The number of keys

**Answer: B** — The number of attributes (columns)

**DBM-P117.** What is the cardinality of a relation (in the relational model)?

- A) The number of keys
- B) The number of attributes
- C) The number of tuples (rows)
- D) The number of tables

**Answer: C** — The number of tuples (rows)

**DBM-P118.** What is a domain?

- A) The set of permitted values for an attribute
- B) A table name
- C) A network domain
- D) A database server

**Answer: A** — The set of permitted values for an attribute

**DBM-P119.** Which relational algebra operator selects rows based on a condition?

- A) Union (∪)
- B) Projection (π)
- C) Selection (σ)
- D) Join (⨝)

**Answer: C** — Selection (σ)

**DBM-P120.** Which relational algebra operator selects columns?

- A) Projection (π)
- B) Selection (σ)
- C) Rename (ρ)
- D) Cartesian product (×)

**Answer: A** — Projection (π)

**DBM-P121.** Which relational algebra operator renames a relation or attribute?

- A) Difference (−)
- B) Rename (ρ)
- C) Selection (σ)
- D) Projection (π)

**Answer: B** — Rename (ρ)

**DBM-P122.** Which operator combines tuples from two relations that are union-compatible, removing duplicates?

- A) Selection (σ)
- B) Rename (ρ)
- C) Union (∪)
- D) Cartesian product (×)

**Answer: C** — Union (∪)

**DBM-P123.** Which operator returns tuples in one relation but not the other?

- A) Intersection (∩)
- B) Join (⨝)
- C) Union (∪)
- D) Set difference (−)

**Answer: D** — Set difference (−)

**DBM-P124.** If relation R has 4 tuples and S has 5 tuples, how many tuples does R × S have?

- A) 1
- B) 5
- C) 9
- D) 20

**Answer: D** — 20

**DBM-P125.** If R has 3 attributes and S has 2 attributes, how many attributes does R × S have?

- A) 2
- B) 3
- C) 5
- D) 6

**Answer: C** — 5

**DBM-P126.** Which relational algebra operation is required for a query like "students who have taken ALL courses"?

- A) Division (÷)
- B) Rename
- C) Union
- D) Selection

**Answer: A** — Division (÷)

**DBM-P127.** What is normalisation?

- A) Duplicating tables for speed
- B) Organising data to reduce redundancy and dependency anomalies
- C) Adding indexes
- D) Encrypting data

**Answer: B** — Organising data to reduce redundancy and dependency anomalies

**DBM-P128.** What does 1NF require?

- A) No partial dependencies
- B) No transitive dependencies
- C) Every determinant is a candidate key
- D) All attributes hold atomic (indivisible) values

**Answer: D** — All attributes hold atomic (indivisible) values

**DBM-P129.** What does 2NF require (in addition to 1NF)?

- A) No multivalued dependencies
- B) Every determinant is a candidate key
- C) No transitive dependency
- D) No partial dependency of non-key attributes on part of a composite key

**Answer: D** — No partial dependency of non-key attributes on part of a composite key

**DBM-P130.** What does 3NF require (in addition to 2NF)?

- A) No join dependency
- B) No transitive dependency of non-key attributes on the key
- C) Atomic values
- D) No partial dependency

**Answer: B** — No transitive dependency of non-key attributes on the key

**DBM-P131.** What does BCNF require?

- A) No partial dependencies only
- B) Only atomic values
- C) For every non-trivial FD X → Y, X is a super key
- D) No multivalued dependencies

**Answer: C** — For every non-trivial FD X → Y, X is a super key

**DBM-P132.** Which normal form deals with multivalued dependencies?

- A) 3NF
- B) 4NF
- C) 2NF
- D) BCNF

**Answer: B** — 4NF

**DBM-P133.** Which normal form deals with join dependencies?

- A) BCNF
- B) 5NF
- C) 4NF
- D) 2NF

**Answer: B** — 5NF

**DBM-P134.** Which normal form is stricter: BCNF or 3NF?

- A) 3NF
- B) BCNF
- C) Neither is stricter
- D) They are the same

**Answer: B** — BCNF

**DBM-P135.** What is a partial dependency?

- A) A key depends on another key
- B) A foreign key depends on a primary key
- C) A non-key attribute depends on only part of a composite key
- D) A non-key attribute depends on a non-key attribute

**Answer: C** — A non-key attribute depends on only part of a composite key

**DBM-P136.** What is a transitive dependency?

- A) A non-key attribute depends on another non-key attribute that depends on the key
- B) A non-key attribute depends on part of the key
- C) A key depends on itself
- D) A multivalued dependency

**Answer: A** — A non-key attribute depends on another non-key attribute that depends on the key

**DBM-P137.** What is a functional dependency X → Y?

- A) Y determines X
- B) X is a foreign key of Y
- C) X and Y are unrelated
- D) Each value of X determines exactly one value of Y

**Answer: D** — Each value of X determines exactly one value of Y

**DBM-P138.** What is a trivial functional dependency?

- A) X → Y with X empty
- B) X → Y where Y is a key
- C) X → Y where X is a key
- D) X → Y where Y is a subset of X

**Answer: D** — X → Y where Y is a subset of X

**DBM-P139.** State Armstrong's axiom of reflexivity.

- A) If X → Y and Y → Z then X → Z
- B) If X → Y then Y → X
- C) If Y ⊆ X then X → Y
- D) If X → Y then XZ → YZ

**Answer: C** — If Y ⊆ X then X → Y

**DBM-P140.** State Armstrong's axiom of transitivity.

- A) If X → Y then XZ → YZ
- B) If Y ⊆ X then X → Y
- C) If X → Y and Y → Z then X → Z
- D) If X → Y then X → YZ only

**Answer: C** — If X → Y and Y → Z then X → Z

**DBM-P141.** State Armstrong's axiom of augmentation.

- A) If X → Y and Y → Z then X → Z
- B) If X → Y then XZ → YZ
- C) If X → Y then Y → X
- D) If Y ⊆ X then X → Y

**Answer: B** — If X → Y then XZ → YZ

**DBM-P142.** What is denormalisation?

- A) Removing all keys
- B) Intentionally adding redundancy to improve read performance
- C) Converting to 1NF
- D) Encrypting a table

**Answer: B** — Intentionally adding redundancy to improve read performance

**DBM-P143.** What does ACID stand for?

- A) Availability, Consistency, Isolation, Durability
- B) Accuracy, Consistency, Integrity, Durability
- C) Atomicity, Consistency, Isolation, Durability
- D) Atomicity, Concurrency, Isolation, Data

**Answer: C** — Atomicity, Consistency, Isolation, Durability

**DBM-P144.** What does atomicity guarantee?

- A) Transactions never overlap
- B) Data is always consistent
- C) A transaction is all-or-nothing
- D) Data is stored permanently

**Answer: C** — A transaction is all-or-nothing

**DBM-P145.** What does consistency guarantee?

- A) Data is encrypted
- B) Transactions run one at a time
- C) Data is duplicated
- D) A transaction moves the database from one valid state to another

**Answer: D** — A transaction moves the database from one valid state to another

**DBM-P146.** What does isolation guarantee?

- A) Data is always available
- B) A transaction is all-or-nothing
- C) Committed data survives failures
- D) Concurrent transactions do not interfere with each other

**Answer: D** — Concurrent transactions do not interfere with each other

**DBM-P147.** What does durability guarantee?

- A) Committed changes survive system failures
- B) Transactions are isolated
- C) Constraints are checked
- D) Transactions are atomic

**Answer: A** — Committed changes survive system failures

**DBM-P148.** Which ACID property is ensured by the recovery manager using logs?

- A) Durability and atomicity
- B) Consistency only
- C) Isolation only
- D) None

**Answer: A** — Durability and atomicity

**DBM-P149.** Which ACID property is ensured by concurrency control?

- A) Durability
- B) Isolation
- C) Atomicity
- D) Encryption

**Answer: B** — Isolation

**DBM-P150.** What is a transaction?

- A) A logical unit of work made up of one or more operations
- B) A backup
- C) A table
- D) A single SELECT only

**Answer: A** — A logical unit of work made up of one or more operations

**DBM-P151.** What is a dirty read?

- A) Reading uncommitted data from another transaction
- B) Reading from a backup
- C) Reading with a wrong index
- D) Reading deleted rows

**Answer: A** — Reading uncommitted data from another transaction

**DBM-P152.** What is a non-repeatable read?

- A) Failing to read a row
- B) Reading uncommitted data
- C) Getting different values when reading the same row twice within a transaction
- D) Reading duplicate rows

**Answer: C** — Getting different values when reading the same row twice within a transaction

**DBM-P153.** What is a phantom read?

- A) A crashed read
- B) Reading a deleted row
- C) New rows appear when a query is repeated within a transaction
- D) Reading uncommitted data

**Answer: C** — New rows appear when a query is repeated within a transaction

**DBM-P154.** Which isolation level prevents dirty reads, non-repeatable reads and phantom reads?

- A) Read Committed
- B) Repeatable Read (in the standard definition)
- C) Serializable
- D) Read Uncommitted

**Answer: C** — Serializable

**DBM-P155.** Which isolation level allows dirty reads?

- A) Read Uncommitted
- B) Serializable
- C) Read Committed
- D) Repeatable Read

**Answer: A** — Read Uncommitted

**DBM-P156.** What is a deadlock in a DBMS?

- A) A full disk
- B) A crashed server
- C) Two or more transactions waiting for each other's locks indefinitely
- D) A slow query

**Answer: C** — Two or more transactions waiting for each other's locks indefinitely

**DBM-P157.** What is a shared (read) lock?

- A) Blocks all reads
- B) Allows multiple transactions to read but not write
- C) Allows write by everyone
- D) Allows only one reader

**Answer: B** — Allows multiple transactions to read but not write

**DBM-P158.** What is an exclusive (write) lock?

- A) Many transactions can read
- B) Only one transaction can read/write the item
- C) No transaction can write
- D) It has no effect

**Answer: B** — Only one transaction can read/write the item

**DBM-P159.** What is two-phase locking (2PL)?

- A) Locking twice per query
- B) Locking two tables only
- C) A protocol with a growing phase (acquire locks) then a shrinking phase (release locks)
- D) Using two databases

**Answer: C** — A protocol with a growing phase (acquire locks) then a shrinking phase (release locks)

**DBM-P160.** What is a serialisable schedule?

- A) A schedule with deadlocks
- B) A concurrent schedule equivalent to some serial schedule
- C) A schedule with no transactions
- D) A schedule using one lock

**Answer: B** — A concurrent schedule equivalent to some serial schedule

**DBM-P161.** What is a checkpoint in recovery?

- A) A user session
- B) A schema change
- C) A backup tape
- D) A point where the DBMS flushes changes and logs to reduce recovery work

**Answer: D** — A point where the DBMS flushes changes and logs to reduce recovery work

**DBM-P162.** What is write-ahead logging (WAL)?

- A) Logs are never written
- B) Logs are encrypted only
- C) Data is written before logs
- D) Log records are written to stable storage before data changes are applied

**Answer: D** — Log records are written to stable storage before data changes are applied

**DBM-P163.** What is an index?

- A) A data structure that speeds up data retrieval
- B) A table constraint
- C) A backup
- D) A type of key that must be unique

**Answer: A** — A data structure that speeds up data retrieval

**DBM-P164.** Which data structure is most commonly used for database indexes?

- A) Stack
- B) B-tree / B+ tree
- C) Linked list
- D) Queue

**Answer: B** — B-tree / B+ tree

**DBM-P165.** What is a clustered index?

- A) A separate structure with pointers only
- B) The table rows are physically ordered by the index key
- C) An index on multiple tables
- D) An index on a view

**Answer: B** — The table rows are physically ordered by the index key

**DBM-P166.** How many clustered indexes can a table have?

- A) Two
- B) Unlimited
- C) One per column
- D) One

**Answer: D** — One

**DBM-P167.** What is a non-clustered (secondary) index?

- A) A primary key
- B) A separate structure with pointers to the rows
- C) The physical order of rows
- D) A hash of the table

**Answer: B** — A separate structure with pointers to the rows

**DBM-P168.** What is hashing in databases used for?

- A) Sorting records
- B) Direct access to records using a hash function
- C) Encrypting records
- D) Compressing records

**Answer: B** — Direct access to records using a hash function

**DBM-P169.** What is a B+ tree leaf level typically used for?

- A) Storing all key values (and pointers to data) in linked order
- B) Storing only the root
- C) Storing metadata
- D) Storing deleted keys

**Answer: A** — Storing all key values (and pointers to data) in linked order

**DBM-P170.** What is a view?

- A) A trigger
- B) A virtual table defined by a query
- C) A physical copy of a table
- D) An index

**Answer: B** — A virtual table defined by a query

**DBM-P171.** What is a stored procedure?

- A) Precompiled SQL code stored in the database
- B) An index type
- C) A table constraint
- D) A backup file

**Answer: A** — Precompiled SQL code stored in the database

**DBM-P172.** What is a trigger?

- A) A manually run script
- B) A view
- C) A backup job
- D) Code that runs automatically on INSERT, UPDATE or DELETE events

**Answer: D** — Code that runs automatically on INSERT, UPDATE or DELETE events

**DBM-P173.** What is a data warehouse?

- A) A repository of integrated historical data for analysis
- B) A backup tape library
- C) A transactional database for daily operations
- D) A cache

**Answer: A** — A repository of integrated historical data for analysis

**DBM-P174.** What is OLTP?

- A) Offline Transaction Processing
- B) Online Table Processing
- C) Online Transaction Processing for day-to-day operations
- D) Optimised Logical Table Processing

**Answer: C** — Online Transaction Processing for day-to-day operations

**DBM-P175.** What is OLAP?

- A) Ordered Logical Access Protocol
- B) Offline Analytical Programming
- C) Online Application Processing
- D) Online Analytical Processing for complex analysis

**Answer: D** — Online Analytical Processing for complex analysis

**DBM-P176.** What is a NoSQL database?

- A) A database that only uses SQL
- B) A non-relational database, often flexible in schema and scalable
- C) A database that cannot be queried
- D) A database without data

**Answer: B** — A non-relational database, often flexible in schema and scalable

**DBM-P177.** Which is a document-oriented NoSQL database?

- A) PostgreSQL
- B) MySQL
- C) Oracle
- D) MongoDB

**Answer: D** — MongoDB

**DBM-P178.** Which is a key-value NoSQL store?

- A) SQLite
- B) Redis
- C) Microsoft Access
- D) MySQL

**Answer: B** — Redis

**DBM-P179.** Which is a column-family NoSQL database?

- A) Cassandra
- B) Neo4j
- C) Redis
- D) MongoDB

**Answer: A** — Cassandra

**DBM-P180.** Which is a graph database?

- A) Neo4j
- B) Cassandra
- C) MySQL
- D) Redis

**Answer: A** — Neo4j

**DBM-P181.** What does the CAP theorem state?

- A) A distributed system can guarantee at most two of Consistency, Availability, Partition tolerance
- B) Only consistency matters
- C) All three can always be guaranteed
- D) Only availability matters

**Answer: A** — A distributed system can guarantee at most two of Consistency, Availability, Partition tolerance

**DBM-P182.** What does BASE stand for in NoSQL?

- A) Basically Available, Soft state, Eventual consistency
- B) Binary Atomic Stable Eventual
- C) Basic Atomic Secure Encrypted
- D) Backup, Archive, Snapshot, Export

**Answer: A** — Basically Available, Soft state, Eventual consistency

**DBM-P183.** What is sharding?

- A) Indexing all columns
- B) Encrypting data
- C) Horizontally partitioning data across multiple servers
- D) Compressing tables

**Answer: C** — Horizontally partitioning data across multiple servers

**DBM-P184.** What is replication?

- A) Splitting tables
- B) Copying data across servers for availability and read scaling
- C) Deleting old data
- D) Encrypting a table

**Answer: B** — Copying data across servers for availability and read scaling

**DBM-P185.** What is a data dictionary (system catalog)?

- A) Metadata about database objects
- B) A backup index
- C) A list of SQL keywords
- D) A table of user passwords

**Answer: A** — Metadata about database objects

**DBM-P186.** Which language is used to define database structures?

- A) TCL
- B) DDL
- C) DML
- D) DCL

**Answer: B** — DDL

**DBM-P187.** Which language is used to manipulate data (insert, update, delete)?

- A) DML
- B) DCL
- C) DDL
- D) TCL

**Answer: A** — DML

**DBM-P188.** What is a cursor?

- A) A pointer for traversing query result rows one at a time
- B) An index
- C) A lock
- D) A mouse pointer

**Answer: A** — A pointer for traversing query result rows one at a time

**DBM-P189.** What is an ORM?

- A) Optimised Relational Model
- B) Object Reference Map
- C) Online Resource Manager
- D) Object-Relational Mapping between objects and relational tables

**Answer: D** — Object-Relational Mapping between objects and relational tables

**DBM-P190.** What is an object-relational impedance mismatch?

- A) A wrong data type
- B) Difficulty mapping object models to relational tables
- C) A missing index
- D) A slow network

**Answer: B** — Difficulty mapping object models to relational tables

**DBM-P191.** What is the difference between DELETE and TRUNCATE regarding transactions (in most systems)?

- A) DELETE is fully logged row by row; TRUNCATE is minimally logged and removes all rows
- B) They are the same
- C) DELETE removes the table
- D) TRUNCATE can use WHERE; DELETE cannot

**Answer: A** — DELETE is fully logged row by row; TRUNCATE is minimally logged and removes all rows

**DBM-P192.** What is an insertion anomaly?

- A) Being unable to add data because other unrelated data is missing
- B) Adding duplicate rows
- C) Inserting too many rows
- D) Inserting NULL into a key

**Answer: A** — Being unable to add data because other unrelated data is missing

**DBM-P193.** What is a deletion anomaly?

- A) Being unable to delete a table
- B) Deleting a duplicate row
- C) Deleting a row unintentionally removes other needed information
- D) Deleting an index

**Answer: C** — Deleting a row unintentionally removes other needed information

**DBM-P194.** What is an update anomaly?

- A) Inconsistent data after changing a value stored in multiple places
- B) Updating a view
- C) An update that is too slow
- D) Being unable to update a key

**Answer: A** — Inconsistent data after changing a value stored in multiple places

**DBM-P195.** What is a lossless-join decomposition?

- A) A decomposition using one table
- B) Joining the decomposed relations gives back exactly the original relation
- C) A decomposition that removes all keys
- D) A decomposition that deletes rows

**Answer: B** — Joining the decomposed relations gives back exactly the original relation

**DBM-P196.** What is dependency preservation?

- A) Foreign keys are dropped
- B) No tables are decomposed
- C) All original functional dependencies can be enforced on the decomposed relations
- D) Primary keys never change

**Answer: C** — All original functional dependencies can be enforced on the decomposed relations

**DBM-P197.** What is generalisation in the ER model?

- A) Splitting an entity into sub-entities
- B) Adding relationships
- C) Removing attributes
- D) Combining entities with common features into a higher-level entity

**Answer: D** — Combining entities with common features into a higher-level entity

**DBM-P198.** What is specialisation in the ER model?

- A) Creating weak entities only
- B) Combining entities into one
- C) Defining sub-entities from a higher-level entity based on distinguishing features
- D) Deleting entities

**Answer: C** — Defining sub-entities from a higher-level entity based on distinguishing features

**DBM-P199.** What is a materialised view?

- A) A view with no query
- B) A trigger
- C) A temporary table
- D) A view whose query result is stored physically

**Answer: D** — A view whose query result is stored physically

**DBM-P200.** Can every view be updated through INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE?

- A) Never
- B) Yes always
- C) Only materialised views
- D) No, only simple updatable views generally

**Answer: D** — No, only simple updatable views generally

**DBM-P201.** What is MVCC?

- A) A type of index
- B) A backup technique
- C) A join algorithm
- D) Multi-Version Concurrency Control keeps multiple versions of data to allow readers not to block writers

**Answer: D** — Multi-Version Concurrency Control keeps multiple versions of data to allow readers not to block writers

**DBM-P202.** What is timestamp-based concurrency control?

- A) Scheduling backups
- B) Ordering transactions by their timestamps to ensure serialisability
- C) Locking with time limits
- D) Deleting old data

**Answer: B** — Ordering transactions by their timestamps to ensure serialisability

**DBM-P203.** What is the two-phase commit (2PC) protocol used for?

- A) Committing twice on one server
- B) Coordinating a commit across multiple databases or nodes
- C) Backing up data
- D) Encrypting commits

**Answer: B** — Coordinating a commit across multiple databases or nodes

**DBM-P204.** What is a cascading rollback?

- A) Aborting one transaction forces others that read its data to abort
- B) Rolling back all tables
- C) Rolling back a backup
- D) A rollback that fails

**Answer: A** — Aborting one transaction forces others that read its data to abort

**DBM-P205.** What is a recoverable schedule?

- A) A schedule without locks
- B) A schedule that uses backups
- C) A transaction commits only after all transactions it read from have committed
- D) A schedule with no writes

**Answer: C** — A transaction commits only after all transactions it read from have committed

**DBM-P206.** What is the heap (unordered) file organisation?

- A) Records are sorted by key
- B) Records are stored in an index only
- C) Records are placed wherever there is space, in no particular order
- D) Records are hashed

**Answer: C** — Records are placed wherever there is space, in no particular order

**DBM-P207.** What is static hashing?

- A) A fixed number of buckets determined in advance
- B) Buckets grow and shrink dynamically
- C) Hashing on disk only
- D) Hashing that uses no function

**Answer: A** — A fixed number of buckets determined in advance

**DBM-P208.** What is the purpose of a query optimiser?

- A) Encrypt queries
- B) Compress tables
- C) Choose an efficient execution plan for a query
- D) Check spelling of SQL

**Answer: C** — Choose an efficient execution plan for a query

**DBM-P209.** What does the EXPLAIN command show in most databases?

- A) The backup status
- B) The execution plan of a query
- C) The schema of a table
- D) The list of users

**Answer: B** — The execution plan of a query

**DBM-P210.** What is a buffer pool (buffer manager) in a DBMS?

- A) A network buffer only
- B) An index
- C) Memory that caches disk pages
- D) A backup queue

**Answer: C** — Memory that caches disk pages

**DBM-P211.** What is the purpose of the transaction log?

- A) Caching queries
- B) Storing user passwords
- C) Listing indexes
- D) Recording changes so transactions can be recovered

**Answer: D** — Recording changes so transactions can be recovered

**DBM-P212.** How does the relational model treat NULL?

- A) As an empty string
- B) As a duplicate value
- C) As an unknown or missing value, not equal to any value including another NULL
- D) As zero

**Answer: C** — As an unknown or missing value, not equal to any value including another NULL

**DBM-P213.** What does a UNIQUE constraint do to NULLs in most databases?

- A) Converts NULL to 0
- B) Allows no NULLs at all
- C) Allows multiple NULLs (SQL standard)
- D) Allows exactly one NULL

**Answer: C** — Allows multiple NULLs (SQL standard)

**DBM-P214.** What is a join dependency?

- A) A foreign key constraint
- B) Two tables share a key
- C) A relation can be reconstructed by joining its projections
- D) A view dependency

**Answer: C** — A relation can be reconstructed by joining its projections

**DBM-P215.** What is 4NF concerned with?

- A) Eliminating transitive dependencies
- B) Atomic values
- C) Eliminating partial dependencies
- D) Eliminating non-trivial multivalued dependencies not implied by keys

**Answer: D** — Eliminating non-trivial multivalued dependencies not implied by keys

**DBM-P216.** What is data mining?

- A) Deleting old data
- B) Discovering patterns in large data sets
- C) Encrypting data
- D) Designing schemas

**Answer: B** — Discovering patterns in large data sets

**DBM-P217.** What is ETL in data warehousing?

- A) Execute, Test, Launch
- B) Extract, Transform, Load
- C) Encrypt, Transfer, Log
- D) Export, Translate, Link

**Answer: B** — Extract, Transform, Load

**DBM-P218.** What is a star schema?

- A) A hierarchical schema
- B) A graph of only dimension tables
- C) A central fact table connected to dimension tables
- D) A single flat table

**Answer: C** — A central fact table connected to dimension tables

**DBM-P219.** What is a fact table?

- A) A table storing measurable events such as sales with foreign keys to dimensions
- B) An audit log
- C) A table of descriptions only
- D) A lookup table

**Answer: A** — A table storing measurable events such as sales with foreign keys to dimensions

**DBM-P220.** Which SQL isolation level is the default in MySQL InnoDB?

- A) Repeatable Read
- B) Serializable
- C) Read Committed
- D) Read Uncommitted

**Answer: A** — Repeatable Read

**DBM-P221.** Which SQL isolation level is the default in PostgreSQL and Oracle?

- A) Repeatable Read
- B) Read Committed
- C) Serializable
- D) Read Uncommitted

**Answer: B** — Read Committed

**DBM-P222.** What is the purpose of a savepoint in a transaction?

- A) To roll back part of a transaction
- B) To create an index
- C) To back up the database
- D) To end a session

**Answer: A** — To roll back part of a transaction

**DBM-P223.** A relation R(A, B, C, D) has functional dependencies {A -> B, B -> C, C -> D}. What is the attribute closure of {A}?

- A) {A}
- B) {A, B}
- C) {A, B, C}
- D) {A, B, C, D}

**Answer: D** — {A, B, C, D}
> ℹ️ Starting with {A}, A->B adds B, B->C adds C, and C->D adds D. Thus, {A}+ = {A, B, C, D}.

**DBM-P224.** Which SQL transaction isolation level prevents Dirty Reads and Non-Repeatable Reads, but allows Phantom Reads?

- A) Read Uncommitted
- B) Read Committed
- C) Repeatable Read
- D) Serializable

**Answer: C** — Repeatable Read
> ℹ️ Repeatable Read prevents reading uncommitted data and ensures re-reading rows returns identical data, but new rows inserted by other transactions (phantoms) can still appear.

**DBM-P225.** In database indexing, what is the core structural difference between a Clustered Index and a Non-Clustered Index?

- A) A table can have multiple clustered indexes, but only one non-clustered index.
- B) A clustered index dictates the physical sorting of table rows on disk, while a non-clustered index stores a separate pointer structure.
- C) Non-clustered indexes reside in RAM, while clustered indexes reside on disk.
- D) Clustered indexes can only index alphanumeric string columns.

**Answer: B** — A clustered index dictates the physical sorting of table rows on disk, while a non-clustered index stores a separate pointer structure.
> ℹ️ Because physical table rows can only be ordered in one way, each database table can have at most one clustered index.

**DBM-P226.** In database trigger definitions, what timing specification executes custom procedural code before an INSERT, UPDATE, or DELETE operation alters table data?

- A) `AFTER`
- B) `INSTEAD OF`
- C) `BEFORE`
- D) `WHILE`

**Answer: C** — BEFORE
> ℹ️ `BEFORE` triggers execute prior to table modification, allowing data validation or column value transformation before write completion.

**DBM-P227.** A database schema is in Third Normal Form (3NF) if for every non-trivial functional dependency X -> Y, which condition is satisfied?

- A) X is a superkey OR Y is a prime attribute.
- B) X is a superkey AND Y is a superkey.
- C) Y is a non-prime attribute.
- D) X contains a single attribute only.

**Answer: A** — X is a superkey OR Y is a prime attribute.
> ℹ️ 3NF eliminates transitive functional dependencies by ensuring left-side X is a superkey or right-side Y is part of a candidate key.

### 8.2 Practice questions (211)

**SQL-P001.** What is the output of the following SQL?

```
SELECT COUNT(*) FROM employees;
```

- A) 3
- B) 9
- C) 10
- D) 11

**Answer: C** — 10
> ℹ️ COUNT(*) counts all rows; COUNT(column) ignores NULLs.

**SQL-P002.** What is the output of the following SQL?

```
SELECT COUNT(dept_id) FROM employees;
```

- A) 3
- B) 8
- C) 9
- D) 10

**Answer: C** — 9

**SQL-P003.** What is the output of the following SQL?

```
SELECT COUNT(manager_id) FROM employees;
```

- A) 4
- B) 8
- C) 9
- D) 10

**Answer: C** — 9

**SQL-P004.** What is the output of the following SQL?

```
SELECT COUNT(DISTINCT dept_id) FROM employees;
```

- A) 3
- B) 4
- C) 9
- D) 10

**Answer: A** — 3

**SQL-P005.** What is the output of the following SQL?

```
SELECT COUNT(DISTINCT city) FROM employees;
```

- A) 3
- B) 4
- C) 8
- D) 10

**Answer: A** — 3

**SQL-P006.** What is the output of the following SQL?

```
SELECT COUNT(DISTINCT salary) FROM employees;
```

- A) 3
- B) 8

- D) Arjun, Asha, Neha, Ravi, Sita
