**SQL-P081.** What is the output of the following SQL?

```
SELECT name FROM employees WHERE dept_id = (SELECT dept_id FROM departments WHERE dept_name = 'Sales') ORDER BY name;
```

- A) Karan, Pooja, Sita
- B) Arjun, Asha, Neha, Ravi
- C) Arjun, Asha, Meena, Neha, Ravi, Vikram
- D) Meena, Vikram

**Answer: A** — Karan, Pooja, Sita

**SQL-P082.** What is the output of the following SQL?

```
SELECT SUM(CASE WHEN salary >= 50000 THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) FROM employees;
```

- A) 2
- B) 3
- C) 5
- D) 6

**Answer: C** — 5

**SQL-P083.** What is the output of the following SQL?

```
SELECT name, CASE WHEN salary >= 55000 THEN 'High' WHEN salary >= 40000 THEN 'Mid' ELSE 'Low' END FROM employees WHERE emp_id IN (103, 106, 105) ORDER BY emp_id;
```

- A) (Meena, High); (Sita, High); (Tarun, Low)
- B) (Meena, Mid); (Sita, Mid); (Tarun, Low)
- C) (Meena, Mid); (Sita, High); (Tarun
- D) (Meena, Mid); (Sita, High); (Tarun, Low)

**Answer: D** — (Meena, Mid); (Sita, High); (Tarun, Low)
> ℹ️ CASE returns the first matching WHEN branch.

**SQL-P084.** What is the output of the following SQL?

```
SELECT UPPER(SUBSTR(name, 1, 3)) FROM employees WHERE emp_id = 104;
```

- A) Kar
- B) ka
- C) ARA
- D) KAR

**Answer: D** — KAR

**SQL-P085.** What is the output of the following SQL?

```
SELECT LENGTH(name) FROM employees WHERE emp_id = 108;
```

- A) 5
- B) 6
- C) 7
- D) 8

**Answer: B** — 6

**SQL-P086.** What is the output of the following SQL?

```
SELECT name || '-' || city FROM employees WHERE emp_id = 105;
```

- A) SitaDelhi

- D) Sita-Delhi

**Answer: D** — Sita-Delhi
> ℹ️ || is the standard SQL string concatenation operator.

**SQL-P087.** What is the output of the following SQL?

```
SELECT COALESCE(dept_id, 0) FROM employees WHERE emp_id = 106;
```

- A) 1
- B) 103
- C) NULL
- D) 0

**Answer: D** — 0
> ℹ️ COALESCE returns its first non-NULL argument.

**SQL-P088.** What is the output of the following SQL?

```
SELECT COALESCE(manager_id, -1) FROM employees WHERE emp_id = 101;
```

- A) -1
- B) 2
- C) NULL
- D) 101

**Answer: A** — -1

**SQL-P089.** What is the output of the following SQL?

```

```

- B) 50101
- C) 110000
- D) 110101

**Answer: D** — 110101
> ℹ️ salary + NULL is NULL, and SUM ignores NULLs.

**SQL-P090.** What is the output of the following SQL?

```

```

- D) 135

**Answer: B** — 45

**SQL-P091.** What is the output of the following SQL?

```

```

- A) 17500

- C) 70000
- D) 100000

**Answer: B** — 40000

**SQL-P092.** What is the output of the following SQL?

```
SELECT r FROM (SELECT name, RANK() OVER (ORDER BY salary DESC) AS r FROM employees) WHERE name = 'Meena';
```

- A) 5
- B) 6
- C) 7
- D) 8

**Answer: B** — 6
> ℹ️ RANK leaves gaps after ties; DENSE_RANK does not.

**SQL-P093.** What is the output of the following SQL?

```
SELECT r FROM (SELECT name, RANK() OVER (ORDER BY salary DESC) AS r FROM employees) WHERE name = 'Ravi';
```

- A) 3
- B) 4
- C) 5
- D) 6

**Answer: B** — 4
> ℹ️ RANK leaves gaps after ties; DENSE_RANK does not.

**SQL-P094.** What is the output of the following SQL?

```
SELECT r FROM (SELECT name, RANK() OVER (ORDER BY salary DESC) AS r FROM employees) WHERE name = 'Sita';
```

- A) 2
- B) 3
- C) 4
- D) 8

**Answer: B** — 3
> ℹ️ RANK leaves gaps after ties; DENSE_RANK does not.

**SQL-P095.** What is the output of the following SQL?

```
SELECT r FROM (SELECT name, DENSE_RANK() OVER (ORDER BY salary DESC) AS r FROM employees) WHERE name = 'Meena';
```

- B) 5
- C) 6
- D) 7

**Answer: B** — 5

**SQL-P096.** What is the output of the following SQL?

```
SELECT r FROM (SELECT name, DENSE_RANK() OVER (ORDER BY salary DESC) AS r FROM employees) WHERE name = 'Neha';
```

- A) 3
- B) 4
- C) 5
- D) 6

**Answer: B** — 4

**SQL-P097.** What is the output of the following SQL?

```
SELECT name, SUM(salary) OVER (PARTITION BY dept_id) FROM employees WHERE emp_id IN (103, 108) ORDER BY emp_id;
```

- A) 0

**SQL-P102.** What is the output of the following SQL?

**SQL-P103.** Which SQL statement is a DDL (Data Definition Language) command?

- A) UPDATE
- B) SELECT
- C) CREATE TABLE
- D) INSERT

**Answer: C** — CREATE TABLE

**SQL-P104.** Which of the following is a DML command?

- A) TRUNCATE
- B) UPDATE
- C) DROP
- D) ALTER

**Answer: B** — UPDATE

**SQL-P105.** Which of the following is a DCL command?

- A) CREATE
- B) GRANT
- C) COMMIT
- D) INSERT

**Answer: B** — GRANT

**SQL-P106.** Which of the following is a TCL (Transaction Control Language) command?

- A) DELETE
- B) REVOKE
- C) ALTER
- D) ROLLBACK

**Answer: D** — ROLLBACK

**SQL-P107.** Which command removes all rows from a table quickly without logging individual row deletions and cannot use a WHERE clause?

- A) DROP
- B) REMOVE
- C) TRUNCATE
- D) DELETE

**Answer: C** — TRUNCATE

**SQL-P108.** Which command removes the table structure along with all its data?

- A) DELETE FROM table
- B) TRUNCATE TABLE
- C) ALTER TABLE
- D) DROP TABLE

**Answer: D** — DROP TABLE

**SQL-P109.** Which statement is used to add a new column to an existing table?

- A) CREATE COLUMN
- B) UPDATE TABLE ... ADD
- C) MODIFY TABLE ... INSERT
- D) ALTER TABLE ... ADD

**Answer: D** — ALTER TABLE ... ADD

**SQL-P110.** Which clause is used to filter groups after a GROUP BY?

- A) LIMIT
- B) WHERE
- C) ORDER BY
- D) HAVING

**Answer: D** — HAVING

**SQL-P111.** Which clause filters individual rows before grouping?

- A) DISTINCT
- B) GROUP BY
- C) HAVING
- D) WHERE

**Answer: D** — WHERE

**SQL-P112.** What is the logical order of execution of a SELECT query's clauses?

- A) FROM, WHERE, GROUP BY, HAVING, SELECT, ORDER BY
- B) FROM, SELECT, WHERE, GROUP BY, ORDER BY, HAVING
- C) WHERE, FROM, HAVING, GROUP BY, SELECT, ORDER BY
- D) SELECT, FROM, WHERE, GROUP BY, HAVING, ORDER BY

**Answer: A** — FROM, WHERE, GROUP BY, HAVING, SELECT, ORDER BY

**SQL-P113.** Which keyword eliminates duplicate rows from a result set?

- A) SINGLE
- B) ONLY
- C) UNIQUE
- D) DISTINCT

**Answer: D** — DISTINCT

**SQL-P114.** Which operator is used to match a pattern in a string?

- A) LIKE
- B) BETWEEN
- C) MATCH
- D) IN

**Answer: A** — LIKE

**SQL-P115.** In a LIKE pattern, what does the underscore (_) wildcard represent?

- A) Any digit
- B) A literal underscore
- C) Exactly one character
- D) Zero or more characters

**Answer: C** — Exactly one character

**SQL-P116.** In a LIKE pattern, what does the percent (%) wildcard represent?

- A) Exactly one character
- B) A literal percent sign
- C) Zero or more characters
- D) Exactly one digit

**Answer: C** — Zero or more characters

**SQL-P117.** Which SQL keyword sorts the result set?

- A) ARRANGE
- B) ORDER BY
- C) SORT BY
- D) GROUP BY

**Answer: B** — ORDER BY

**SQL-P118.** What is the default sort order of ORDER BY?

- A) Ascending
- B) Random
- C) Insertion order
- D) Descending

**Answer: A** — Ascending

**SQL-P119.** Which constraint ensures that a column cannot contain NULL values?

- A) NOT NULL
- B) DEFAULT
- C) UNIQUE
- D) CHECK

**Answer: A** — NOT NULL

**SQL-P120.** Which constraint ensures all values in a column are different (but allows NULL in most databases)?

- A) DEFAULT
- B) PRIMARY KEY
- C) CHECK
- D) UNIQUE

**Answer: D** — UNIQUE

**SQL-P121.** How many primary keys can a table have?

- A) One (which may be composite)
- B) None
- C) As many as there are columns
- D) Two

**Answer: A** — One (which may be composite)

**SQL-P122.** Which constraint enforces referential integrity between two tables?

- A) FOREIGN KEY
- B) UNIQUE
- C) DEFAULT
- D) CHECK

**Answer: A** — FOREIGN KEY

**SQL-P123.** Which constraint limits the values that can be placed in a column using a condition such as salary > 0?

- A) UNIQUE
- B) INDEX
- C) CHECK
- D) DEFAULT

**Answer: C** — CHECK

**SQL-P124.** Which constraint supplies a value automatically when none is specified during INSERT?

- A) FOREIGN KEY
- B) CHECK
- C) UNIQUE
- D) DEFAULT

**Answer: D** — DEFAULT

**SQL-P125.** Can a foreign key column contain NULL values?

- A) Only if it is also a primary key
- B) Yes, unless it is declared NOT NULL
- C) Only in the parent table
- D) No, never

**Answer: B** — Yes, unless it is declared NOT NULL

**SQL-P126.** Which join returns only rows that have matching values in both tables?

- A) INNER JOIN
- B) CROSS JOIN
- C) FULL OUTER JOIN
- D) LEFT JOIN

**Answer: A** — INNER JOIN

**SQL-P127.** Which join returns all rows from the left table and matching rows from the right table (NULL where no match)?

- A) RIGHT JOIN
- B) CROSS JOIN
- C) LEFT JOIN
- D) INNER JOIN

**Answer: C** — LEFT JOIN

**SQL-P128.** Which join produces the Cartesian product of two tables?

- A) INNER JOIN
- B) SELF JOIN
- C) CROSS JOIN
- D) NATURAL JOIN

**Answer: C** — CROSS JOIN

**SQL-P129.** If table A has 5 rows and table B has 4 rows, how many rows does A CROSS JOIN B return?

- A) 4
- B) 5
- C) 9
- D) 20

**Answer: D** — 20

**SQL-P130.** Which type of join joins a table to itself?

- A) Outer join
- B) Natural join
- C) Cross join
- D) Self join

**Answer: D** — Self join

**SQL-P131.** Which aggregate function ignores NULL values when applied to a column?

- A) All of SUM, AVG, MIN, MAX, COUNT(column)
- B) Only COUNT(*)
- C) Only SUM
- D) None of them

**Answer: A** — All of SUM, AVG, MIN, MAX, COUNT(column)

**SQL-P132.** What does COUNT(*) count?

- A) Only rows with numeric values
- B) Only non-NULL values in the first column
- C) Only distinct rows
- D) All rows, including those with NULLs

**Answer: D** — All rows, including those with NULLs

**SQL-P133.** What is the difference between UNION and UNION ALL?

- A) UNION works on one table only
- B) UNION removes duplicates, UNION ALL keeps them
- C) There is no difference
- D) UNION ALL removes duplicates, UNION keeps them

**Answer: B** — UNION removes duplicates, UNION ALL keeps them

**SQL-P134.** Which set operator returns rows present in both queries?

- A) EXCEPT
- B) UNION
- C) INTERSECT
- D) MERGE

**Answer: C** — INTERSECT

**SQL-P135.** Which set operator returns rows from the first query that are not in the second?

- A) JOIN
- B) EXCEPT (MINUS in Oracle)
- C) UNION
- D) INTERSECT

**Answer: B** — EXCEPT (MINUS in Oracle)

**SQL-P136.** What is a subquery?

- A) A query with no FROM clause
- B) A query that runs after a COMMIT
- C) A query stored as a table
- D) A query nested inside another query

**Answer: D** — A query nested inside another query

**SQL-P137.** What is a correlated subquery?

- A) A subquery with no WHERE clause
- B) A subquery that returns no rows
- C) A subquery that references columns of the outer query
- D) A subquery that runs only once

**Answer: C** — A subquery that references columns of the outer query

**SQL-P138.** Which operator returns TRUE if a subquery returns at least one row?

- A) ANY
- B) ALL
- C) EXISTS
- D) IN

**Answer: C** — EXISTS

**SQL-P139.** What is the result of comparing NULL = NULL in SQL?

- A) TRUE
- B) UNKNOWN (not TRUE)
- C) FALSE
- D) 0

**Answer: B** — UNKNOWN (not TRUE)

**SQL-P140.** Which expression correctly tests for NULL?

- A) column == NULL
- B) column = NULL
- C) column EQUALS NULL
- D) column IS NULL

**Answer: D** — column IS NULL

**SQL-P141.** Which function returns the first non-NULL value from its arguments?

- A) COALESCE
- B) NVL2
- C) ISNULL
- D) NULLIF

**Answer: A** — COALESCE

**SQL-P142.** What does NULLIF(a, b) return?

- A) b if a equals b
- B) TRUE if a equals b
- C) a if a is NULL, otherwise b
- D) NULL if a equals b, otherwise a

**Answer: D** — NULL if a equals b, otherwise a

**SQL-P143.** Which SQL function returns the number of characters in a string?

- A) COUNT
- B) CHARS
- C) LENGTH (LEN in SQL Server)
- D) SIZE

**Answer: C** — LENGTH (LEN in SQL Server)

**SQL-P144.** Which function extracts part of a string?

- A) SLICE
- B) CUT
- C) SUBSTRING (SUBSTR)
- D) EXTRACT

**Answer: C** — SUBSTRING (SUBSTR)

**SQL-P145.** Which function converts a string to upper case?

- A) UCASE_ALL
- B) CAPITAL
- C) TOUP
- D) UPPER

**Answer: D** — UPPER

**SQL-P146.** What does the ROUND(123.456, 1) function return?

- A) 123
- B) 123.4
- C) 123.46
- D) 123.5

**Answer: D** — 123.5

**SQL-P147.** What does the ROUND(2.5) return in most SQL databases (round half away from zero)?

- A) 2
- B) 2.5
- C) 3
- D) 4

**Answer: C** — 3

**SQL-P148.** Which statement creates an index on the column name of table emp?

- A) ALTER INDEX idx_name ON emp(name);
- B) INDEX emp(name);
- C) CREATE INDEX idx_name ON emp(name);
- D) ADD INDEX TO emp.name;

**Answer: C** — CREATE INDEX idx_name ON emp(name);

**SQL-P149.** What is the main purpose of an index?

- A) To encrypt data
- B) To speed up data retrieval
- C) To enforce a foreign key
- D) To store backup copies

**Answer: B** — To speed up data retrieval

**SQL-P150.** What is a drawback of having too many indexes on a table?

- A) Slower INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE and more storage
- B) Slower SELECT queries always
- C) They block all writes
- D) They remove constraints

**Answer: A** — Slower INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE and more storage

**SQL-P151.** Which SQL object is a virtual table based on the result of a stored query?

- A) Index
- B) View
- C) Trigger
- D) Sequence

**Answer: B** — View

**SQL-P152.** Which statement creates a view?

- A) NEW VIEW v AS ...;
- B) CREATE TABLE VIEW v;
- C) MAKE VIEW v FROM ...;
- D) CREATE VIEW v AS SELECT ...;

**Answer: D** — CREATE VIEW v AS SELECT ...;

**SQL-P153.** Which database object automatically executes in response to INSERT, UPDATE or DELETE events?

- A) Trigger
- B) Index
- C) Cursor
- D) View

**Answer: A** — Trigger

**SQL-P154.** Which database object can accept parameters, contain logic and be called with EXEC/CALL?

- A) Index
- B) View
- C) Stored procedure
- D) Constraint

**Answer: C** — Stored procedure

**SQL-P155.** What is a cursor used for in SQL?

- A) Creating tables
- B) Enforcing uniqueness
- C) Speeding up joins
- D) Processing a result set one row at a time

**Answer: D** — Processing a result set one row at a time

**SQL-P156.** Which command saves all changes made in the current transaction permanently?

- A) FLUSH
- B) SAVEPOINT
- C) ROLLBACK
- D) COMMIT

**Answer: D** — COMMIT

**SQL-P157.** Which command undoes changes made in the current transaction?

- A) REVOKE
- B) COMMIT
- C) TRUNCATE
- D) ROLLBACK

**Answer: D** — ROLLBACK

**SQL-P158.** Which command sets a point within a transaction to which you can later roll back?

- A) CHECKPOINT
- B) MARKER
- C) SAVEPOINT
- D) BOOKMARK

**Answer: C** — SAVEPOINT

**SQL-P159.** Which SQL clause limits the number of rows returned in MySQL/PostgreSQL/SQLite?

- A) ROWNUM
- B) FETCHONLY
- C) LIMIT
- D) TOP

**Answer: C** — LIMIT

**SQL-P160.** Which keyword limits rows returned in SQL Server?

- A) ROWNUM
- B) TOP
- C) FIRST
- D) LIMIT

**Answer: B** — TOP

**SQL-P161.** What does the ALL keyword do in "salary > ALL (subquery)"?

- A) The condition must hold for at least one value
- B) It removes duplicates
- C) The condition must hold against every value returned by the subquery
- D) It returns all columns

**Answer: C** — The condition must hold against every value returned by the subquery

**SQL-P162.** What does the ANY keyword do in "salary > ANY (subquery)"?

- A) It ignores the subquery
- B) The condition must hold for at least one value returned
- C) The condition must hold for every value
- D) It returns any random row

**Answer: B** — The condition must hold for at least one value returned

**SQL-P163.** What does a NATURAL JOIN join on?

- A) The primary keys only
- B) The first column of each table
- C) No columns (Cartesian product)
- D) All columns with the same name in both tables

**Answer: D** — All columns with the same name in both tables

**SQL-P164.** Which statement is used to change existing data in a table?

- A) UPDATE
- B) CHANGE
- C) ALTER
- D) MODIFY

**Answer: A** — UPDATE

**SQL-P165.** What happens if UPDATE is executed without a WHERE clause?

- A) All rows are updated
- B) No rows are updated
- C) Only the first row is updated
- D) A syntax error occurs

**Answer: A** — All rows are updated

**SQL-P166.** What happens if DELETE FROM emp; is executed without a WHERE clause?

- A) All rows are deleted but the table structure remains
- B) Only the last row is deleted
- C) A syntax error occurs
- D) The table is dropped

**Answer: A** — All rows are deleted but the table structure remains

**SQL-P167.** Which statement correctly inserts a row into emp(id, name)?

- A) INSERT VALUES (1, 'A') INTO emp;
- B) ADD INTO emp (1, 'A');
- C) INSERT INTO emp(id, name) VALUES (1, 'A');
- D) INSERT emp VALUES id=1, name='A';

**Answer: C** — INSERT INTO emp(id, name) VALUES (1, 'A');

**SQL-P168.** Which clause specifies the table(s) from which to retrieve data?

- A) TABLE
- B) FROM
- C) SOURCE
- D) WHERE

**Answer: B** — FROM

**SQL-P169.** Which SQL keyword is used to rename a column or table in a query result temporarily?

- A) RENAME
- B) AS
- C) TO
- D) ALIAS

**Answer: B** — AS

**SQL-P170.** Which operator tests whether a value lies within an inclusive range?

- A) WITHIN
- B) RANGE
- C) BETWEEN
- D) SPAN

**Answer: C** — BETWEEN

**SQL-P171.** Which operator checks a value against a list of values?

- A) IN
- B) ANY LIST
- C) CONTAINS
- D) MATCHES

**Answer: A** — IN

**SQL-P172.** Which of the following returns the current date in standard SQL?

- A) TODAY()
- B) CURRENT_DATE
- C) DATE_NOW
- D) GETDAYS

**Answer: B** — CURRENT_DATE

**SQL-P173.** What is the result type of the AVG() function on an INTEGER column in most databases?

- A) Always an integer
- B) A numeric value that may have a decimal part
- C) Boolean
- D) Always a string

**Answer: B** — A numeric value that may have a decimal part

**SQL-P174.** Which function returns the number of distinct non-NULL values?

- A) COUNT UNIQUE(column)
- B) COUNT(DISTINCT column)
- C) UNIQUE(column)
- D) DISTINCT COUNT(column)

**Answer: B** — COUNT(DISTINCT column)

**SQL-P175.** Which statement is TRUE about the PRIMARY KEY constraint?

- A) It can contain duplicates
- B) A table can have several primary keys
- C) It uniquely identifies each row and cannot be NULL
- D) It may be NULL in one row only

**Answer: C** — It uniquely identifies each row and cannot be NULL

**SQL-P176.** What is the purpose of the AUTO_INCREMENT/IDENTITY property?

- A) Automatically generate sequential numeric values
- B) Automatically create an index on every column
- C) Automatically encrypt a column
- D) Automatically delete old rows

**Answer: A** — Automatically generate sequential numeric values

**SQL-P177.** Which command changes the data type of an existing column in standard SQL?

- A) CHANGE TABLE TYPE
- B) SET COLUMN TYPE ON TABLE
- C) UPDATE COLUMN TYPE
- D) ALTER TABLE ... ALTER COLUMN ... TYPE (or MODIFY)

**Answer: D** — ALTER TABLE ... ALTER COLUMN ... TYPE (or MODIFY)

**SQL-P178.** What does the GRANT statement do?

- A) Adds a foreign key
- B) Gives privileges to a user or role
- C) Creates a backup
- D) Saves a transaction

**Answer: B** — Gives privileges to a user or role

**SQL-P179.** What does the REVOKE statement do?

- A) Drops a user table
- B) Removes privileges previously granted
- C) Removes a foreign key
- D) Cancels a transaction

**Answer: B** — Removes privileges previously granted

**SQL-P180.** Which SQL clause is used with aggregate functions to divide rows into groups?

- A) ORDER BY
- B) GROUP BY
- C) PARTITION
- D) SPLIT BY

**Answer: B** — GROUP BY

**SQL-P181.** What is a composite primary key?

- A) A key that is automatically generated
- B) A primary key stored in two tables
- C) A primary key made up of two or more columns
- D) A key that allows NULLs

**Answer: C** — A primary key made up of two or more columns

**SQL-P182.** What is a candidate key?

- A) A minimal set of columns that can uniquely identify a row
- B) Any column with an index
- C) A key that allows duplicates
- D) A key copied from another table

**Answer: A** — A minimal set of columns that can uniquely identify a row

**SQL-P183.** Which statement about a view is TRUE?

- A) It cannot be queried with SELECT
- B) It always stores a physical copy of the data
- C) It can only be created on a single column
- D) It does not store data itself (unless materialized)

**Answer: D** — It does not store data itself (unless materialized)

**SQL-P184.** What is the function of the SQL DEFAULT constraint?

- A) Assigns a default value to a column when no value is provided
- B) Prevents deletion of a row
- C) Makes a column unique
- D) Creates a default index

**Answer: A** — Assigns a default value to a column when no value is provided

**SQL-P185.** What does ON DELETE CASCADE do in a foreign key definition?

- A) Deletes child rows automatically when the parent row is deleted
- B) Blocks deletion of parent rows
- C) Copies the parent row to a log
- D) Sets child values to NULL

**Answer: A** — Deletes child rows automatically when the parent row is deleted

**SQL-P186.** What does the ISOLATION level "READ COMMITTED" prevent?

- A) Non-repeatable reads and phantoms
- B) Lost commits
- C) Deadlocks
- D) Dirty reads

**Answer: D** — Dirty reads

**SQL-P187.** Which SQL statement is used to retrieve data?

- A) EXTRACT
- B) SELECT
- C) FETCH ROWS
- D) GET

**Answer: B** — SELECT

**SQL-P188.** Which of these is NOT an aggregate function?

- A) MAX
- B) CONCAT
- C) COUNT
- D) SUM

**Answer: B** — CONCAT

**SQL-P189.** Which SQL wildcard pattern matches any name that ends with 'a'?

- A) '_a'
- B) '%a%'
- C) 'a%'
- D) '%a'

**Answer: D** — '%a'

**SQL-P190.** Which query returns employees whose name starts with 'S' and has exactly 4 characters?

- A) WHERE name LIKE 'S%4'
- B) WHERE name LIKE 'S___'
- C) WHERE name LIKE 'S____'
- D) WHERE name = 'S4'

**Answer: B** — WHERE name LIKE 'S___'

**SQL-P191.** Which is the correct syntax to select all columns from a table named emp?

- A) SELECT emp.*.ALL;
- B) GET * FROM emp;
- C) SELECT * FROM emp;
- D) SELECT ALL emp;

**Answer: C** — SELECT * FROM emp;

**SQL-P192.** Which clause is evaluated last in the logical processing of a SELECT statement?

- A) GROUP BY
- B) WHERE
- C) ORDER BY (then LIMIT)
- D) FROM

**Answer: C** — ORDER BY (then LIMIT)

**SQL-P193.** Can a WHERE clause contain an aggregate function like SUM() directly?

- A) No, use HAVING instead
- B) Only in subqueries of UPDATE
- C) Only with GROUP BY
- D) Yes, always

**Answer: A** — No, use HAVING instead

**SQL-P194.** Which type of JOIN is written in the old comma style "FROM a, b" without a WHERE condition?

- A) Inner join
- B) Left join
- C) Cross join
- D) Natural join

**Answer: C** — Cross join

**SQL-P195.** Which SQL statement combines INSERT, UPDATE and DELETE into a single statement based on a source table?

- A) MERGE
- B) UPSERT_ALL
- C) COMBINE
- D) JOINUPDATE

**Answer: A** — MERGE

**SQL-P196.** Which type of key is a column in one table that refers to the primary key of another table?

- A) Alternate key
- B) Super key
- C) Candidate key
- D) Foreign key

**Answer: D** — Foreign key

**SQL-P197.** Which SQL statement gives the number of employees in each department?

- A) SELECT dept_id, COUNT(*) FROM emp GROUP BY dept_id;
- B) SELECT GROUP dept_id, COUNT(*) FROM emp;
- C) SELECT COUNT(dept_id) FROM emp WHERE dept_id;
- D) SELECT dept_id, COUNT(*) FROM emp ORDER BY dept_id;

**Answer: A** — SELECT dept_id, COUNT(*) FROM emp GROUP BY dept_id;

**SQL-P198.** Which query returns the second-highest salary in the standard, portable way?

- A) SELECT salary FROM emp ORDER BY salary LIMIT 2;
- B) SELECT MAX(salary) FROM emp WHERE salary > (SELECT MAX(salary) FROM emp);
- C) SELECT MAX(salary) FROM emp WHERE salary < (SELECT MAX(salary) FROM emp);
- D) SELECT SECOND(salary) FROM emp;

**Answer: C** — SELECT MAX(salary) FROM emp WHERE salary < (SELECT MAX(salary) FROM emp);

**SQL-P199.** Which query finds duplicate values of column email in table users?

- A) SELECT email FROM users WHERE COUNT(*) > 1;
- B) SELECT email FROM users GROUP BY email HAVING COUNT(*) > 1;
- C) SELECT DISTINCT email FROM users HAVING email > 1;
- D) SELECT email FROM users ORDER BY COUNT(email);

**Answer: B** — SELECT email FROM users GROUP BY email HAVING COUNT(*) > 1;

**SQL-P200.** Which SQL clause is used to combine rows from two tables based on a related column?

- A) MERGE ... BY
- B) LINK ... WITH
- C) JOIN ... ON
- D) COMBINE ... USING

**Answer: C** — JOIN ... ON

**SQL-P201.** Which of the following is TRUE about SQL injection prevention?

- A) Use SELECT * everywhere
- B) Use parameterised queries / prepared statements
- C) Disable all indexes
- D) Concatenate user input into the query string

**Answer: B** — Use parameterised queries / prepared statements

**SQL-P202.** Which keyword is used to define the range of a window in a window function (e.g. SUM() OVER (...))?

- A) OVER
- B) WINDOW BY
- C) RANGE ONLY
- D) SPAN

**Answer: A** — OVER

**SQL-P203.** Which window function assigns unique consecutive numbers to rows, even for ties?

- A) NTILE()
- B) ROW_NUMBER()
- C) RANK()
- D) DENSE_RANK()

**Answer: B** — ROW_NUMBER()

**SQL-P204.** What is a Common Table Expression (CTE)?

- A) A type of index
- B) A temporary named result set defined with WITH
- C) A user-defined data type
- D) A permanent table copy

**Answer: B** — A temporary named result set defined with WITH

**SQL-P205.** Which SQL data type is best for storing exact monetary values?

- A) BLOB
- B) FLOAT
- C) DECIMAL/NUMERIC
- D) VARCHAR

**Answer: C** — DECIMAL/NUMERIC

**SQL-P206.** What is the difference between CHAR(10) and VARCHAR(10)?

- A) CHAR stores numbers only
- B) CHAR is variable-length, VARCHAR is fixed-length
- C) CHAR is fixed-length, VARCHAR is variable-length
- D) There is no difference

**Answer: C** — CHAR is fixed-length, VARCHAR is variable-length

**SQL-P207.** What is the output of the following SQL query executed on the reference table?

```sql
SELECT COUNT(*) FROM employees WHERE dept_id IS NULL;
```

- A) 0
- B) 1
- C) 2
- D) 10

**Answer: B** — 1
> ℹ️ Employee Tarun (emp_id 106) has `dept_id` as NULL. The count is 1.

**SQL-P208.** What is the output of the following SQL query executed on the reference table?

```sql
SELECT COUNT(*) FROM employees WHERE salary > 50000;
```

- A) 2
- B) 3
- C) 4
- D) 5

**Answer: B** — 3
> ℹ️ Employees with salary > 50000 are Asha (60000), Sita (55000), and Arjun (70000). Total count = 3.

**SQL-P209.** What is the output of the following SQL query executed on the reference table?

```sql
SELECT AVG(salary) FROM employees WHERE dept_id = 1;
```

- A) 35000
- B) 37500
- C) 40000
- D) 45000

**Answer: B** — 37500
> ℹ️ Dept 1 employees are Meena (45000) and Vikram (30000). Average = (45000 + 30000) / 2 = 37500.

**SQL-P210.** What is the output of the following SQL query executed on the reference table?

```sql
SELECT dept_id, COUNT(*) FROM employees GROUP BY dept_id HAVING COUNT(*) >= 4;
```

- A) Dept 1 with count 2
- B) Dept 2 with count 4
- C) Dept 3 with count 3
- D) No rows returned

**Answer: B** — Dept 2 with count 4
> ℹ️ Dept 2 has 4 employees (Asha, Ravi, Neha, Arjun), satisfying `HAVING COUNT(*) >= 4`.

**SQL-P211.** What is the output of the following SQL query executed on the reference table?

```sql
SELECT name FROM employees WHERE manager_id IS NULL;
```

- A) Ravi
- B) Asha
- C) Tarun
- D) No rows returned

**Answer: B** — Asha
> ℹ️ Asha (emp_id 101) is the top manager whose `manager_id` is NULL.

## 9. Java / OOP

### 9.2 Practice questions (220)

**JAV-P001.** What is the result of compiling and running the following Java program?

```
public class Main {
public static void main(String[] args) {
int x = 7, y = 2;
System.out.println(x / y + " " + x % y + " " + (double) x / y);
}
}
```

- A) 3.5 1 3.5
- B) 3 1 3
- C) 4 1 3.5
- D) 3 1 3.5

**Answer: D** — 3 1 3.5
> ℹ️ Integer division truncates; the cast applies to x before dividing.

**JAV-P002.** What is the result of compiling and running the following Java program?

```
public class Main {
public static void main(String[] args) {
System.out.println(1 + 2 + "3" + 4 + 5);
}
}
```

- A) 3
- B) 338
- C) 3345
- D) 12345

**Answer: C** — 3345
> ℹ️ Evaluated left to right: 1 + 2 = 3, then string concatenation begins.

**JAV-P003.** What is the result of compiling and running the following Java program?

```
public class Main {
public static void main(String[] args) {
char c = 'A';
c += 2;
System.out.println(c);
System.out.println(c + 1);
}
}
```

- A) C 68
- B) 67 68
- C) C 67
- D) C C

**Answer: A** — C 68
> ℹ️ c + 1 is an int expression.

**JAV-P004.** What is the result of compiling and running the following Java program?

```
public class Main {
public static void main(String[] args) {
String a = "hi", b = "hi", c = new String("hi");
System.out.println((a == b) + " " + (a == c) + " " + a.equals(c));
}
}
```

- A) true false true
- B) false false true
- C) true true true
- D) true false false

**Answer: A** — true false true
> ℹ️ Literals are interned; new String creates a distinct object.

**JAV-P005.** What is the result of compiling and running the following Java program?

```
public class Main {
public static void main(String[] args) {
int i = 5;
int j = i++ + ++i;
System.out.println(i + " " + j);
}
}
```

- A) 6 11
- B) 7 11
- C) 7 12
- D) 6 10

**Answer: C** — 7 12

**JAV-P006.** What is the result of compiling and running the following Java program?

```
public class Main {
public static void main(String[] args) {
int a = 5;
if (a > 3 || ++a > 5) { }
System.out.println(a);
}
}
```

- A) 3
- B) 4
- C) 5
- D) 6

**Answer: C** — 5
> ℹ️ || short-circuits: ++a is never evaluated.

**JAV-P007.** What is the result of compiling and running the following Java program?

```
public class Main {
public static void main(String[] args) {
int a = 5;
if (a > 3 | ++a > 5) { }
System.out.println(a);
}
}
```

- A) 4
- B) 5
- C) 6
- D) 7

**Answer: C** — 6
> ℹ️ Non-short-circuit | evaluates both operands.

**JAV-P008.** What is the result of compiling and running the following Java program?

```
public class Main {
public static void main(String[] args) {
int x = 10;
System.out.println(x > 5 ? x < 8 ? "A" : "B" : "C");
}
}
```

- A) A
- B) AB
- C) C
- D) B

**Answer: D** — B

**JAV-P009.** What is the result of compiling and running the following Java program?

```
public class Main {
public static void main(String[] args) {
int n = 2;
switch (n) {
  case 1: System.out.print("A");
  case 2: System.out.print("B");
  case 3: System.out.print("C"); break;
  default: System.out.print("D");
}
}
}
```

- A) ABC
- B) B
- C) BCD
- D) BC

**Answer: D** — BC
> ℹ️ Without break, execution falls through to the next case.

**JAV-P010.** What is the result of compiling and running the following Java program?

```
public class Main {
public static void main(String[] args) {
int s = 0;
for (int i = 0; i < 5; i++) {
  if (i == 3) continue;
  s += i;
}
System.out.println(s);
}
}
```

- A) 3
- B) 6
- C) 7
- D) 10

**Answer: C** — 7

**JAV-P011.** What is the result of compiling and running the following Java program?

```
public class Main {
public static void main(String[] args) {
int i = 0;
while (true) {
  if (i++ >= 3) break;
}
System.out.println(i);
}
}
```

- A) 2
- B) 3
- C) 4
- D) 5

**Answer: C** — 4

**JAV-P012.** What is the result of compiling and running the following Java program?

```
public class Main {
public static void main(String[] args) {
int[] a = new int[3];
a[1] = 5;
System.out.println(a[0] + a[1] + a[2] + " " + a.length);
}
}
```

- A) 5 3.0
- B) 0 3
- C) 5 2
- D) 5 3

**Answer: D** — 5 3
> ℹ️ int arrays default to 0.

**JAV-P013.** What is the result of compiling and running the following Java program?

```
public class Main {
public static void main(String[] args) {
int[] a = new int[3];
System.out.println(a[3]);
}
}
```

- A) ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException
- B) NullPointerException
- C) 0
- D) Compilation error

**Answer: A** — ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException

**JAV-P014.** What is the result of compiling and running the following Java program?

```
public class Main {
public static void main(String[] args) {
String s = null;
System.out.println(s.length());
}
}
```

- A) Compilation error
- B) NullPointerException
- C) 0
- D) null

**Answer: B** — NullPointerException

**JAV-P015.** What is the result of compiling and running the following Java program?

```
public class Main {
public static void main(String[] args) {
System.out.println(10 / 0);
}
}
```

- A) ArithmeticException
- B) 0
- C) Infinity
- D) Compilation error

**Answer: A** — ArithmeticException
> ℹ️ Integer division by zero throws ArithmeticException.

**JAV-P016.** What is the result of compiling and running the following Java program?

```
public class Main {
public static void main(String[] args) {
System.out.println(10.0 / 0 + " " + (0.0 / 0 != 0.0 / 0));
}
}
```

- A) NaN true
- B) Infinity false
- C) Infinity true
- D) ArithmeticException

**Answer: C** — Infinity true
> ℹ️ Floating-point division by zero gives Infinity; NaN is not equal to itself.

**JAV-P017.** What is the result of compiling and running the following Java program?

```
public class Main {
static int count;
int id;
Main() { id = ++count; }
public static void main(String[] args) {
new Main();
new Main();
Main m = new Main();
System.out.println(m.id + " " + count);
}
}
```

- A) 3 1
- B) 1 1
- C) 3 3
- D) 1 3

**Answer: C** — 3 3

**JAV-P018.** What is the result of compiling and running the following Java program?

```
public class Main {
static class A { void show() { System.out.println("A"); } }
static class B extends A { void show() { System.out.println("B"); } }
public static void main(String[] args) {
A obj = new B();
obj.show();
}
}
```

- A) A
- B) B
- C) AB
- D) Compilation error

**Answer: B** — B
> ℹ️ Runtime polymorphism: the overriding method of the actual object runs.

**JAV-P019.** What is the result of compiling and running the following Java program?

```
public class Main {
static void f(int x) { System.out.println("int"); }
static void f(long x) { System.out.println("long"); }
static void f(Integer x) { System.out.println("Integer"); }
public static void main(String[] args) {
byte b = 1;
f(b);
f(5L);
}
}
```

- A) int long
- B) long long
- C) int int
- D) Integer long

**Answer: A** — int long
> ℹ️ A byte widens to int; 5L matches long.

**JAV-P020.** What is the result of compiling and running the following Java program?

```
public class Main {
static class P { String n = "P"; String get() { return "P"; } }
static class C extends P { String n = "C"; String get() { return "C"; } }
public static void main(String[] args) {
P p = new C();
System.out.println(p.n + p.get());
}
}
```

- A) CC
- B) PC
- C) CP
- D) PP

**Answer: B** — PC
> ℹ️ Fields are resolved by reference type; methods by object type.

**JAV-P021.** What is the result of compiling and running the following Java program?

```
public class Main {
static class A { A() { System.out.print("A"); } }
static class B extends A { B() { System.out.print("B"); } }
public static void main(String[] args) {
new B();
}
}
```

- A) BA
- B) A
- C) AB
- D) B

**Answer: C** — AB
> ℹ️ The superclass constructor runs first.

**JAV-P022.** What is the result of compiling and running the following Java program?

```
public class Main {
static class T {
  T() { this(5); System.out.print("0"); }
  T(int x) { System.out.print(x); }
}
public static void main(String[] args) {
new T();
}
}
```

- A) 50
- B) 05
- C) 50 5
- D) 5

**Answer: A** — 50

**JAV-P023.** What is the result of compiling and running the following Java program?

```
public class Main {
static { System.out.print("S"); }
{ System.out.print("I"); }
Main() { System.out.print("C"); }
public static void main(String[] args) {
new Main();
new Main();
}
}
```

- A) SCIIC
- B) SICSIC
- C) SICIC
- D) ICIC

**Answer: C** — SICIC
> ℹ️ Static block once; instance block then constructor for each object.

**JAV-P024.** What is the result of compiling and running the following Java program?

```
public class Main {
static int f() {
  try { return 1; }
  finally { System.out.print("F"); }
}
public static void main(String[] args) {
System.out.println(f());
}
}
```

- A) F
- B) 1
- C) F1
- D) 1F

**Answer: C** — F1
> ℹ️ finally runs before the method actually returns.

**JAV-P025.** What is the result of compiling and running the following Java program?

```
public class Main {
public static void main(String[] args) {
try { int a = 5 / 0; }
catch (ArithmeticException e) { System.out.print("C"); }
finally { System.out.print("F"); }
System.out.println("E");
}
}
```

- A) CE
- B) CFE
- C) FCE
- D) CF

**Answer: B** — CFE

**JAV-P026.** What is the result of compiling and running the following Java program?

```
public class Main {
public static void main(String[] args) {
try { int a = 1 / 0; }
catch (Exception e) { }
catch (ArithmeticException e) { }
}
}
```

- A) ArithmeticException
- B) No output
- C) Runtime error
- D) Compilation error

**Answer: D** — Compilation error
> ℹ️ A catch for a subclass after its superclass is unreachable, so it does not compile.

**JAV-P027.** What is the result of compiling and running the following Java program?

```
public class Main {
public static void main(String[] args) {
String s = "Accenture";
System.out.println(s.substring(2, 5) + s.indexOf('t') + s.charAt(0));
}
}
```

- A) cen5c
- B) cen6A
- C) ccen5A
- D) cen5A

**Answer: D** — cen5A
> ℹ️ substring(begin, end) excludes the end index.

**JAV-P028.** What is the result of compiling and running the following Java program?

```
public class Main {
public static void main(String[] args) {
String s = "abc";
s.toUpperCase();
System.out.println(s);
}
}
```

- A) abc
- B) ABC
- C) abc ABC
- D) Compilation error

**Answer: A** — abc
> ℹ️ Strings are immutable; the result was discarded.

**JAV-P029.** What is the result of compiling and running the following Java program?

```
public class Main {
public static void main(String[] args) {
StringBuilder sb = new StringBuilder("abc");
sb.append("de").reverse();
System.out.println(sb);
}
}
```

- A) abcedc
- B) edcba
- C) abcde
- D) edcab

**Answer: B** — edcba

**JAV-P030.** What is the result of compiling and running the following Java program?

```
public class Main {
public static void main(String[] args) {
StringBuilder sb = new StringBuilder("hello");
sb.insert(2, "XY").deleteCharAt(0);
System.out.println(sb);
}
}
```

- A) heXYllo
- B) XYello
- C) eXYlo
- D) eXYllo

**Answer: D** — eXYllo

**JAV-P031.** What is the result of compiling and running the following Java program?

```
import java.util.*;
public class Main {
public static void main(String[] args) {
ArrayList<Integer> l = new ArrayList<>(List.of(1, 2, 3));
l.remove(Integer.valueOf(1));
l.remove(0);
System.out.println(l);
}
}
```

- A) [2]
- B) [3]
- C) [2, 3]
- D) [1]

**Answer: B** — [3]
> ℹ️ remove(Object) vs remove(int index).

**JAV-P032.** What is the result of compiling and running the following Java program?

```
import java.util.*;
public class Main {
public static void main(String[] args) {
List<Integer> l = new ArrayList<>(List.of(10, 20, 30));
l.remove(1);
System.out.println(l);
}
}
```

- A) [10, 20]
- B) [10, 30]
- C) [20, 30]
- D) [10, 20, 30]

**Answer: B** — [10, 30]
> ℹ️ remove(1) is remove(int index), not remove the value 1.

**JAV-P033.** What is the result of compiling and running the following Java program?

```
import java.util.*;
public class Main {
public static void main(String[] args) {
Set<Integer> s = new HashSet<>();
s.add(1); s.add(2); s.add(1);
System.out.println(s.size());
}
}
```

- A) 0

**JAV-P036.** What is the result of compiling and running the following Java program?

```
}
}
```

- D) 2 1

**JAV-P077.** What is the role of the JIT compiler?

**Answer: A** — Compiles bytecode into native machine code at runtime

**JAV-P078.** Why is Java called platform independent?

- B) It only runs on Windows

**Answer: A** — Bytecode runs on any platform with a JVM

**JAV-P082.** Can a programmer force garbage collection in Java?

**JAV-P091.** What are the four pillars of OOP?

- C) Encapsulation, Inheritance, Polymorphism, Abstraction

**Answer: C** — Encapsulation, Inheritance, Polymorphism, Abstraction

**JAV-P092.** What is encapsulation?

- A) Creating objects from classes
- B) Bundling data with methods and restricting direct access
- C) Deriving a class from another
- D) Overloading methods

**Answer: B** — Bundling data with methods and restricting direct access

**JAV-P093.** How is encapsulation achieved in Java?

- A) Public fields
- B) Private fields with public getters/setters
- C) Interfaces only

**Answer: B** — Private fields with public getters/setters

**JAV-P094.** What is inheritance?

- A) Having many methods with the same name
- B) A class acquiring properties and behaviour of another class
- C) Creating an instance
- D) Hiding data

**Answer: B** — A class acquiring properties and behaviour of another class

**JAV-P095.** Which keyword is used to inherit a class in Java?

- A) inherits
- B) implements
- C) extends
- D) super

**Answer: C** — extends

**JAV-P096.** Which keyword is used to implement an interface?

- A) inherits
- B) extends
- C) implements
- D) uses

**Answer: C** — implements

**JAV-P097.** Does Java support multiple inheritance of classes?

- B) Only for abstract classes
- C) Yes, using extends twice

**Answer: D** — No, but a class can implement multiple interfaces

**JAV-P098.** What is polymorphism?

- A) One interface, many implementations or forms
- B) Creating packages
- C) Reusing code by extending
- D) Hiding implementation

**Answer: A** — One interface, many implementations or forms

**JAV-P099.** What is method overloading?

- A) A method calling itself
- B) A subclass redefining a superclass method
- C) Multiple methods with the same name but different parameters in the same class
- D) Methods with different names

**Answer: C** — Multiple methods with the same name but different parameters in the same class

**JAV-P100.** What is method overriding?

- B) A subclass providing its own implementation of a superclass method
- C) Declaring two constructors
- D) Hiding fields

**Answer: B** — A subclass providing its own implementation of a superclass method

**JAV-P101.** Which type of polymorphism is method overloading?

- B) Neither
- C) Compile-time (static)
- D) Both

**Answer: C** — Compile-time (static)

**JAV-P102.** Which type of polymorphism is method overriding?

- A) Runtime (dynamic)
- B) Compile-time (static)
- C) Neither
- D) Both

**Answer: A** — Runtime (dynamic)

**JAV-P103.** What is abstraction?

- A) Hiding implementation details and showing only essential features
- B) Deriving from parent

- D) Storing data in private fields only

**Answer: A** — Hiding implementation details and showing only essential features

**JAV-P104.** Can an abstract class be instantiated?

- B) No
- C) Only inside its package
- D) Yes

**JAV-P105.** Can an abstract class have a constructor?

- B) Only static

- D) Only if final

**Answer: A** — Yes

**JAV-P106.** Can an abstract class have concrete methods?

- B) Yes
- C) No
- D) Only private

**Answer: B** — Yes

**JAV-P107.** What is an interface in Java?

**Answer: C** — A contract of abstract methods (and default/static methods) that classes implement

**JAV-P108.** Can an interface have instance variables?

- D) Only in Java 8

**Answer: A** — No, only public static final constants

**JAV-P109.** What is the difference between an abstract class and an interface?

- B) Abstract classes cannot have methods
- C) A class can extend many abstract classes
- D) Interfaces can have constructors

**Answer: A** — A class can implement many interfaces but extend only one abstract class

**JAV-P110.** What does the final keyword do on a class?

**JAV-P111.** What does the final keyword do on a method?

**JAV-P112.** What does the final keyword do on a variable?

**JAV-P113.** What does the static keyword mean for a field?

**JAV-P114.** Can a static method access instance variables directly?

**JAV-P115.** What does the super keyword refer to?

- A) The immediate parent class
- B) The current object
- C) The child class
- D) A static context

**Answer: A** — The immediate parent class

**JAV-P116.** What does the this keyword refer to?

- A) A static method
- B) The parent class
- C) The current object
- D) The class loader

**Answer: C** — The current object

**JAV-P117.** Which access modifier gives the widest visibility?

- A) private
- B) default (package-private)
- C) public
- D) protected

**Answer: C** — public

**JAV-P118.** Which access modifier allows access only within the same class?

- A) private
- B) default
- C) public
- D) protected

**Answer: A** — private

**JAV-P119.** Which access modifier allows access within the same package and by subclasses?

- A) private
- B) public
- C) default
- D) protected

**Answer: D** — protected

**JAV-P120.** What is the default access level when no modifier is specified?

- A) Package-private
- B) private
- C) public
- D) protected

**Answer: A** — Package-private

**JAV-P121.** What is a constructor?

- A) A method that destroys objects
- B) A static block
- C) A private variable
- D) A special method called when an object is created

**Answer: D** — A special method called when an object is created

**JAV-P122.** Does a constructor have a return type?

- A) Yes, void
- B) No, not even void
- C) Yes, the class type
- D) Yes, int

**Answer: B** — No, not even void

**JAV-P123.** What happens if you define no constructor in a class?

- A) Compilation fails
- B) The class cannot be instantiated
- C) A static constructor is used
- D) The compiler provides a default no-argument constructor

**Answer: D** — The compiler provides a default no-argument constructor

**JAV-P124.** Can constructors be overridden?

- A) No
- B) Only when final
- C) Yes
- D) Only when abstract

**Answer: A** — No

**JAV-P125.** Can constructors be overloaded?

- A) Yes
- B) Only in abstract classes
- C) No
- D) Only if private

**Answer: A** — Yes

**JAV-P126.** What is the parent class of all Java classes?

- A) Object
- B) Main
- C) Class
- D) Super

**Answer: A** — Object

**JAV-P127.** Which method is used to compare the contents of two strings?

- A) compare()
- B) same()
- C) equals()
- D) ==

**Answer: C** — equals()

**JAV-P128.** What is the difference between == and equals() for objects?

- A) equals() compares memory addresses only
- B) == compares content; equals() compares references
- C) == compares references; equals() compares content if overridden
- D) They are identical

**Answer: C** — == compares references; equals() compares content if overridden

**JAV-P129.** What is the contract between equals() and hashCode()?

- A) hashCode must always return 0
- B) Equal objects must have different hash codes
- C) There is no contract
- D) Equal objects must have equal hash codes

**Answer: D** — Equal objects must have equal hash codes

**JAV-P130.** Why is String immutable in Java?

- A) Because it is final only
- B) To use less memory always
- C) For security, thread safety and string pooling
- D) Because it is a primitive

**Answer: C** — For security, thread safety and string pooling

**JAV-P131.** What is the String pool?

- A) A connection pool
- B) A list of all objects
- C) A thread pool
- D) A memory area that stores unique string literals

**Answer: D** — A memory area that stores unique string literals

**JAV-P132.** What is the difference between String, StringBuilder and StringBuffer?

- A) StringBuffer is not thread-safe; StringBuilder is
- B) All three are immutable
- C) String is immutable; StringBuilder is mutable and not synchronised; StringBuffer is mutable and synchronised
- D) String is mutable

**Answer: C** — String is immutable; StringBuilder is mutable and not synchronised; StringBuffer is mutable and synchronised

**JAV-P133.** Which is thread-safe?

- A) StringBuffer
- B) ArrayList
- C) HashMap
- D) StringBuilder

**Answer: A** — StringBuffer

**JAV-P134.** What is an exception in Java?

- A) A class modifier
- B) A compile-time keyword
- C) A type of loop
- D) An event that disrupts normal program flow

**Answer: D** — An event that disrupts normal program flow

**JAV-P135.** What is the base class of all exceptions and errors?

- A) Throwable
- B) Exception
- C) Error
- D) RuntimeException

**Answer: A** — Throwable

**JAV-P136.** Which of the following is a checked exception?

- A) ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException
- B) ArithmeticException
- C) IOException
- D) NullPointerException

**Answer: C** — IOException

**JAV-P137.** Which of the following is an unchecked exception?

- A) SQLException
- B) IOException
- C) NullPointerException
- D) FileNotFoundException

**Answer: C** — NullPointerException

**JAV-P138.** Which keyword is used to manually throw an exception?

- A) raise
- B) throw
- C) throws
- D) try

**Answer: B** — throw

**JAV-P139.** Which keyword declares that a method may throw an exception?

- A) throw
- B) finally
- C) catch
- D) throws

**Answer: D** — throws

**JAV-P140.** When does a finally block execute?

- A) Only when an exception occurs
- B) Never with return
- C) Only when no exception occurs
- D) Almost always, whether or not an exception occurs

**Answer: D** — Almost always, whether or not an exception occurs

**JAV-P141.** What is try-with-resources used for?

- A) Catching all exceptions
- B) Creating threads
- C) Overloading methods
- D) Automatically closing resources that implement AutoCloseable

**Answer: D** — Automatically closing resources that implement AutoCloseable

**JAV-P142.** What is the difference between throw and throws?

- A) throw is only for errors
- B) They are identical
- C) throw raises an exception; throws declares it in a method signature
- D) throws raises an exception; throw declares it

**Answer: C** — throw raises an exception; throws declares it in a method signature

**JAV-P143.** Which collection does not allow duplicate elements?

- A) Queue
- B) ArrayList
- C) List
- D) Set

**Answer: D** — Set

**JAV-P144.** Which collection maintains insertion order and allows duplicates?

- A) TreeSet
- B) HashSet
- C) ArrayList
- D) HashMap

**Answer: C** — ArrayList

**JAV-P145.** Which collection stores key-value pairs?

- A) List
- B) Map
- C) Queue
- D) Set

**Answer: B** — Map

**JAV-P146.** Which Map keeps keys in sorted order?

- A) HashMap
- B) LinkedHashMap
- C) TreeMap
- D) Hashtable

**Answer: C** — TreeMap

**JAV-P147.** Which Map preserves insertion order?

- A) LinkedHashMap
- B) ConcurrentHashMap
- C) HashMap
- D) TreeMap

**Answer: A** — LinkedHashMap

**JAV-P148.** Does HashMap allow a null key?

- A) Yes, many null keys
- B) Yes, one null key
- C) Only in Java 7
- D) No

**JAV-P149.** Does Hashtable allow null keys?

- A) Yes, one
- B) Yes
- C) No
- D) Yes, many

**Answer: C** — No

**JAV-P150.** What is the time complexity of get() in a HashMap (average)?

- A) O(1)
- B) O(n)
- C) O(n log n)
- D) O(log n)

**Answer: A** — O(1)

**JAV-P151.** What is the difference between ArrayList and LinkedList?

- A) LinkedList has fast random access
- B) ArrayList is doubly linked
- C) They are identical
- D) ArrayList has fast random access; LinkedList has fast insertion/removal at ends

**Answer: D** — ArrayList has fast random access; LinkedList has fast insertion/removal at ends

**JAV-P152.** What is the difference between Comparable and Comparator?

- A) Comparator is inside the class
- B) Comparable needs two arguments
- C) They are identical
- D) Comparable defines natural ordering inside the class; Comparator defines external ordering

**Answer: D** — Comparable defines natural ordering inside the class; Comparator defines external ordering

**JAV-P153.** Which method does the Comparable interface declare?

- A) equals
- B) compareTo
- C) sort
- D) compare

**Answer: B** — compareTo

**JAV-P154.** What is a generic in Java?

- A) A way to write type-safe reusable code with type parameters
- B) A special class
- C) A package
- D) A raw type

**Answer: A** — A way to write type-safe reusable code with type parameters

**JAV-P155.** What is autoboxing?

- A) Creating arrays
- B) Boxing objects manually
- C) Automatic conversion between primitives and wrapper objects
- D) Converting to string

**Answer: C** — Automatic conversion between primitives and wrapper objects

**JAV-P156.** What is a thread?

- A) A class loader
- B) A lightweight unit of execution within a process
- C) A type of loop
- D) A separate process

**Answer: B** — A lightweight unit of execution within a process

**JAV-P157.** Which method starts a new thread's execution?

- A) start()
- B) run()
- C) execute()
- D) begin()

**Answer: A** — start()

**JAV-P158.** What is the difference between start() and run()?

- A) They are identical
- B) run() creates a new thread
- C) start() only works once per JVM
- D) start() creates a new thread; run() executes in the current thread

**Answer: D** — start() creates a new thread; run() executes in the current thread

**JAV-P159.** What does the synchronized keyword do?

- B) Makes a variable constant

**Answer: A** — Allows only one thread at a time to execute the block/method on the same lock

**JAV-P160.** What does the volatile keyword guarantee?

**JAV-P161.** What is a deadlock in Java?

- C) A crashed JVM

**Answer: B** — Two or more threads waiting on each other's locks forever

**JAV-P162.** Which interface represents a task that returns a result and can throw a checked exception?

- C) Callable

**JAV-P163.** What is a lambda expression?

**JAV-P164.** What is a functional interface?

**JAV-P165.** Which package is imported by default in every Java class?

**JAV-P167.** What does the instanceof operator do?

**JAV-P168.** What is the difference between an Error and an Exception?

**JAV-P169.** What is the singleton design pattern?

**JAV-P170.** What is serialization in Java?

- B) Compiling classes

**JAV-P171.** Which keyword prevents a field from being serialised?

**JAV-P172.** What is an enum in Java?

- B) A special class representing a fixed set of constants

**JAV-P173.** What is an inner (non-static nested) class?

- B) A final class
- C) A class in another package

**JAV-P174.** What is an anonymous class?

- B) A class with no fields

**JAV-P175.** What is a wrapper class?

**JAV-P176.** What does the @Override annotation do?

**JAV-P177.** What is a Java record (Java 16+)?

**JAV-P178.** What does the var keyword (Java 10+) do?

**JAV-P184.** What does the toString() method of Object return by default?

**JAV-P185.** What is the difference between an abstract class and a concrete class?

**JAV-P186.** What makes a class immutable?

**JAV-P187.** What is a marker interface?

- C) An interface with no methods, such as Serializable

**JAV-P188.** What does the clone() method do?

**JAV-P189.** What is the difference between shallow and deep copy?

- B) A deep copy also copies referenced objects; a shallow copy copies references
- C) They are identical

**JAV-P190.** What is the difference between an interface's default method and an abstract method?

**JAV-P191.** Can an interface have static methods (Java 8+)?

**JAV-P192.** What is the purpose of the transient keyword?

- B) Makes a field constant

**Answer: D** — Excludes a field from serialisation

**JAV-P193.** What is method hiding?

**JAV-P194.** What is the ClassLoader responsible for?

**JAV-P195.** What are the JVM memory regions where class metadata is stored (Java 8+)?

**JAV-P197.** Which exception occurs when the heap is exhausted?

**JAV-P198.** What is the difference between ArrayList and Vector?

```java
public class Test {
    public static void main(String[] args) {
        int x = 5;
        int y = x++ + ++x;
        System.out.println(y);
    }
}
```

- A) 10
- B) 11
- C) 12
- D) 13

**Answer: C** — 12
> ℹ️ `x++` evaluates to 5 and sets x to 6. `++x` increments x to 7 and evaluates to 7. `5 + 7 = 12`.

**JAV-P217.** What is the output of the following Java code?

```java
public class Test {
    public static void main(String[] args) {
        String s1 = "Accenture";
        String s2 = new String("Accenture");
        System.out.println((s1 == s2) + " " + s1.equals(s2));
    }
}
```

- A) true true
- B) false false
- C) true false
- D) false true

**Answer: D** — false true
> ℹ️ `s1 == s2` evaluates to false because `s2` points to a distinct object in heap memory. `s1.equals(s2)` compares string content and returns true.

**JAV-P218.** What will happen when compiling and executing the following Java code?

```java
class Parent {
    final void display() {
        System.out.println("Parent");
    }
}
class Child extends Parent {
    void display() {
        System.out.println("Child");
    }
}
```

- A) Prints "Child"
- B) Prints "Parent"
- C) Compilation error: cannot override final method
- D) Runtime exception: NoSuchMethodError

**Answer: C** — Compilation error: cannot override final method
> ℹ️ In Java, methods declared with the `final` keyword cannot be overridden by subclasses.

**JAV-P219.** Which interface in Java Collection Framework extends `Collection` and does NOT allow duplicate elements?

- A) `List`
- B) `Queue`
- C) `Set`
- D) `Map`

**Answer: C** — Set
> ℹ️ `Set` extends `Collection` and models mathematical set abstractions containing no duplicate elements.

**JAV-P220.** What is the default initial capacity and load factor for a standard Java `HashMap`?

- A) Capacity 10, Load factor 0.5
- B) Capacity 16, Load factor 0.75
- C) Capacity 32, Load factor 0.8
- D) Capacity 8, Load factor 0.6

**Answer: B** — Capacity 16, Load factor 0.75
> ℹ️ Standard Java `HashMap` initializes with a bucket capacity of 16 and rehashes when 75% (0.75 load factor) occupied.

- D) Merge sort

**Answer: D** — Merge sort

**CFU-P239.** Which data structure is used to implement a priority queue efficiently?

- A) Heap
- B) Stack
- C) Array of pointers
- D) Circular list only

**Answer: A** — Heap

**CFU-P240.** What is the average time complexity of a hash table lookup?

- A) O(log n)
- B) O(n log n)
- C) O(n)
- D) O(1)

**Answer: D** — O(1)

**CFU-P241.** In which traversal of a binary search tree are elements visited in sorted order?

- A) Level order
- B) Inorder
- C) Postorder
- D) Preorder

**Answer: B** — Inorder

**CFU-P242.** What is the 8-bit Two's Complement representation of the decimal integer `-18`?

- A) 11101110
- B) 11101101
- C) 00010010
- D) 11110010

**Answer: A** — 11101110
> ℹ️ Binary of +18 is 00010010. One's complement is 11101101. Adding 1 yields Two's complement: 11101110.

**CFU-P243.** In a Demand Paging operating system using the LRU (Least Recently Used) page replacement algorithm with 3 physical frames, consider page reference string: `1, 2, 3, 4, 1, 2`. How many total page faults occur starting with empty frames?

- A) 4
- B) 5
- C) 6
- D) 3

**Answer: C** — 6
> ℹ️ Frames sequence: [] -> [1] (fault 1) -> [1,2] (fault 2) -> [1,2,3] (fault 3) -> [4,2,3] (fault 4, 1 replaced) -> [4,1,3] (fault 5, 2 replaced) -> [4,1,2] (fault 6, 3 replaced).

**CFU-P244.** Which CPU scheduling algorithm can suffer from the "Convoy Effect", where small processes wait behind a long execution process?

- A) Round Robin (RR)
- B) Shortest Job First (SJF)
- C) First-Come, First-Served (FCFS)
- D) Priority Scheduling (Preemptive)

**Answer: C** — First-Come, First-Served (FCFS)
> ℹ️ FCFS is non-preemptive; a CPU-intensive job holds the processor while shorter I/O-bound processes bottleneck behind it.

**CFU-P245.** What is the maximum number of nodes in a binary tree of height `h` (where height of a tree with root only is defined as 0)?

- A) 2^h
- B) 2^(h+1) - 1
- C) 2^h - 1
- D) 2^(h-1)

**Answer: B** — 2^(h+1) - 1
> ℹ️ Sum of nodes per level is 2^0 + 2^1 + ... + 2^h = 2^(h+1) - 1.

**CFU-P246.** In computer architecture, what does a Bus Master do?

- A) Controls CPU clock frequencies.
- B) Temporarily takes control of the system bus to initiate data transfers independently of the CPU (e.g. DMA controller).
- C) Translates virtual memory addresses to physical addresses.
- D) Encrypts data passing over system buses.

**Answer: B** — Bus Master
> ℹ️ A bus master acquires ownership of the system bus to perform high-speed data transfers without CPU intervention.

## Appendix A: corrections to the source answer key

These are places where the answer printed in your uploaded file was wrong, self-contradictory or depends on version. The answer shown above is the one I believe is correct.

- **PSE-S005**: Source key said B (7, 5, 7) but the source's own explanation computes a = 7 − 3 = 4, so the output is 4, 5, 7. Corrected to A.
- **MSO-S008**: Ambiguous in the original. Strictly, Ctrl+Shift+F puts focus in the Font box; Ctrl+D opens the Font dialog. Option A is the closest match reported.
- **MSO-S010**: Classic Outlook calls this Delay Delivery; newer Outlook labels it Schedule Send. Answer depends on version.
- **MSO-S011**: Word's default paper size depends on region/installation: Letter (US) vs A4 (India/Europe). Most reports key this as Letter.
- **MSO-S012**: CONCATENATE, CONCAT and TEXTJOIN all combine text; only JOIN does not exist. Most reports key CONCATENATE.
- **NET-S006**: A VLAN's primary role is logically segmenting one physical switch into multiple broadcast domains; reduced broadcast traffic is a benefit.
- **CYB-S010**: Ambiguous: TLS uses RSA/ECDHE for key exchange and AES for bulk encryption. AES is the most commonly reported key.
- **PSE-S008**: Source key printed 'C) 15' but 15 is option B. mystery(5) = 5 × mystery(3) = 5 × 3 × mystery(1) = 15.
- **PSE-S010**: Source key said 15; the actual value is 5 + 3 + 1 + 0 = 9 (option B).
- **MSO-S015**: Answer as reported by the source.
- **MSO-S017**: Answer as reported by the source.
- **MSO-S024**: Answer as reported by the source (Shift-click selects a range; Ctrl-click adds a word).
- **MSO-S038**: Both Home and Insert tabs have a New Slide button; source key says Home.
- **MSO-S046**: Answer as reported by the source.
- **MSO-S049**: Answer as reported by the source.
- **MSO-S050**: Answer as reported by the source.
- **MSO-S057**: Source key says 'Contextual error', but the source's own earlier question says green = grammar, blue = contextual, red = spelling (the standard Word convention). Answer given follows the standard convention.
- **MSO-S060**: Reported key is FrontPage, but none of the options is technically right — the real feature is AutoRecover/AutoSave. Kept as reported by the source.
